An ordinal analysis of Π N \Pi_{N} -Collection
Toshiyasu Arai

Abstract
In this paper we give an ordinal analysis of a set theory with Π N \Pi_{N} -Collection for each positive integer N N .



1
Introduction

Throughout in this paper
N
denotes a fixed positive integer.
In this paper we give an ordinal analysis of a Kripke-Platek
set theory with the axiom of Infinity and one of
\Pi_{N}
-Collection, denoted
by
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
.
Our proof is an extension of
[
4
,
5
]
.
Since
[
5
]
has not yet appeared, some proofs are duplicated for the readers’ conveniences.

In
[
5
]
we analyzed proof-theoretically
a set theory

{\sf KP}\ell^{r}+(M\prec_{\Sigma_{1}}V)
extending
{\sf KP}\ell^{r}

with an axiom stating that
‘there exists a transitive set
M
such that
M\prec_{\Sigma_{1}}V
’.
An ordinal analysis of an extension

{\sf KP}i+(M\prec_{\Sigma_{1}}V)
is given in M. Rathjen
[
14
]
.
Our proof is an extension of
[
2
,
5
]
.
In
[
2
]
,
a set theory
{\sf KP}\Pi_{N}
of
\Pi_{N}
-reflection is analyzed, while

[
2
]

is an extension of M. Rathjen’s analysis in
[
13
]

for

\Pi_{3}
-reflection.

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC+BI}
[
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-AC+BI}
]
denotes a second order arithmetic obtained from
\mbox{ACA}_{0}+\mbox{BI}

by adding the axiom of
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}
-Dependent Choice
[
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}
-Axiom of Choice], resp.
It is easy to see that
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC+BI}
is interpreted canonically to the set theory

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}+(V=L)
with the axiom
V=L
of constructibility.
It is well known that
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC}_{0}
implies
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-AC}
, which yields
\Delta^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-CA}
,
a fortiori
\Sigma^{1}_{N+1}\mbox{-CA}
, cf. Lemma VII.6.6 of
[
15
]
.
Moreover it is known that
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC+BI}
is

\Pi^{1}_{4}
-conservative over
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-AC+BI}

[over
\Delta^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-CA+BI}
], resp.,
cf. Exercise VII.5.13 and
Theorem VII.6.16 of
[
15
]
.

Let
n
be a positive integer.
We say that an ordinal
\alpha
is
n
-
stable
if
L_{\alpha}\prec_{\Sigma_{n}}L

for the constructible universe
L=\bigcup_{\alpha}L_{\alpha}
.
In general, a transitive and non-empty set
M
is
n
-
stable
if
M\prec_{\Sigma_{n}}V

for the universe
V
.
We see that

(V,\in)\models{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
if

V
enjoys the
\Delta_{0}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
-collection, where

st_{i}
denotes the predicate
for the class
\{M\in V:M\prec_{\Sigma_{i}}V\}
of
i
-stable sets
in
V
.

We introduce an extension
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
of
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
in the language
\{\in\}\cup\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N}
,
which codifies
\Sigma(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
-reflection.
We aim to give an ordinal analysis of the theory
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.

In the following theorems,

\Omega
denotes the least recursively regular ordinal
\omega_{1}^{CK}
, and
\psi_{\Omega}

a collapsing function such that
\psi_{\Omega}(\alpha)<\Omega
.

\mathbb{I}_{N}
is an ordinal term denoting an ordinal such that

L_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\models{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}+(V=L)
.

First we show the following Theorem
1.1
.

Theorem 1.1

Suppose
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\vdash\theta^{L_{\Omega}}

for a
\Sigma_{1}
-sentence
\theta
in the language
\{\in\}
of set theory.
Then

L_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}\models\theta
holds.

It is not hard to see that the ordinal
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
is computable.
Let
<
denote a computable well-ordering of type
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})

on the set of natural numbers.
Conversely we show that
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC+BI}}
proves that
each initial segment of
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
is well-founded.

Theorem 1.2

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC+BI}}\vdash Wo[\alpha]

for
each

\alpha<\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
.

For
T\supset{\rm ACA}_{0}
,
|T|
denotes
the proof-theoretic ordinal of
T
, i.e., the supremum of order types
of computable well-orderings
\prec
on the set of natural numbers for which

T
proves the fact that
\prec
is a well-ordering.
Also let

|{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}|_{\Sigma_{1}^{\Omega}}
denote the

\Sigma_{1}^{\Omega}
-ordinal of
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
, i.e., the ordinal

\min\{\alpha\leq\omega_{1}^{CK}:\forall\theta\in\Sigma_{1}\left({\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}\vdash\theta^{L_{\Omega}}\Rightarrow L_{\alpha}\models\theta\right)\}
.
For more on ordinal analysis see
[
3
]
.
We conclude the following Theorem
1.3
, where

\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
denotes the order type of
the initial segment
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega
of a notation system
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
of
ordinals.

Theorem 1.3

|\Delta^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-CA+BI}}|=|\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-AC+BI}}|=|\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC+BI}}|=|{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}|_{\Sigma_{1}^{\Omega}}=\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
.

Let
\mathbf{Z}_{2}=\Sigma^{1}_{\infty}\mbox{{\rm-DC}}
be the full second order arithmetic with
the Dependent Choice schema, and
{\sf ZFC}\mbox{{\rm-}}{\sf Power}
denote the set theory

ZFC
minus the power set axiom.

\mathbf{Z}_{2}
proves the (
\Pi^{1}_{1}
-)soundness of

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC}}+\mbox{BI}
,
and hence
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
proves that

(OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}),<)
is a well ordering for
each

N
.

\mathbf{Z}_{2}
is canonically interpreted in
({\sf ZFC}\mbox{{\rm-}}{\sf Power})+(V=L)
, which is

\Pi^{1}_{1}
-conservative over
{\sf ZFC}\mbox{{\rm-}}{\sf Power}
.

Assume
{\sf ZFC}\mbox{{\rm-}}{\sf Power}\vdash\theta
for a sentence
\theta
.
Since
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
subsumes
\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
and

\Sigma_{N}\mbox{-Separation}
, there is an
N
such that

S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\vdash\theta
.
Therefore we conclude the following.

Theorem 1.4

\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega}):=\sup\{\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{N}):0<N<\omega\}=|\mathbf{Z}_{2}|=|{\sf ZFC}\mbox{{\rm-}}{\sf Power}|_{\Sigma_{1}^{\Omega}}
.

Let us mention the contents of this paper.
In the next section
2
a second order arithmetic
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC+BI}

is interpreted to a set theory

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}+(V=L)
, and

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
is shown to be
a subtheory of a set theory
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
In section
3
ordinals for our analysis of
\Pi_{N}
-Collection are introduced,
and a computable notation system
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is extracted.

Theorem
1.1
is proved in sections
4
and
5
.
In section
4
operator controlled derivations are introduced.
In section
5
, stable ordinals are removed
from derivations.
Although our proof of Theorem
1.1
is based on operator controlled derivations introduced by W. Buchholz
[
9
]
, it is hard for us to give its sketch here.
See subsection
4.2
for an outline of the proof.

Theorem
1.2
is proved
in sections
6
and
7
.
For
0\leq i\leq N
, we introduce
i
-maximal distinguished sets
, which are

\Sigma^{1}_{2+i}
-definable.
A
0
-maximal distinguished set is
\Sigma^{1}_{2}
-definable as in
[
4
]
.

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}
-(Dependent) Choice is needed to handle limits of
N
-stable ordinals.
Our proof of Theorem
1.2
is based on maximal distinguished class introduced
again by Buchholz
[
7
]
.
A sketch of the well-foundedness proof is outlined in subsection
6.1
.

In the final section
8
let us conclude some standard outcomes of
an ordinal analysis of the theory
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
.

IH denotes the Induction Hypothesis, MIH the Main IH, SIH the Subsidiary IH,
and SSIH the Sub-Subsidiary IH.

2
\Pi_{N}
-Collection

In this section a second order arithmetic
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC+BI}
is interpreted canonically to a set theory

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}+(V=L)
, and

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
is shown to be
a subtheory of a set theory
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.

For subsystems of second order arithmetic, we follow largely Simpson’s monograph
[
15
]
.
The schema Bar Induction, BI is denoted by TI in
[
15
]
.
BI allows the transfinite induction schema for well-founded relations.

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-AC+BI}
denotes a second order arithmetic obtained from

\Pi^{1}_{1}\mbox{-CA}_{0}+\mbox{BI}

by adding the axiom
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-AC}
,

\forall n\exists XF(n,X)\to\exists Y\forall nF(n,Y_{n})

for each
\Pi^{1}_{N+1}
-formula
F(n,X)
, where
m\in Y_{n}\Leftrightarrow(n,m)\in Y

for a bijective pairing function
(\cdot,\cdot)
.

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC+BI}
denotes a second order arithmetic obtained from

\Pi^{1}_{1}\mbox{-CA}_{0}+\mbox{BI}

by adding the axiom
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC}
for each
\Pi^{1}_{N+1}
-formula
F(n,X,Y)
,

\forall n\forall X\exists YF(n,X,Y)\to\forall X_{0}\exists Y\forall n[Y_{0}=X_{0}\land F(n,Y_{n},Y_{n+1})]
.
It is easy to see that the formulas
F
can be
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}
in the axioms.

The axioms of the set theory
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
consists of those of
{\sf KP}\omega

(Kripke-Platek set theory with the Axiom of Infinity, cf.
[
6
,
12
]
) plus
\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
:
for each
\Pi_{N}
-formula
A(x,y)
in the language of set theory,

\forall x\in a\exists yA(x,y)\to\exists b\forall x\in a\exists y\in bA(x,y)
.

\Sigma_{N}\mbox{-Separation}
denotes the axiom
\exists y\forall x(x\in y\leftrightarrow x\in a\land\varphi(x))

for each
\Sigma_{N}
-formula
\varphi(x)
.

\Delta_{N+1}\mbox{-Separation}
denotes the axiom

\forall x\in a(\varphi(x)\leftrightarrow\lnot\psi(x))\to\exists y\forall x(x\in y\leftrightarrow x\in a\land\varphi(x))

for each
\Sigma_{N+1}
-formulas
\varphi(x)
and
\psi(x)
.

\Sigma_{N+1}\mbox{-Replacement}
denotes the axiom stating that if

\forall x\in a\exists!y\varphi(x,y)
, then there exists a function
f
with its domain
\mathrm{dom}(f)=a
such that

\forall x\in a\,\varphi(x,f(x))

for each
\Sigma_{N+1}
-formula
\varphi(x,y)
.

Lemma 2.1

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}
proves each of

\Sigma_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Separation}}
,

\Delta_{N+1}-\\
\mbox{{\rm Separation}}
and

\Sigma_{N+1}\mbox{{\rm-Replacement}}
.

Proof
. We show that
\{x\in a:\varphi(x)\}
exists as a set for each
\Sigma_{i}
-formula

\varphi
by (meta)induction on
i\leq N
.
The case
i=0
follows from
\Delta_{0}
-Separation.
Let
\varphi\equiv\exists y\,\theta(x,y)

with a
\Pi_{i-1}
-matrix
\theta
.
We have by logic
\forall x\in a\exists y(\exists z\theta(x,z)\rightarrow\theta(x,y))
.
By
\Pi_{i}
-Collection pick a set
b
so that

\forall x\in a\exists y\in b(\varphi(x)\rightarrow\theta(x,y))
.
In other words,

\{x\in a:\varphi(x)\}=\{x\in a:\exists y\in b\,\theta(x,y)\}
.
If
i=1
, then
\exists c[c=\{x\in a:\exists y\in b\,\theta(x,y)\}]
by
\Delta_{0}
-Separation.
Let
2\leq i\leq N
.
By
\Pi_{i-2}
-Collection we obtain a
\Pi_{i-1}
-formula
\sigma
such that

\exists y\in b\,\theta(x,y)\leftrightarrow\sigma(x)
.
By IH we obtain
\exists c[c=\{x\in a:\sigma(x)\}]
.

\Delta_{N+1}\mbox{-Separation}
follows from
\Sigma_{N}\mbox{-Separation}
as in
[
6
]
, p.17, Theorem 4.5(
\Delta
Separation),
and

\Sigma_{N+1}\mbox{-Replacement}
follows from
\Delta_{N+1}\mbox{-Separation}
as in
[
6
]
, p.17, Theorem 4.6(
\Sigma
Replacement).

\Box

For a formula
A
in the language of second order arithmetic let
A^{set}
denote the formula obtained from
A
by interpreting the first order variable
x
as
x\in\omega
and the second order variable
X
as
X\subset\omega
.

The following is the Quantifier Theorem in p.125 of
[
12
]
, in which

{\sf KP}l^{r}
is defined as a set theory for limits of admissible sets with restricted induction.

{\sf KP}l^{r}
is a subtheory of
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}
.

Ad(x)
designates that
x
is an admissible set.

Lemma 2.2

For each
\Sigma^{1}_{N+1}
-formula
F(n,a,Y)
,
there exists a
\Sigma_{N}
-formula
A_{\Sigma}(d,n,a,Y)
in the language of set theory so that for

F_{\Sigma}(n,a,Y):\Leftrightarrow\exists d[Ad(d)\land Y\in d\land A_{\Sigma}(d,n,a,Y)]
,

{\sf KP}l^{r}\vdash n,a\in\omega\land Y\subset\omega\to\{F^{set}(n,a,Y)\leftrightarrow F_{\Sigma}(n,a,Y)\}.

For an ordinal
\alpha
,
L_{\alpha}
denotes the initial segment of Gödel’s constructible universe

L=\bigcup_{\alpha}L_{\alpha}
.

x\in L
is a
\Sigma_{1}
-formula.

<_{L}
denotes a canonical
\Delta_{1}
well ordering of
L
such that
if
y<_{L}x\in L_{\alpha}
, then
y\in L_{\alpha}
, cf. p.162 of
[
6
]
.

V=L
denotes the axiom of Constructibility.

Lemma 2.3

For each sentence
A
in the language of second order arithmetic,

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}{\rm-DC+BI}\vdash A\Rightarrow{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}+(V=L)\vdash A^{set}.

Proof
. By the Quantifier Theorem
2.2

F^{set}(n,X,Y)
is equivalent to a
\Pi_{N}
-formula
\varphi(n,X,Y)

for a
\Pi^{1}_{N+1}
-formula
F(n,X,Y)
,
n\in\omega
and
X\subset\omega
.
It suffices to show for a
\Pi_{N}
-formula
\varphi(n,X,Y)
that assuming

\forall n\in\omega\forall X\subset\omega\exists Y\subset\omega\varphi(n,X,Y)
and
X_{0}\subset\omega
,
there exists a function
f
with its domain
\mathrm{dom}(f)=\omega
such that

\forall n\in\omega[f(0)=X_{0}\land\varphi(n,f(n),f(n+1))]
.
By induction on
k\in\omega
using
V=L
we see that there exists a unique family
(Y_{n})_{n<k}
of subsets of
\omega

such that

\forall n<k[\varphi(n,Y_{n},Y_{n+1})\land\forall Z<_{L}Y_{n+1}\lnot\varphi(n,Y_{n},Z)]
, where

\forall Z<_{L}Y\lnot\varphi(n,Y,Z)
is equivalent to a
\Sigma_{N}
-formula under

\Pi_{N-1}
-Collection.
By
\Sigma_{N+1}
-Replacement pick a function
g
with
\mathrm{dom}(g)=\omega
and
rng(g)\subset{}^{<\omega}{\cal P}(\omega)

so that for any
k\in\omega

g(k)
is the unique sequence
(Y_{n})_{n<k}\in{}^{k}{\cal P}(\omega)
with
Y_{0}=X_{0}
.
Then the function
f(n)=\left(g(n+1)\right)(n)
is a desired one.

\Box

It is easy to see that

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}+(V=L)\vdash A\Rightarrow{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}\vdash A^{L}
for any
A
, and
each
\Pi^{1}_{1}
-sentence
B
on
\omega
is absolute for
L
,

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}\vdash B\leftrightarrow B^{L}
.

Next we show that
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{-Collection}
is contained in a set theory

S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
The language of the theory
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
is
\{\in,\mathsf{M}_{0}\}\cup\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N}
with unary predicate constants
st_{i}

and an individual constant
\mathsf{M}_{0}
.

st_{i}(a)
is intended to denote the fact that
a
is an
i
-stable set
and
\mathsf{M}_{0}
is intended to denote the least admissible set
L_{\omega_{1}^{CK}}
above
L_{\omega}
.
The axioms of
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
are obtained from those
1
1

1

In the axiom schemata
\Delta_{0}
-Separation and
\Delta_{0}
-Collection,

\Delta_{0}
-formulas remain to mean a
\Delta_{0}
-formula in which
st_{i}
does not occur,
while the axiom of foundation may be applied to a formula in which
st_{i}
may occur.
of
{\sf KP}\omega
by adding the following axioms.
By a
\Delta_{0}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i<k})
-formula we mean a bounded formula in the language

\mathcal{L}_{k}=\{\in,\mathsf{M}_{0}\}\cup\{st_{i}\}_{i<k}
.

1.

The axioms for the admissible set
\mathsf{M}_{0}
:

\mathsf{M}_{0}\neq\emptyset
,
\forall x\in\mathsf{M}_{0}\forall y\in x(y\in\mathsf{M}_{0})
, and the axioms stating that

(\mathsf{M}_{0},\in)\models{\sf KP}\omega
.

2.

\Delta_{0}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})\mbox{-collection}
:

\forall x\in a\exists y\,\theta(x,y)\to\exists b\forall x\in a\exists y\in b\,\theta(x,y)

for each
\Delta_{0}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
-formula
\theta
in which the predicates
st_{i}
may occur.
Note that
\Sigma_{1}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})\mbox{-collection}
follows from this.

3.

\forall a\exists b[a\in b\land st_{N}(b)]

(1)

4.

For each
i+1\leq N
:

st_{i+1}(a)\to M_{0}\in a\land\forall y\in a\forall z\in y(z\in a)\land lst_{i}(a)

(2)

where
lst_{i}(a):\Leftrightarrow\forall b\in a\exists c\in a\left(b\in c\land st_{i}(c)\right)
and

st_{0}(c):\Leftrightarrow(0=0)
.

5.

For
0<i\leq N
:

st_{i}(a)\land\varphi(u)\land u\in a\to\varphi^{a}(u)

(3)

for each
\Sigma_{1}(\{st_{j}\}_{j<i})
-formula
\varphi\equiv(\exists x\,\theta)
in the language

\mathcal{L}_{i}=\{\in,M_{0}\}\cup\{st_{j}\}_{j<i}
, where

\varphi^{a}\equiv(\exists x\in a\,\theta)
.

Note that if
lst_{i+1}(a)
for a transitive set
a
, then
lst_{i}(a)
holds.

Lemma 2.4

S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\vdash st_{i}(M)\land u\in M\to[\varphi^{M}(u)\leftrightarrow\varphi(u)]

for set-theoretic
\Sigma_{i}
-formulas
\varphi
.

Proof
. Argue in
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
The case
i=1
follows from the axiom (
3
).
We show

st_{k}(a)\land u\in a\to\left[\theta^{a}(u)\leftrightarrow\exists b\in a\{st_{i}(b)\land u\in b\land\theta^{b}(u)\}\right]

(4)

for
0\leq i<k\leq N+1
and
\Pi_{1}(\{st_{j}\}_{j<i-1})
-formula
\theta(u)
, where

a=V
,

st_{N+1}(V):\Leftrightarrow(0=0)
and
\theta^{V}(u):\Leftrightarrow\theta
when
k=N+1
.

Assume
st_{k}(a)
and
\theta^{a}(u)
with
u\in a
.
By the axioms (
1
) and (
2
)
there exists a set
b\in a
such that
st_{i}(b)
and
u\in b
.

\theta^{b}(u)
follows logically.
Conversely assume
\theta^{b}(u)
for

b\in a
such that
st_{i}(b)
and
u\in b
.
(
3
) yields
\theta(u)
, a fortiori
\theta^{a}(u)
.
Thus (
4
) is shown.

Let
\varphi(u)\in\Sigma_{1+n}(\{st_{j}\}_{j<i})
and
st_{i+n}(a)
with
u\in a
.
From (
4
) we see by (meta-)induction on
n
that
there exists a
\Sigma_{1}(\{st_{j}\}_{j<i+n})
-formula
\theta
such that

\varphi^{a}\leftrightarrow\theta^{a}
and
\varphi\leftrightarrow\theta
.

Now we show
\varphi^{M}(u)\leftrightarrow\varphi(u)
, where
0\leq n<N
,

st_{1+n}(M)
,
\varphi\in\Sigma_{1+n}
and
u\in M
.
Suppose
\varphi^{M}(u)
.
Pick a
\Sigma_{1}(\{st_{j}\}_{j<n})
-formula
\theta
such that

\varphi^{M}(u)\leftrightarrow\theta^{M}(u)
and
\varphi(u)\leftrightarrow\theta(u)
.

\theta(u)
follows logically, and
\varphi(u)
follows.
Conversely assume
\varphi(u)
. Then we obtain
\theta(u)
, and
(
3
) yields
\theta^{M}(u)
, and hence
\varphi^{M}(u)
.

\Box

Lemma 2.5

S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
is an extension of
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}
.
Namely
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
proves
\Pi_{N}
-Collection.

Proof
. Argue in
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
Let
A(x,y)
be a
\Pi_{N}
-formula in the language of set theory.
We obtain by the axiom (
1
)
and Lemma
2.4

A(x,y)\leftrightarrow\exists b(st_{N}(b)\land x,y\in b\land A^{b}(x,y))

(5)

Assume
\forall x\in a\exists yA(x,y)
.
Then we obtain

\forall x\in a\exists y\exists b(st_{N}(b)\land x,y\in b\land A^{b}(x,y))
by (
5
).
Since
st_{N}(b)\land x,y\in b\land A^{b}(x,y)
is a

\Sigma_{1}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
-formula,
pick a set
c
such that

\forall x\in a\exists y\in c\exists b\in c(st_{N}(b)\land x,y\in b\land A^{b}(x,y))

by
\Delta_{0}(\{st_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
-Collection.
Again by (
5
) we obtain
\forall x\in a\exists y\in cA(x,y)
.

\Box

3
Ordinals for
\Pi_{N}
-Collection

In this section up to subsection
3.2

we work in a set theory
{\sf ZFC}(\{St_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
,
where each
St_{i}
is a unary predicate symbol.
Let
St_{0}
denote the set of uncountable cardinals below
\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

\Omega
and
\mathbb{I}_{N}
are strongly critical numbers with
\Omega<\mathbb{I}_{N}
, i.e.,
non-zero ordinals closed under the binary Veblen function

\varphi\alpha\beta=\varphi_{\alpha}(\beta)
.
We assume that

St_{i+1}\subset St_{i}
for
i<N
,
each
St_{i}
is an unbounded class of ordinals below
\mathbb{I}_{N}
such that
the least element of
St_{i}
is larger than
\Omega
,
\Omega<\min(\bigcup_{0<i\leq N}St_{i})
.
The predicate
St_{i}
is identified with the class
\{\alpha\in ON:\alpha\in St_{i}\}
.

\alpha^{\dagger i}
denotes the least ordinal
>\alpha
in the class
St_{i}
when
\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

\alpha^{\dagger i}:=\mathbb{I}_{N}
if
\alpha\geq\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
Put

\alpha^{\dagger}:=\alpha^{\dagger 1}
.
Let
SSt_{i}:=\{\alpha^{\dagger i}:\alpha\in ON\}
and
LSt_{i}=St_{i}\setminus SSt_{i}
.

\Gamma_{a}
denotes the
a
-th strongly critical number.
For ordinals
\alpha
,
\varepsilon(\alpha)
denotes the least epsilon number above
\alpha
, and

\Gamma(\alpha)
the least strongly critical number above
\alpha
.
For ordinals
\alpha,\beta
, and
\gamma
,

\gamma=\alpha-\beta
designates that
\alpha=\beta+\gamma
.

\alpha\dot{+}\beta
denotes the sum
\alpha+\beta

when
\alpha+\beta
equals to the commutative (natural) sum
\alpha\#\beta
, i.e., when
either
\alpha=0
or
\alpha=\alpha_{0}+\omega^{\alpha_{1}}
with

\omega^{\alpha_{1}+1}>\beta
.

u,v,w,x,y,z,\ldots
range over sets in the universe,

a,b,c,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\ldots
range over ordinals
<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
, and

\xi,\zeta,\eta,\ldots
range over ordinals
<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
and ordinals
\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
are denoted by
\pi,\kappa,\rho,\sigma,\tau,\lambda,\ldots
.

Let
\mathbb{S}\in St_{i}
with
i>0
.
A ‘Mahlo degree’
m(\pi)
of ordinals
\pi<\mathbb{S}

with higher reflections is defined to be a finite function

f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
Let
\Lambda\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a strongly critical number.
To denote ordinals
<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
it is convenient for us to introduce an ordinal function

\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

\xi<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and
b<\Lambda
as in
[
4
,
5
]
,
which is a
b
-th iterate of the exponential

\tilde{\theta}_{1}(\xi;\Lambda)=\Lambda^{\xi}

with the base

\Lambda
.

Definition 3.1

Let
\Lambda\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a strongly critical number.

\varphi_{b}(\xi)
denotes the binary Veblen function on

(\mathbb{I}_{N})^{\dagger 0}=\omega_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1}
with
\varphi_{0}(\xi)=\omega^{\xi}
, and

\tilde{\varphi}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda):=\varphi_{b}(\Lambda\cdot\xi)
.

Let
b,\xi<(\mathbb{I}_{N})^{\dagger 0}
.

\theta_{b}(\xi)
[
\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)
] denotes
a
b
-th iterate of
\varphi_{0}(\xi)=\omega^{\xi}
[of
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}(\xi;\Lambda)=\Lambda^{\xi}
], resp.
Specifically ordinals

\theta_{b}(\xi),\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)<(\mathbb{I}_{N})^{\dagger 0}
are defined by recursion on
b
as follows.

\theta_{0}(\xi)=\tilde{\theta}_{0}(\xi;\Lambda)=\xi
,

\theta_{\omega^{b}}(\xi)=\varphi_{b}(\xi)
,

\tilde{\theta}_{\omega^{b}}(\xi;\Lambda)=\tilde{\varphi}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)
, and

\theta_{c\dot{+}\omega^{b}}(\xi)=\theta_{c}(\theta_{\omega^{b}}(\xi))
,

\tilde{\theta}_{c\dot{+}\omega^{b}}(\xi;\Lambda)=\tilde{\theta}_{c}(\tilde{\theta}_{\omega^{b}}(\xi;\Lambda);\Lambda)
.

A finite set
SC(a)
of strongly critical numbers is defined recursively as follows.

SC(0)=\emptyset
,
SC(a)=\bigcup_{i\leq m}SC(a_{i})
for
a=\omega^{a_{m}}\dot{+}\cdots\dot{+}\omega^{a_{0}}
,
and

SC(a)=SC(b)\cup SC(c)
for
a=\varphi_{b}(c)
if
a
is not strongly critical.

SC(a)=\{a\}
if
a
is strongly critical.

Let
\Lambda\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a strongly critical number.
Let us define a normal form of non-zero ordinals
\xi<\Gamma(\Lambda)
and a finite set
SC_{\Lambda}(\xi)

of strongly critical numbers
<\Lambda
.
Let
\xi=\Lambda^{\zeta}
.
If
\zeta<\Lambda^{\zeta}
, then
\tilde{\theta}_{1}(\zeta;\Lambda)
is the normal form of
\xi
,
denoted by

\xi=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{1}(\zeta;\Lambda)
and
SC_{\Lambda}(\xi)=SC_{\Lambda}(\zeta)\cup\{1\}
..
Assume
\zeta=\Lambda^{\zeta}
, and let
b>0
be the maximal ordinal such that
there exists an ordinal
\eta
with

\zeta=\tilde{\varphi}_{b}(\eta;\Lambda)>\eta
.
Then
\xi=\tilde{\varphi}_{b}(\eta;\Lambda)=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{\omega^{b}}(\eta;\Lambda)
and

SC_{\Lambda}(\xi)=SC_{\Lambda}(b)\cup SC_{\Lambda}(\eta)
.

Let
\xi=\Lambda^{\zeta_{m}}a_{m}+\cdots+\Lambda^{\zeta_{0}}a_{0}
, where

\zeta_{m}>\cdots>\zeta_{0}
and
0<a_{0},\ldots,a_{m}<\Lambda
.
Let
\Lambda^{\zeta_{i}}=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{i}}(\eta_{i};\Lambda)
with

b_{i}=\omega^{c_{i}}
for each
i
.
Then

\xi=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\eta_{m};\Lambda)\cdot a_{m}+\cdots+\tilde{\theta}_{b_{0}}(\eta_{0};\Lambda)\cdot a_{0}

and

SC_{\Lambda}(\xi):=\{a_{i}:i\leq m\}\cup\bigcup_{i\leq m}(SC_{\Lambda}(b_{i})\cup SC_{\Lambda}(\eta_{i}))
, where

SC_{\Lambda}(0)=\emptyset
.

Definition 3.2

Let
\xi<\Gamma(\Lambda)
be a non-zero ordinal with its normal form:

\xi=\sum_{i\leq m}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{i}}(\xi_{i};\Lambda)\cdot a_{i}=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)\cdot a_{m}+\cdots+\tilde{\theta}_{b_{0}}(\xi_{0};\Lambda)\cdot a_{0}

(6)

where

\tilde{\theta}_{b_{i}}(\xi_{i};\Lambda)>\xi_{i}
,

\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)>\cdots>\tilde{\theta}_{b_{0}}(\xi_{0};\Lambda)
,

b_{i}=\omega^{c_{i}}<\Lambda
, and

0<a_{0},\ldots,a_{m}<\Lambda
.

\tilde{\theta}_{b_{0}}(\xi_{0};\Lambda)
is said to be the
tail
of
\xi
, denoted

\tilde{\theta}_{b_{0}}(\xi_{0};\Lambda)=tl(\xi)
, and

\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)
the
head
of
\xi
, denoted

\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)=hd(\xi)
.

1.

\zeta
is a
segment
of
\xi

iff there exists an
n\,(0\leq n\leq m+1)

such that

\zeta=_{NF}\sum_{i\geq n}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{i}}(\xi_{i};\Lambda)\cdot a_{i}=\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)\cdot a_{m}+\cdots+\tilde{\theta}_{b_{n}}(\xi_{n};\Lambda)\cdot a_{n}

for
\xi
in (
6
).

2.

Let
\zeta=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)
with
\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)>\xi
and
b=\omega^{b_{0}}
,
and
c
be an ordinal.
An ordinal
\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\zeta;\Lambda)
is defined recursively as follows.
If
b\geq c
, then
\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\zeta;\Lambda)=\tilde{\theta}_{b-c}(\xi;\Lambda)
.
Let
c>b
.
If
\xi>0
, then

\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\zeta;\Lambda)=\tilde{\theta}_{-(c-b)}(\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda);\Lambda)
for the head term

hd(\xi)=\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)
of

\xi
in (
6
).
If
\xi=0
, then let
\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\zeta;\Lambda)=0
.

3.

Let
\xi<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
be such that

SC_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\xi)\subset\Lambda
for a strongly critical number
\Lambda<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
Then
\xi[\Lambda:\mathbb{I}_{N}]
denotes an ordinal
<\Gamma(\Lambda)
obtained from
\xi

by changing the base
\mathbb{I}_{N}
into
\Lambda
.
This means that

\xi[\Lambda:\mathbb{I}_{N}]
is obtained from
\xi
in (
6
)
by replacing

\theta_{b_{i}}(\xi_{i};\mathbb{I}_{N})\cdot a_{i}
by
\theta_{b_{i}}(\xi_{i}[\Lambda:\mathbb{I}_{N}];\Lambda)\cdot a_{i}
.

Proposition 3.3

Let
\xi,\zeta<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
be such that

SC_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\xi,\zeta)\subset\Lambda
for a strongly critical number
\Lambda<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
Then

\xi<\zeta
iff
\xi[\Lambda:\mathbb{I}_{N}]<\zeta[\Lambda:\mathbb{I}_{N}]
.

Definition 3.4

1.

A function
f:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
with a
finite
support

{\rm supp}(f)=\{c<\Lambda:f(c)\neq 0\}
is said to be a
finite function

with
base

\Lambda

if

\forall i>0(a_{i}=1)
and
a_{0}=1
when
b_{0}>1

in

f(c)=_{NF}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{m}}(\xi_{m};\Lambda)\cdot a_{m}+\cdots+\tilde{\theta}_{b_{0}}(\xi_{0};\Lambda)\cdot a_{0}

for any
c\in{\rm supp}(f)
.

It is identified with the finite function
f\!\upharpoonright\!{\rm supp}(f)
.
When
c\not\in{\rm supp}(f)
, let
f(c):=0
.

f,g,h,\ldots
range over finite functions.

Let

\mathrm{fld}(f):=\bigcup\{\{c,f(c)\}:c\in{\rm supp}(f)\}
.

For an ordinal
c
,
f_{c}
and
f^{c}
are restrictions of
f
to the domains

{\rm supp}(f_{c})=\{d\in{\rm supp}(f):d<c\}
and
{\rm supp}(f^{c})=\{d\in{\rm supp}(f):d\geq c\}
.

g_{c}*f^{c}
denotes the concatenated function such that

{\rm supp}(g_{c}*f^{c})={\rm supp}(g_{c})\cup{\rm supp}(f^{c})
,

(g_{c}*f^{c})(a)=g(a)
for
a<c
, and

(g_{c}*f^{c})(a)=f(a)
for
a\geq c
.

2.

Let
f
be a finite function and
c\leq\Lambda
,
\xi<\Gamma(\Lambda)
ordinals.
A relation
f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\xi
is defined by induction on the
cardinality of the finite set
\{d\in{\rm supp}(f):d>c\}
as follows.
If
f^{c}=\emptyset
, then
f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\xi
holds.
Let
f^{c}\neq\emptyset
.
If
f^{c+1}=\emptyset
, then

f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\xi
iff

f(c)<\xi
.
Otherwise for

d=\min\{d>0:c+d\in{\rm supp}(f)\}
,

f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\xi
iff
there exists a segment
\mu
of
\xi
such that

f(c)<\mu

and

f<_{\Lambda}^{c+d}\tilde{\theta}_{-d}(tl(\mu);\Lambda)
, where

tl(\mu)
is the tail of
\mu
with base
\Lambda
.

The following Proposition
3.5
is shown in
[
4
]
.

Proposition 3.5

1.

\zeta\leq\xi<\Gamma(\Lambda)\Rightarrow\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\zeta;\Lambda)\leq\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\xi;\Lambda)
.

2.

\tilde{\theta}_{c}(\tilde{\theta}_{-c}(\zeta;\Lambda);\Lambda)\leq\zeta
for
\zeta<\Gamma(\Lambda)
.

Although the following Proposition
3.6
is shown in
[
5
]
,
let us reproduce its proof.

Proposition 3.6

f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\xi\leq\zeta\Rightarrow f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\zeta
.

Proof
. By induction on the cardinality
n

of the finite set

\{d\in{\rm supp}(f):d>c\}=\{c+d_{1}<\cdots<c+d_{n}\}
with
c<c+d_{1}
.
If
n=0
, then there is nothing to prove.
Let
n>0
.
We have
f(c)<\mu
,
and
f<_{\Lambda}^{c+d_{1}}\tilde{\theta}_{-d_{1}}(tl(\mu);\Lambda)

for a segment
\mu
of
\xi
.
We show the existence of a segment
\lambda
of
\zeta
such that

\mu\leq\lambda
,
and
\tilde{\theta}_{-d_{1}}(tl(\mu);\Lambda)\leq\tilde{\theta}_{-d_{1}}(tl(\lambda);\Lambda)
.
Then IH yields
f<_{\Lambda}^{c+d_{1}}\tilde{\theta}_{-d_{1}}(tl(\lambda);\Lambda)
, and
f<_{\Lambda}^{c}\zeta
follows.

If
\mu
is a segment of
\zeta
, then
\lambda=\mu
works.
Otherwise
\xi<\zeta
and there exists a segment
\lambda
of
\zeta
such that
\mu<\lambda
,
and

tl(\mu)<tl(\lambda)
.
We obtain
\tilde{\theta}_{-d_{1}}(tl(\mu);\Lambda)\leq\tilde{\theta}_{-d_{1}}(tl(\lambda);\Lambda)

by Proposition
3.5
.
1
.

3.1
Skolem hulls and Mahlo classes

In this subsection Skolem hulls
C_{a}(\beta)
, collapsing functions
\psi
and
Mahlo classes
Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
are introduced.

\psi
-functions are introduced in Buchholz
[
8
]
.

Definition 3.7

Let
A\subset\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a set, and
\alpha\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
a limit ordinal.

\alpha\in M(A):\Leftrightarrow A\cap\alpha\mbox{ is stationary in }\alpha\Leftrightarrow\mbox{ every club subset of }\alpha\mbox{ meets }A.

In the following Definition
3.8
,

\varphi\alpha\beta=\varphi_{\alpha}(\beta)
denotes the binary Veblen function on
(\mathbb{I}_{N})^{\dagger 0}
.
For

a<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

\beta,c<\mathbb{I}_{N}
, and

\xi<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
define simultaneously
classes
C_{a}(\beta)\subset\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)\subset(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)\,(i>0)
, and
ordinals
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
and
\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\leq\kappa
by recursion on ordinals
a
as follows.

Definition 3.8

Let
a<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

\beta,c<\mathbb{I}_{N}
, and

\xi<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

1.

(Inductive definition of
C_{a}(\beta)
)

(a)

\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup\beta\subset C_{a}(\beta)
, where

\Omega\in SSt_{0}
.

(b)

If
x,y\in C_{a}(\beta)
,
then
x+y\in C_{a}(\beta)

and

\varphi xy\in C_{a}(\beta)
.

(c)

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}(b)
with
\pi\in C_{a}(\beta)\cap SSt_{0}\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
,

b\in C_{a}(\beta)\cap a
such that

\{\pi,b\}\subset C_{b}(\alpha)
.
Then
\alpha\in C_{a}(\beta)
.

(d)

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
with
b\in C_{a}(\beta)\cap a
.
Then

\alpha\in C_{a}(\beta)\cap(LSt_{N}\cup\{\mathbb{I}_{N}\})
.

(e)

Let
\alpha\in C_{a}(\beta)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
Then
\alpha^{\dagger i}\in C_{a}(\beta)\cap SSt_{i}
for each
0<i\leq N
.

(f)

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(b)

with

b<a
, and a finite function
f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
such that

\{\pi,b\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\beta)\cap C_{b}(\alpha)
.
Then
\alpha\in C_{a}(\beta)
.

2.

(Definitions of
Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
and
Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f)
for
0<i\leq N
)

The classes
Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
are defined for

c<\mathbb{I}_{N}
,
a<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and
\xi<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
By main induction on ordinals
\pi<\mathbb{I}_{N}

with subsidiary induction on
c<\mathbb{I}_{N}

we define

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
iff
\pi\in LSt_{i-1}
,

\{a,c,\xi\}\subset C_{a}(\pi)
and
the following condition is met for any
finite functions
f,g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})

such that
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{c}\xi
:

\mathrm{fld}(f,g)\subset C_{a}(\pi)\,\&\,\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g_{c})\Rightarrow\pi\in M(Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g_{c}*f^{c}))

where
\mathrm{fld}(f,g)=\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\mathrm{fld}(g)
and

\displaystyle Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d)):d\in{\rm supp}(f^{c})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d)):c\leq d\in{\rm supp}(f)\}.

Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g_{c})=\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,d}(g(d)):d\in{\rm supp}(g_{c})\}=\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,d}(g(d)):c>d\in{\rm supp}(g)\}
.
When
f=\emptyset
or
f^{c}=\emptyset
, let
Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\emptyset):=LSt_{i-1}
.

3.

(Definition of
\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
)

Let
a,\pi
be ordinals,
and

f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
a finite function.
Then
\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(a)
denotes the least ordinal
\kappa<\pi
such that

\kappa\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)\,\&\,C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\pi\subset\kappa\,\&\,\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\kappa)

(7)

if such a
\kappa
exists. Otherwise set
\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(a)=\pi
.

4.

\psi_{\Omega}(a):=\min(\{\Omega\}\cup\{\beta:C_{a}(\beta)\cap\Omega\subset\beta\})

and

\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a):=\min(\{\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup\{\kappa\in LSt_{N}:C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset\kappa\})

(8)

5.

For classes
A\subset\mathbb{I}_{N}
, let

\alpha\in M^{a}_{i,c}(A)
iff
\alpha\in A
and
for any finite functions

g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})

\alpha\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\,\&\,\mathrm{fld}(g_{c})\subset C_{a}(\alpha)\Rightarrow\alpha\in M\!\left(Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap A\right)

(9)

The following Propositions
3.9
,
3.10
and
3.11
are seen as in
[
5
]
.

Proposition 3.9

Assume
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\zeta)
and
\xi<\zeta
with
\xi\in C_{a}(\pi)
.
Then

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)\cap M^{a}_{i,c}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi))
.

Proof
. Proposition
3.6
yields

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
.

\pi\in M^{a}_{i,c}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi))
is seen from the function
f
such that

f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{c}\zeta
with
{\rm supp}(f)=\{c\}
and
f(c)=\xi
.

\Box

Proposition 3.10

Suppose
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
.

1.

Let
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{c}\xi
with
\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\pi)
.
Then

\pi\in M_{i,c}^{a}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c}))
.

2.

Let
\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(A)
for
d>c
and
A\subset\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
Then
\pi\in M_{i,c}^{a}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)\cap A)
.

Proof
.
3.10
.
1
.
Let
g
be a function such that
\pi\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})
with
\mathrm{fld}(g_{c})\subset C_{a}(\pi)
.
We obtain

\pi\in M\!\left(Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c})\right)

by Definition
3.8
.
2
of
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
.

3.10
.
2
.
Let
\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(A)
for
d>c
. Then
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)\cap A
.
Let
g
be a function such that
\pi\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})
with
\mathrm{fld}(g_{c})\subset C_{a}(\pi)
.
We obtain by (
9
) and
d>c
with the function

,

\pi\in M\!\left(Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)\cap A\right)
, where

and

.

Proposition 3.11

Each of

x\in C_{a}(y)
,

x\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f)
and
x=\psi^{f}_{\kappa}(a)

is a
\Delta_{1}(\{St_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
-predicate in
{\sf ZFC}(\{St_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
.

Proof
. An inspection of Definition
3.8
shows that

x\in C_{a}(y)
,
\psi^{f}_{\kappa}(a)
and
x\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f)

are simultaneously defined by recursion on

a<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
, in which
x\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f)
is defined by recursion on ordinals
x<\mathbb{I}_{N}

with subsidiary recursion on
c<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

3.2
A small large cardinal hypothesis

It is convenient for us to assume the existence of a small large cardinal in justification of
Definition
3.8
.

Shrewd cardinals
as well as
\mathcal{A}
-shrewd cardinals

are introduced by M. Rathjen
[
14
]
.

Definition 3.12

(Rathjen
[
14
]
)

Let
\eta>0
.
A cardinal
\kappa
is

\eta
-
shrewd
iff for any
P\subset V_{\kappa}
, and a
set-theoretic formula
\varphi(x,y)
if

V_{\kappa+\eta}\models\varphi[P,\kappa]
, then there are
0<\kappa_{0},\eta_{0}<\kappa
such that

V_{\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0}}\models\varphi[P\cap V_{\kappa_{0}},\kappa_{0}]
.
For classes
\mathcal{A}
,

\kappa
is

\mathcal{A}
-
\eta
-
shrewd
iff for any
P\subset V_{\kappa}
, and a formula

\varphi(x,y)
in the language
\{\in,R\}
with a unary predicate
R

if

(V_{\kappa+\eta};\mathcal{A})\models\varphi[P,\kappa]
, then there are
0<\kappa_{0},\eta_{0}<\kappa
such that

(V_{\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0}};\mathcal{A})\models\varphi[P\cap V_{\kappa_{0}},\kappa_{0}]
,
where
(V_{\alpha};\mathcal{A})
denotes the structure
(V_{\alpha},\in;\mathcal{A}\cap V_{\alpha})
,
and for the formulas
\varphi
in the language

\{\in,R\}
,
R(t)
is interpreted as
t\in\mathcal{A}\cap V_{\alpha}

in
(V_{\alpha};\mathcal{A})\models\varphi
.

Obviously each
\mathcal{A}
-
\eta
-shrewd cardinal is
\eta
-shrewd.
We see easily that each
\eta
-shrewd cardinal is regular.
A cardinal
\kappa
is said to be
(<\eta)
-shrewd

[
\mathcal{A}
-
(<\eta)
-shrewd
] if

\kappa
is
\delta
-shrewd [
\mathcal{A}
-
\delta
-shrewd] for every
\delta<\eta
, resp.

On the other side subtle cardinals are introduced by R. Jensen and K. Kunen.
The following Lemma
3.13
is shown in
[
14
]
by Rathjen.

Lemma 3.13

(Lemma 2.7 of
[
14
]
)

Let
\pi
be a subtle cardinal.
The set

\{\kappa\in V_{\pi}:(V_{\pi};\mathcal{A})\models\mbox{`$\kappa$ is $\mathcal{A}$-shrewd'}\}

of
\mathcal{A}
-shrewd cardinals in
(V_{\pi};\mathcal{A})

is stationary in
\pi
for each class
\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 3.14

Let
\pi
be a cardinal.
The classes
\mathcal{B}_{n}
and
\mathcal{A}_{n}
are defined recursively for
n<\omega
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\kappa\in V_{\pi}:V_{\pi}\models\mbox{`$\kappa$ is an uncountable cardinal'}\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\langle i,\sigma\rangle:i\leq n,\sigma\in\mathcal{B}_{i}\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\kappa\in V_{\pi}:(V_{\pi};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\mbox{`$\kappa$ is an
$\mathcal{A}_{n}$-shrewd cardinal'}\}.

We say that a cardinal
\kappa\in V_{\pi}
is
n
-
shrewd
in
\pi
iff

\kappa\in\mathcal{B}_{n}
.
An
n
-shrewd carinal is an
n
-shrewd limit
iff the set of
n
-shrewd cardinals is cofinal in it.

\mathcal{B}_{1}
is the set of shrewd cardinals in
V_{\pi}
, and a
1
-shrewd cardinal
is a shrewd cardinal in
\pi
.
Each
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}
-shrewd cardinal is
\mathcal{A}_{n}
-shrewd, and
each
(n+1)
-shrewd cardinal is
n
-shrewd.

Lemma 3.15

Let
\pi
be a subtle cardinal.

1.

The set of
n
-shrewd cardinals in
\pi
is stationary in
\pi
for each
n<\omega
.

2.

Let
\kappa
be an
(n+1)
-shrewd cardinal in
\pi
.
If

(V_{\kappa+\eta};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\varphi[P,\kappa]

for
0<\eta<\pi
,
P\subset V_{\kappa}
and a formula
\varphi(x,y)
in
\{\in,R\}
,
then there are an
n
-shrewd limit
\kappa_{0}<\kappa

and

0<\eta_{0}<\kappa
such that

(V_{\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0}};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\varphi[P\cap V_{\kappa_{0}},\kappa_{0}]
.

Proof
.
3.15
.
1
.
From Lemma
3.13
we see that
the set of

\mathcal{A}_{n-1}
-shrewd cardinals is stationary in
a subtle cardinal
\pi
.

3.15
.
2
.
Let
\kappa
be an
(n+1)
-shrewd cardinal in
\pi
.
Then
\kappa
is
n
-shrewd, and hence

(V_{\kappa+\eta};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\exists x(x\in P)\land R(\langle n,\kappa\rangle)

for each
P=\{\alpha\}\subset V_{\kappa}
with
\kappa<\kappa+\eta<\pi
.
Since
\kappa
is
\mathcal{A}_{n}
-shrewd, there are
0<\kappa_{0},\eta_{0}<\kappa
such that

(V_{\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0}};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\exists x(x\in P\cap V_{\kappa_{0}})\land R(\langle n,\kappa_{0}\rangle)
.
This means that
\alpha<\kappa_{0}
is
n
-shrewd.
Therefore

\kappa
is an
n
-shrewd limit.

Suppose

(V_{\kappa+\eta};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\varphi[P,\kappa]

for
0<\eta<\pi
,
P\subset V_{\kappa}
and a formula
\varphi(x,y)
in
\{\in,R\}
.
Then
(V_{\kappa+\eta};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\varphi[P,\kappa]\land R(\langle n,\kappa\rangle)\land\forall\alpha<\kappa\exists\sigma<\kappa(\sigma>\alpha\land R(\langle n,\sigma\rangle))
.
Since
\kappa
is
\mathcal{A}_{n}
-shrewd,
there are an
n
-shrewd limit
\kappa_{0}<\kappa

and

0<\eta_{0}<\kappa
such that

(V_{\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0}};\mathcal{A}_{n})\models\varphi[P\cap V_{\kappa_{0}},\kappa_{0}]
.

\Box

In this subsection we work in an extension
T
of
{\sf ZFC}
by adding
the axiom stating that
there exists a regular cardinal
\mathbb{I}_{N}
in which the set of
N
-shrewd cardinals is stationary.

\Omega
denotes the least uncountable ordinal
\omega_{1}
,
For
0<i\leq N
,

St_{i}=\mathcal{B}_{i}
the class of
i
-shrewd cardinals in
V_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.

LSt_{i}
denotes the class of

i
-shrewd limits in
V_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
Let
St_{N+1}=SSt_{N+1}=\{\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
with
\mathbb{I}_{N}=\Omega^{\dagger(N+1)}
.
Also
St_{0}
denotes the class of uncountable cardinals in
V_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
, and

LSt_{0}
the class of limit cardinals in
V_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
A
successor
n
-shrewd cardinal
is an
n
-shrewd cardinal in
V_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
, but not in
LSt_{n}
.

Lemma 3.16

T\vdash\forall a<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})[\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)<\mathbb{I}_{N}]
.

Proof
. We see that the set

C=\{\kappa<\mathbb{I}_{N}:C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset\kappa\}

is a club subset of the regular cardinal
\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
This shows the existence of a
\kappa\in LSt_{N}\cap C
, and hence

\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)<\mathbb{I}_{N}
by the definition (
8
).

\Box

\alpha^{\dagger i^{(k)}}
is defined by recursion on
k<\omega
by

\alpha^{\dagger i^{(0)}}=\alpha
and

\alpha^{\dagger i^{(k+1)}}=(\alpha^{\dagger i^{(k)}})^{\dagger i}
.

Proposition 3.17

Let

a\in C_{a}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))
,
b\in C_{b}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b))
,

c\in C_{c}(\psi_{\Omega}(c))
and
d\in C_{d}(\psi_{\Omega}(d))
.

1.

\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
iff

a<b
.

2.

\Omega^{\dagger N^{(k)}}<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
for every
k<\omega
.

3.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)
and
0<k<\omega
.
Then
\alpha^{\dagger N^{(k)}}<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
iff
\alpha<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
.

\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)<\alpha^{\dagger N^{(k)}}
iff
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)\leq\alpha
.

4.

\psi_{\Omega}(c)<\psi_{\Omega}(d)
iff

c<d
.

5.

If
x<y
, then
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(x)\leq\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(y)
.

Proof
.
3.17
.
2
and
3.17
.
3
.
Let
\beta=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
.
By the definition (
8
) and

\Omega\in C_{b}(\beta)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset\beta
we obtain
\Omega<\beta
.
Let
\alpha\in\{\Omega,\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\}
.
If
\alpha<\beta
, then
\beta\in LSt_{N}
yields
\alpha^{\dagger N^{(k)}}<\beta
.

3.17
.
5
.
We obtain
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(y)\in LSt_{N}
and

C_{x}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(y))\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset C_{y}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(y))\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(y)

by
x<y
and Lemma
3.16
.
Hence
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(x)\leq\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(y)
.

\Box

3.3
\psi
-functions

In this subsection we work in
{\sf ZFC}(\{St_{i}\}_{0<i\leq N})
with
St_{i}=\mathcal{B}_{i}
, and
show that
\psi_{i,\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)<\mathbb{S}
for
i
-shrewd cardinal
\mathbb{S}

in Lemma
3.19
,
and introduce an
irreducibility
of finite functions in Definition
3.24

using Lemma
3.21
,
which is needed to define a normal form in ordinal notations.

Lemma 3.18

Let
\mathbb{S}
be an
i
-shrewd cardinal with
0<i\leq N
,

a<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
h:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
a finite function
with

\{a\}\cup SC(h)\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
. Then

\mathbb{S}\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(h)\cap M(Mh^{a}_{i,0}(h))
.

Proof
. By induction on
\xi<\varphi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(0)
we show
\mathbb{S}\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)

for
\{a,c,\xi\}\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.

Let
\{a,c,\xi\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})

with
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{c}\xi
and
a<\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
We show
\mathbb{S}\in M^{a}_{i,c}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c}))
,
which yields
\mathbb{S}\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
.
IH yields
\mathbb{S}\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c})
by Proposition
3.5
.
2
,

\tilde{\theta}_{-e}(\zeta;\mathbb{I}_{N})\leq\zeta
.
By the definition (
9
) it suffices to show that

\forall g[\mathbb{S}\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\,\&\,SC(g_{c})\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})\Rightarrow\mathbb{S}\in M\left(Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c})\right)].

Let
g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
be a finite function such that
\mathrm{fld}(g_{c})\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
and

\mathbb{S}\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})
.
We have to show
\mathbb{S}\in M(A\cap B)
for
A=Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap\mathbb{S}
and

B=Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c})\cap\mathbb{S}
.
Let
C
be a club subset of
\mathbb{S}
.

We have

\mathbb{S}\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c})
,
and

\{a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g_{c},f^{c})\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.
Pick a
b<\mathbb{S}
so that
\{a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g_{c},f^{c})\subset C_{a}(b)
.
Since the cardinality of the set
C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
is equal to
\mathbb{S}
, pick a bijection

F:\mathbb{S}\to C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.
Each
\alpha<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with
\alpha\in C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
is identified with its code, denoted
by
F^{-1}(\alpha)<\mathbb{S}
.
Let
P
be the class

P=\{(\pi,d,\alpha)\in\mathbb{S}^{3}:\pi\in Mh^{F(\alpha)}_{i,F(d)}(F(\xi))\}
,
where

F(d)\in C_{a}(\mathbb{S})\cap(c+1)
and

F(\alpha)<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with

\{F(d),F(\alpha)\}\subset C_{a}(\pi)
.
For fixed
i
,
a
and
c
, the set

\{(d,\zeta)\in\left(C_{a}(\mathbb{S})\cap(c+1)\right)\times\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N}):\mathbb{S}\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\zeta)\}

is defined from the classes
P
and
\{St_{j}\}_{j<i}
by recursion on ordinals
d\leq c
.

Let
\varphi
be a formula in
\{\in\}\cup\{St_{j}\}_{j<i}

such that
(V_{\mathbb{S}+c^{\dagger i}};\{St_{j}\}_{j<i})\models\varphi[P,C,\mathbb{S},b]
iff

\mathbb{S}\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f^{c})
and
C
is a club subset of
\mathbb{S}
,
where
\{St_{j}\}_{j<i}=\mathcal{A}_{i-1}
.
Since
\mathbb{S}
is
i
-shrewd in
V_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
, pick
b<\mathbb{S}_{0}<\eta<\mathbb{S}
such that

(V_{\mathbb{S}_{0}+\eta};\{St_{j}\}_{j<i})\models\varphi[P\cap\mathbb{S}_{0},C\cap\mathbb{S}_{0},\mathbb{S}_{0},b]
.
We obtain
\mathbb{S}_{0}\in A\cap B\cap C
.

Therefore
\mathbb{S}\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
is shown
for every

\{c,\xi\}\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.
This yields
\mathbb{S}\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(h)
for

\mathrm{fld}(h)\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.

\mathbb{S}\in M(Mh^{a}_{i,0}(h))
follows from the
i
-shrewdness of
\mathbb{S}
.

\Box

Lemma 3.19

Let
\mathbb{S}
be
an
i
-shrewd cardinal,
a
an ordinal, and

f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
a finite function
such that
\{a,\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.
Then

\psi_{i,\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)<\mathbb{S}
holds.

Proof
. Suppose
\{a,\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})
.
By Lemma
3.18
we obtain

\mathbb{S}\in M(Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f))
.
The set

C=\{\kappa<\mathbb{S}:C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\kappa,\{a,\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\kappa)\}

is a club subset of
the regular cardinal
\mathbb{S}
,
and
Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)
is stationary in
\mathbb{S}
.
This shows the existence of a
\kappa\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(f)\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}
, and hence

\psi_{i,\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)<\mathbb{S}
by the definition (
7
).

\Box

Proposition 3.20

Let
\alpha
be either
\Omega
or an
i
-shrewd cardinal for
0<i\leq N

and
\mathbb{S}=\alpha^{\dagger i}
.
Assume
\{a,\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\mathbb{S})

for an ordinal
a
and a finite function
f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
Then

\alpha^{\dagger j}<\psi^{f}_{i,\mathbb{S}}(a)
for every
j<i
, and
\psi^{f}_{i,\mathbb{S}}(a)\in LSt_{i-1}\setminus St_{i}
.

Proof
. Let
\kappa=\psi^{f}_{i,\mathbb{S}}(a)<\mathbb{S}
.
We obtain
\alpha\in C_{a}(\kappa)
by
\alpha^{\dagger i}=\mathbb{S}\in C_{a}(\kappa)
,
and
\alpha^{\dagger j}\in C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{S}
for
\mathbb{S}\in LSt_{j}
.

\alpha<\kappa
is seen from

\alpha^{\dagger j}\in C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\kappa

in the definition (
7
).

\Box

The following Lemma
3.21
and
Corollary
3.23
are seen as in
[
5
]
.

Lemma 3.21

Assume
\mathbb{I}_{N}>\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\xi)\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi_{0})
,
\xi_{0}\neq 0
,
and
d<c
.
Moreover let
\xi_{1}\in C_{a}(\pi)
for

\xi_{1}\leq\tilde{\theta}_{c-d}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
and
tl(\xi)\geq\xi_{1}
when
\xi\neq 0
.
Then

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\xi+\xi_{1})\cap M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\xi+\xi_{1}))
.

Proof
.
\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\xi+\xi_{1}))
follows from
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\xi+\xi_{1})

and
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi_{0})\subset M^{a}_{i,c}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\emptyset))

by Proposition
3.10
.
1
.

Let
f
be a finite function such that

\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\pi)
, and

f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{d}\xi+\xi_{1}
.
We show
\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f^{d}))
by main induction on
the cardinality of the finite set
\{e\in{\rm supp}(f):e>d\}

with subsidiary induction on
\xi_{1}
.

First let
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{d}\mu
for a segment
\mu
of
\xi
. By Proposition
3.9
we obtain

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(\mu)
and
\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f^{d}))
.

In what follows let
f(d)=\xi+\zeta
with
\zeta<\xi_{1}
.
By SIH we obtain
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d))\cap M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d)))
.
If
\{e\in{\rm supp}(f):e>d\}=\emptyset
, then
Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f^{d})=Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d))
, and we are done.
Otherwise let
e=\min\{e\in{\rm supp}(f):e>d\}
.
By SIH we can assume
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{e}\tilde{\theta}_{-(e-d)}(tl(\xi_{1});\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
We obtain
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{e}\tilde{\theta}_{-(e-d)}(\tilde{\theta}_{c-d}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N});\mathbb{I}_{N})=\tilde{\theta}_{-e}(\tilde{\theta}_{c}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N});\mathbb{I}_{N})

by
\xi_{1}\leq\tilde{\theta}_{c-d}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N})
, Propositions
3.6
and

3.5
.
1
.
We claim that
\pi\in M^{a}_{i,c_{0}}(Mh_{i,c_{0}}^{a}(f^{c_{0}}))
for
c_{0}=\min\{c,e\}
.
If
c=e
, then the claim follows from the assumption
\pi\in Mh_{i,c}^{a}(\xi_{0})
and
f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{e}\xi_{0}
.
Let
e=c+e_{0}>c
. Then
\tilde{\theta}_{-e}(\tilde{\theta}_{c}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N});\mathbb{I}_{N})=\tilde{\theta}_{-e_{0}}(hd(\xi_{0});\mathbb{I}_{N})
, and

f<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{c}\xi_{0}
with
f(c)=0
yields the claim.
Let
c=e+c_{1}>e
. Then
\tilde{\theta}_{-e}(\tilde{\theta}_{c}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N});\mathbb{I}_{N})=\tilde{\theta}_{c_{1}}(\xi_{0};\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
MIH yields the claim.

On the other hand we have

Mh_{i,d}^{a}(f^{d})=Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d))\cap Mh_{i,c_{0}}^{a}(f^{c_{0}})
.

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d))\cap M^{a}_{i,c_{0}}(Mh_{i,c_{0}}^{a}(f^{c_{0}}))
with
d<c_{0}
yields by
Proposition
3.10
.
2
,

\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d))\cap Mh_{i,c_{0}}^{a}(f^{c_{0}}))
, i.e.,

\pi\in M^{a}_{i,d}(Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f^{d}))
.

\Box

Definition 3.22

For finite functions
f,g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g)\prec Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)
iff the following holds:

\forall\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)\left(\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset C_{a}(\pi)\Rightarrow\pi\in M(Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g))\right).

Corollary 3.23

Let
f,g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})

be finite functions and
c\in{\rm supp}(f)
.
Assume that
there exists an ordinal

d<c

such that

(d,c)\cap{\rm supp}(f)=(d,c)\cap{\rm supp}(g)=\emptyset
,

g_{d}=f_{d}
,

g(d)<f(d)+\tilde{\theta}_{c-d}(f(c);\mathbb{I}_{N})\cdot\omega
,
and

g<_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}^{c}f(c)
.

Then

Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g)\prec Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)
holds.
In particular if
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)
and

\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset C_{a}(\pi)
, then

\psi_{i,\pi}^{g}(a)<\pi
.

Proof
. Let
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)=\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,e}(f(e)):e\in{\rm supp}(f)\}
and

\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset C_{a}(\pi)
.
Lemma
3.21
with

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d))\cap Mh^{a}_{i,c}(f(c))
yields

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,d}(g(d))\cap M^{a}_{i,c}(Mh^{a}_{i,c}(g^{c}))
.
On the other hand we have
\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g_{d})=\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,e}(f(e)):e\in{\rm supp}(f)\cap d\}
.
Hence
\pi\in M(Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g))
.

Now suppose
\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset C_{a}(\pi)
.
The set

C=\{\kappa<\pi:C_{a}(\kappa)\cap\pi\subset\kappa,\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset C_{a}(\kappa)\}

is a club subset of the regular cardinal
\pi
,
and
Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g)
is stationary in
\pi
.
This shows the existence of a
\kappa\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g)\cap C\cap\pi
, and hence

\psi_{i,\pi}^{g}(a)<\pi
by the definition (
7
).

\Box

Definition 3.24

An
irreducibility
of finite functions
f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})

is defined by induction on the cardinality

n
of the finite set
{\rm supp}(f)
.
If
n\leq 1
,
f
is defined to be irreducible.
Let
n\geq 2
and
c<c+d
be the largest two elements in
{\rm supp}(f)
, and let
g
be
a finite function
such that
{\rm supp}(g)={\rm supp}(f_{c})\cup\{c\}
,
g_{c}=f_{c}
and

g(c)=f(c)+\tilde{\theta}_{d}(f(c+d);\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

Then
f
is irreducible iff

tl(f(c))>\tilde{\theta}_{d}(f(c+d);\mathbb{I}_{N})
and

g
is irreducible.

Definition 3.25

Let
f,g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})

be irreducible finite functions, and
b
an ordinal.
Let us define a relation
f<^{b}_{lx}g

by induction on the cardinality
\#\{e\in{\rm supp}(f)\cup{\rm supp}(g):e\geq b\}
as follows.

f<^{b}_{lx}g
holds iff
f^{b}\neq g^{b}
and
for the ordinal
c=\min\{c\geq b:f(c)\neq g(c)\}
,
one of the following conditions is met:

1.

f(c)<g(c)
and let
\mu
be the shortest segment of
g(c)
such that
f(c)<\mu
.
Then for any
c<c+d\in{\rm supp}(f)
,
if
tl(\mu)\leq\tilde{\theta}_{d}(f(c+d);\mathbb{I}_{N})
, then

f<_{lx}^{c+d}g
holds.

2.

f(c)>g(c)
and let
\nu
be the shortest segment of
f(c)
such that
\nu>g(c)
.
Then there exist a
c<c+d\in{\rm supp}(g)
such that

f<_{lx}^{c+d}g
and

tl(\nu)\leq\tilde{\theta}_{d}(g(c+d);\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

In
[
4
]

the following Proposition
3.26
is shown.

Proposition 3.26

Let
f:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
If
f<^{0}_{lx}g
, then

Mh^{a}_{i,0}(f)\prec Mh^{a}_{i,0}(g)
.

Proposition 3.27

Let
f,g:\mathbb{I}_{N}\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N})
be
irreducible functions, and assume that

\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(b)<\pi
and
\psi_{i,\kappa}^{g}(a)<\kappa
.

Then
\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(b)<\psi_{i,\kappa}^{g}(a)
iff one of the following cases holds:

1.

\pi\leq\psi_{i,\kappa}^{g}(a)
.

2.

b<a
,
\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(b)<\kappa
, and

\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,b\}\subset C_{a}(\psi_{i,\kappa}^{g}(a))
.

3.

b>a
, and
\mathrm{fld}(g)\cup\{\kappa,a\}\not\subset C_{b}(\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(b))
.

4.

b=a
,
\kappa<\pi
, and

\kappa\not\in C_{b}(\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(b))
.

5.

b=a
,
\pi=\kappa
,
\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\psi_{i,\kappa}^{g}(a))
, and

f<^{0}_{lx}g
.

6.

b=a
,
\pi=\kappa
,

\mathrm{fld}(g)\not\subset C_{b}(\psi_{i,\pi}^{f}(b))
.

Proof
. This is seen from Proposition
3.26
as in
[
2
]
.

3.4
A computable notation system for
\Pi_{N}
-collection

Although Propositions
3.17
,
3.20
, and

3.27
suffice for us to define a computable notation system for

C_{\varepsilon(\mathbb{I}_{N})}(0)
, we need a notation system closed under
Mostowski collapsings
to remove stable ordinals from derivations as in
[
5
]
,
cf. section
5
.
Two new constructors
\mathbb{I}_{N}[\cdot]
and
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
are used to generate terms in
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

Definition 3.28

\rho\prec\sigma
denotes the transitive closure
of the relation
\{(\rho,\sigma):\exists f,a(\rho=\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(a))\}
.
Let
\rho\preceq\sigma:\Leftrightarrow\rho\prec\sigma\lor\rho=\sigma
.

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}
and
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
.
We define a set
M_{\rho}=C_{b}(\rho)
from
\rho
in (
10
)
in such a way that
C_{b}(\rho)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\rho
.
Then a Mostowski collapsing
M_{\rho}\ni\alpha\mapsto\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
in Definition
3.33

maps ordinal terms
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
to
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]<\mathbb{S}
isomorphically.
The transitive collapse
(M_{\rho})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}=\{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\alpha\in M_{\rho}\}

is an initial segment in
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
such that

(M_{\rho})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}<\kappa
if
\rho<\kappa\prec\mathbb{S}
.
Note that both
\rho
and
\kappa
can be interpreted as uncountable cardinals, and
the cardinality of the set
M_{\rho}
is equal to
\rho
.

Let us define simultaneously the followings:
A set
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
of terms over constants
0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}
and
constructors
+,\varphi,\psi
,
\mathbb{I}_{N}[*]
,
*^{\dagger i}\,(0<i\leq N)
, and

*_{0}[*_{1}/*_{2}]
.
Its subsets

SSt_{i}
,
LSt_{i}
with
St_{i}=SSt_{i}\cup LSt_{i}
, and sets

M_{\rho}\,(\rho\in\Psi)
,
finite sets

K_{X}(\alpha)
of subterms of
\alpha
for
X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
Let
SSt=\bigcup_{0<i\leq N}SSt_{i}
and
LSt=\bigcup_{0<i\leq N}LSt_{i}
.
For each
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, there exists a unique
i
such that
\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}
.

For
i>0
,
\kappa\in St_{i}
is intended to designate that
\kappa
is an
i
-shrewd cardinal,
or
\kappa
is an
i
-stable ordinal.

\kappa\in SSt_{i}
[
\kappa\in LSt_{i}
] is intended to designate that
\kappa
is a
successor
i
-stable ordinal [
\kappa
is a limit of
i
-stable ordinals], resp.

\kappa\in St_{0}
is intended to designate that
\kappa
is an uncountable cardinal,
or
\kappa
is either a recursively regular ordinal or their limit.
We have

St_{i}=SSt_{i}\cup LSt_{i}
with
SSt_{i}\cap LSt_{i}=\emptyset
, and

St_{i+1}\subset LSt_{i}
.
If
\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}
, then the ordinal term

\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)
in Definition
3.31
.
5
denotes
the ordinal
\psi_{i,\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)
in (
7
) of Definition
3.8
.
3
.

\alpha=_{NF}\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0}
means that
\alpha=\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0}

with
\alpha_{m}\geq\cdots\geq\alpha_{0}

and each
\alpha_{i}
is a non-zero additive principal number.

\alpha=_{NF}\varphi\beta\gamma
means that
\alpha=\varphi\beta\gamma
and
\beta,\gamma<\alpha
.

Sets
SC(\alpha)
of strongly critical numbers are slightly modified as
SC(\Omega)=SC(\mathbb{I}_{N})=\emptyset
. Specifically

SC(0)=\emptyset
,
SC(\alpha)=\bigcup_{i\leq m}SC(\alpha_{i})
for
\alpha=_{NF}\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0}
,
and

SC(a)=SC(b)\cup SC(c)
for

.

SC(\Omega)=SC(\mathbb{I}_{N})=\emptyset
.

SC(a)=\{a\}
if
a\not\in\{\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
is strongly critical.

For
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
, let
m(\alpha)=f
.

\mathrm{fld}(f)=\bigcup\{\{c,f(c)\}:c\in{\rm supp}(f)\}
.
Immediate subterms of terms are defined as follows.

k(\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0})=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{m}\}
,

k(\varphi\alpha\beta)=\{\alpha,\beta\}
,

k(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=\{\mathbb{I}_{N},a\}
, and

k(\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(\alpha))=\{\sigma,\alpha\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)
.

Note that in the following Definition
3.31
, e.g.,
there is no clause for constructing
\kappa=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}(a)
from
a

for
\mathbb{S}\not\in SSt
.

Sets

Definition 3.33

(Definitions of
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
and
K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])

iff
\alpha<\rho
.
Moreover

or
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\rho\in SSt
.

Also

is defined
recursively as follows.

commutes with
\psi
,

\varphi
,

.

,

.

2.

and

.

For
\vec{i}\leq i+1
,

(\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}})[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:=(\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}])\in SSt_{i+1}^{M}
, cf. Definition
3.31
.
8
.
Here

\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\neq\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}}
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\rho)
if

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\in\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho],\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\}
.

3.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)
. Then

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(a[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\{\rho,a[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\})\cup\{a[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\}
.

4.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)
. Then

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\psi_{\kappa[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}^{f[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}(a[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
, where
(f[\rho/\mathbb{S}]):\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])
,

{\rm supp}(f[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=({\rm supp}(f))[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\{c[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:c\in{\rm supp}(f)\}
and

(f[\rho/\mathbb{S}])(c[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=(f(c))[\rho/\mathbb{S}]

for
f:\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\to\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])
and
c\in{\rm supp}(f)
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\{\kappa[\rho/\mathbb{S}],a[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\}\cup SC(f[\rho/\mathbb{S}]))\cup\{a[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\}
.

M_{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}=C_{b[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
for

b=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\alpha)
and
b[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

5.

Let
\alpha=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]\neq\mathbb{I}_{N}
. Then

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}]]
, where

\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]\in M_{\rho}
iff
\tau\in M_{\rho}
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

6.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]}(a)
for
\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]\neq\mathbb{I}_{N}
. Then

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}]]}(a[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\{\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}],a[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\})\cup\{a[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\}
.

7.

Let
\alpha=\tau^{\dagger\vec{j}}
with
\mathbb{S}<\tau\in LSt^{M}
. Then

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=(\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}])^{\dagger\vec{j}}
,
where

\tau^{\dagger\vec{j}}\in M_{\rho}

iff
\tau\in M_{\rho}
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

8.

Let
\alpha=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{j}}[\tau/\mathbb{T}]
, where
\tau\prec\mathbb{T}\in SSt^{M}
.
Then
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{j}}_{1}[\tau_{1}/\mathbb{T}_{1}]
, where

\tau_{1}=\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\prec\mathbb{T}_{1}=\mathbb{T}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\in SSt^{M}
and

\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{j}}_{1}=(\mathbb{T}_{1})^{\dagger\vec{j}}
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\tau[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

9.

Let
\alpha=\varphi\beta\gamma
. Then

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\varphi(\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}])(\gamma[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=K_{X}(\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}],\gamma[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

10.

For
\alpha=\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0}\,(m>0)
,

\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=(\alpha_{m}[\rho/\mathbb{S}])+\cdots+(\alpha_{0}[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

K_{X}(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=\bigcup\{K_{X}(\alpha_{i}[\rho/\mathbb{S}])):i\leq m\}
.

A relation
\alpha<\beta
for
\alpha,\beta\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is defined according to
Lemmas
3.16
and
3.19
,
Propositions
3.17
,
3.20
, and

3.27
,
and
Corollary
3.23
, provided that

\alpha\in C_{\gamma}(\delta)
is replaced by
K_{\delta}(\alpha)<\gamma
.
The relation enjoys

\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)<\kappa
according to Lemma
3.19
and Corollary
3.23
.
Moreover we obtain

\mathbb{S}^{\dagger i}<\psi_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger(i+1)}}^{g_{0}}(b_{0})<\mathbb{S}^{\dagger(i+1)}
for
i+1\leq N
,
and

LSt_{N}\ni\tau_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(c_{0})<\psi_{\tau_{0}^{\dagger}}^{h_{0}}(d_{0})<\tau_{0}^{\dagger}<\mathbb{I}_{N}

by Proposition
3.20
and Lemma
3.16
.
Hence if
\mathbb{S}<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(c_{0})
, then

\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{S}^{\dagger i}<\psi_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger(i+1)}}^{g_{0}}(b_{0})<\mathbb{S}^{\dagger(i+1)}<\tau_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(c_{0})<\psi_{\tau_{0}^{\dagger}}^{h_{0}}(d_{0})<\tau_{0}^{\dagger}<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
The Mostowski collapsing
\cdot[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
maps these inequalities isomorphically to

\rho<\mathbb{S}^{\dagger i}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]<\psi_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger(i+1)}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}^{g}(b)<\mathbb{S}^{\dagger(i+1)}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]<\tau=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(c)<\psi_{\tau^{\dagger}}^{h}(d)<\tau^{\dagger}<\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]<\rho^{\dagger 0}
, where

b=b_{0}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
, etc.

Definition 3.34

For terms
\pi,\kappa\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
a relation
\pi\prec^{R}\kappa
is defined recursively as follows.

1.

Let
\pi\prec\kappa\preceq\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i+1}^{M}
, and

\vec{i}\leq i+1
.
Then
each of

\pi\prec^{R}\kappa
,

\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\pi/\mathbb{S}]\prec^{R}\kappa
and
\mathbb{I}_{N}[\pi]\prec^{R}\kappa

holds.
Moreover
\pi^{\dagger\vec{i}}\prec^{R}\kappa
holds provided that

\pi^{\dagger\vec{i}}\not\in SSt
.

2.

\tau\prec^{R}\pi\prec^{R}\kappa\Rightarrow\tau\prec^{R}\kappa
.

Let
\pi\preceq^{R}\kappa:\Leftrightarrow\pi\prec^{R}\kappa\lor\pi=\kappa
.
For
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, let

L(\mathbb{S}):=\{\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}):\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}\}.

Note that
L(\mathbb{S})\cap SSt=\emptyset
, and

SSt\ni\rho^{\vec{i}}\not\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
for
LSt_{i}\ni\rho\prec\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i+1}

and
\vec{i}\leq i
.
For each strongly critical number
\Omega<\alpha\not\in\{\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup St
,
there exists a unique
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
such that
\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
.
If
\beta\prec^{R}\mathbb{T}
and
\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
with
\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}
, then

\beta<\alpha
.
In other words,
L(\mathbb{T})<L(\mathbb{S})
for layers
L(\mathbb{S})
.
Moreover if
\eta\not\in\bigcup_{\mathbb{S}\in SSt}L(\mathbb{S})
and
\eta\in\Psi
,
then either
\eta\prec\Omega
or
\eta\prec\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

Definition 3.35

Let
\beta,\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
be strongly critical numbers.

\beta<\alpha
iff one of the following cases holds:

1.

\beta=\psi_{\Omega}(b)
,
\alpha=\psi_{\Omega}(a)
and

b<a
.

2.

\beta=\psi_{\pi}(b)
,
\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}(a)
,

\pi=\kappa\in\{\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup\{\sigma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}):\exists\rho(\sigma=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])\}
, and

b<a
.

3.

\beta=\Omega
and
\Omega\neq\alpha\neq\psi_{\Omega}(a)
.

4.

\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}<\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{j}}
iff
(\mathbb{S})*\vec{i}<_{lx}(\mathbb{T})*\vec{j}

for
\mathbb{S},\mathbb{T}\in\{\Omega\}\cup(LSt\cap\Psi)
, where

\vec{i}=(i_{0},i_{1},\ldots,i_{n})<_{lx}(j_{0},j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})=\vec{j}
iff
either
\exists k\leq\min\{n,m\}(\forall p<k(i_{p}=j_{p})\,\&\,i_{k}<j_{k})
or
n<m\,\&\,\forall p\leq n(i_{p}=j_{p})
.

5.

(a)

There is an
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
such that

\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}>\beta\in LSt_{N}
.

(b)

There is a
\mathbb{T}\in SSt
such that

\beta\prec^{R}\mathbb{T}<\alpha
.

6.

There are
\mathbb{T},\mathbb{S}\in SSt
such that

\beta\prec^{R}\mathbb{T}
and
\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
with
\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}
.

7.

There is an
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
such that

\beta,\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
and one of the following holds:

(a)

\beta=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(b)
,
\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}^{g}(a)
, and there is a

\rho\preceq^{R}\mathbb{S}
such that

\kappa,\pi\preceq\rho
and
one of the following holds:

i.

\pi\leq\alpha
.

ii.

b<a
,
\beta<\kappa
, and

K_{\alpha}(\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,b\})<a

iii.

b>a
and
b\leq K_{\beta}(\mathrm{fld}(g)\cup\{\kappa,a\})
.

iv.

b=a
,
\kappa<\pi
, and
b\leq K_{\beta}(\kappa)
.

v.

b=a
,
\pi=\kappa
,
K_{\alpha}(\mathrm{fld}(f))<a
, and

f<^{0}_{lx}g
.

vi.

b=a
,
\pi=\kappa
, and

b\leq K_{\beta}(\mathrm{fld}(g))
.

(b)

There are
\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
,

c,d
and
\vec{i},\vec{j}
such that
\beta\preceq^{R}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(d))^{\dagger\vec{i}}
,

\alpha\preceq^{R}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(c))^{\dagger\vec{j}}

and
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(d)<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(c)
.

(c)

There are
\vec{i}
,
\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]
such that

\rho\prec\mathbb{T}\preceq^{R}\mathbb{S}
,

\beta\preceq^{R}\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{T}]
and
\alpha\preceq^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]
.

(d)

There are
\rho\prec\mathbb{T}\preceq^{R}\mathbb{S}
,

\sigma,\tau\prec\mathbb{U}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{T}]
,
\vec{k}
and
\vec{l}
such that

\tau<\sigma
,
(\tau,\sigma)\neq(\beta,\alpha)
,

\alpha=\sigma\lor\alpha\preceq^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\sigma]\lor\alpha\preceq^{R}\mathbb{U}^{\dagger\vec{k}}[\sigma/\mathbb{U}]
,
and

\beta=\tau\lor\beta\preceq^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]\lor\beta\preceq^{R}\mathbb{U}^{\dagger\vec{l}}[\tau/\mathbb{U}]
.

Lemma 3.36

(OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}),<)
is a computable linear order.
Specifically
each of
\alpha<\beta
and
\alpha=\beta
is decidable for
\alpha,\beta\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
and
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is decidable for terms
\alpha

over symbols
\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N},+,\varphi,\psi\}
,
\{{*}^{\dagger i}:0<i\leq N\}
,

\mathbb{I}_{N}[*]
and
*_{0}[*_{1}/*_{2}]
.

In particular the order type of the initial segment
\{\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}):\alpha<\Omega\}

is less than
\omega_{1}^{CK}
if it is well-founded.

In what follows by ordinals we mean ordinal terms in
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

\ell\alpha
denotes the length of ordinal terms
\alpha
, which means the number of
occurrences of symbols in
\alpha
.

Proposition 3.37

If
\mathbb{S}\in St_{i+1}=SSt_{i+1}\cup LSt_{i+1}
and
\alpha<\mathbb{S}
,
then
\alpha^{\dagger i}<\mathbb{S}
.

Proof
. This is seen from Proposition
3.32
and Definition
3.35
.

\Box

Proposition 3.38

\{\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(m(\rho))\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset M_{\rho}
for
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
.

\mathbb{S}\in SSt

,
a=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
,

\{\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset C_{a}(\alpha)=M_{\rho}
by

3.31
.
5
.
Otherwise
\{\mathbb{S}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(m(\rho))\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset M_{\rho}

(
12
)
in Definition
3.31
.
6
.

\Box

An ordinal term
\sigma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is said to be
regular
if
either
\sigma\in\{\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup\{\sigma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}):\exists\rho(\sigma=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])\}
or

\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(a)
is in
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
for some
f
and
a
.

Reg

denotes the set of regular terms.
Then

Reg=SSt^{M}\cup\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]:\exists\mathbb{S}\in SSt^{M}(\rho\prec\mathbb{S})\}\cup\{\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
.
We see that for each
\alpha\in\Psi
, there exists a
\kappa\in Reg_{0}:=(Reg\setminus\Psi)
such that

\alpha\prec\kappa
.
Such a
\kappa
is either in
\{\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
or
one of the form
\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]
,
\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}}

or
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
with a non-empty
\vec{i}
.

Lemma 3.41

For
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
,

\{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\alpha\in M_{\rho}\}
is a transitive collapse of
M_{\rho}
in the following sense.
Let
\{\alpha,\beta,\gamma\}\subset M_{\rho}
.

1.

\beta<\alpha\Leftrightarrow\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]<\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.

2.

\beta\prec^{R}\alpha\Leftrightarrow\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\prec^{R}\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.

3.

\mathbb{S}<\gamma\Rightarrow\left(K_{\gamma}(\beta)<\alpha\Leftrightarrow K_{\gamma[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}(\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}])<\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\right)
.

4.

OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\{\gamma[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\gamma\in M_{\rho}\cap\alpha\}
.

Proof
. We show Lemmas
3.41
.
1
-

3.41
.
3

simultaneously
by induction on the sum
2^{\ell\alpha}+2^{\ell\beta}
for

\alpha,\beta\in M_{\rho}
.
We see easily that

\mathbb{S}>\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])>\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]>\rho
when

\alpha>\mathbb{S}
.
Also
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\leq\alpha
.

.

and
3.41
.

.

.

3.5

Lemma 3.42

1.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\Omega}(a)
with
a\in C_{a}(\alpha)
.
Then
C_{a}(\alpha)\cap\Omega\subset\alpha
.

2.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)
with
a\in C_{a}(\alpha)
.
Then
C_{a}(\alpha)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset\alpha
.

3.

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, and

\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)<\kappa
with

\kappa\preceq\mathbb{S}
and

\{\kappa,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(\alpha)
.
Then
C_{a}(\alpha)\cap\kappa\subset\alpha
.

Proof
. We see
\beta\in C_{a}(\alpha)\cap\Omega\Rightarrow\beta<\alpha=\psi_{\Omega}(a)

by induction on the lengths
\ell\beta
of
\beta
.
Lemmas

3.42
.
2
and
3.42
.
3

are seen similarly using the fact

\rho<\alpha\Rightarrow\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]<\alpha
for
\alpha\in\{\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a),\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\}
.

\Box

Proposition 3.43

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, and

\rho=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)<\kappa
with

\kappa\preceq\mathbb{S}
and

C_{\gamma}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\kappa
for
\gamma\leq a
.
Then
C_{\gamma}(\rho)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\rho
.

Proof
. If
\kappa=\mathbb{S}
, then

C_{\gamma}(\rho)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset C_{a}(\rho)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\rho

by
\gamma\leq a
and Lemma
3.42
.
3
.
Let
\kappa=\psi_{\pi}^{g}(b)<\mathbb{S}
.
We have
\kappa\in C_{a}(\rho)
by (
7
),
and hence
b<a
by
\mathbb{S}>\kappa>\rho
.
We obtain

C_{\gamma}(\rho)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset C_{\gamma}(\kappa)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\kappa
.

\gamma\leq a
with Lemma
3.42
.
3

yields

C_{\gamma}(\rho)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset C_{\gamma}(\rho)\cap\kappa\subset C_{a}(\rho)\cap\kappa\subset\rho
.

Definition 3.44

(Mostowski uncollapsing)

Let
\alpha
be an ordinal term and
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
with

\mathbb{S}\in SSt
.
If there exists a
\beta\in M_{\rho}
such that

\alpha=\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
, then
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}:=\beta
.
Otherwise
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}:=\alpha
.
Let

X[\rho/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}:=\{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}:\alpha\in X\}
for a set
X
of ordinal terms.

We see that
ordinal terms
\rho
and
\beta\in M_{\rho}

with
\rho\leq\alpha=\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]<\Gamma(\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])

are uniquely determined from
\alpha
, when
such
\beta
and
\rho
exist.

Following Buchholz
[
9
]
, let us introduce operators
{\cal H}_{\gamma}
on ordinals for ordinal analysis in sections
4
and
5
.
For a finite set
\Theta
of ordinals, let
C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
denote the Skolem hull of
\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup\Theta

under the functions
+,\varphi
,
\alpha\mapsto\alpha^{\dagger i}
and

\beta\mapsto\psi_{\pi}^{f}(\beta)\,(\beta<\alpha)
as in Definition
3.8
.
1
.

The set
C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
is closed under these functions in the sense that, e.g.,
if
\{\beta,\gamma\}\subset C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
, then
\beta+\gamma\in C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
.
However the set
C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
is not closed under these functions in a reverse direction.
This means, e.g.,
\{\beta,\gamma\}\not\subset C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
when
\beta+\gamma\in C_{\alpha}(\Theta)
.
For example
\Omega+\psi_{\Omega}(1)\in C_{0}(\{\Omega+\psi_{\Omega}(1)\})
, but

\psi_{\Omega}(1)\not\in C_{0}(\{\Omega+\psi_{\Omega}(1)\})
.
It is convenient for us to have sets of ordinals closed under some functions in both directions.
This motivates the sets
{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
in the following Definition
3.45
.

We need sets of ordinals closed under
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}\mapsto\mathbb{S}
in subsection
5.2
.
The set
{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
is better than
C_{\gamma}(\Theta)

unless we assume that the set
\Theta
is closed under
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}\mapsto\mathbb{S}
.

Definition 3.45

1.

We say that a set
\Theta
of ordinals is
C
-bounded
if there are ordinals
\alpha
and
\beta
such that
\Theta\subset C_{\alpha}(\beta)
.

2.

For
C
-bounded sets
\Theta
of ordinals, let

{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]:=\bigcap\{C_{\alpha}(\beta):\gamma<\alpha,\,\Theta\subset C_{\alpha}(\beta)\}

where

\gamma\in C_{\alpha}(\beta)\Leftrightarrow K_{\beta}(\gamma)<\alpha

for
\{\gamma,\alpha,\beta\}\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

Each finite set
\Theta
is
C
-bounded since
\Theta\subset C_{\alpha}(\beta)
, where

\beta=\max(\Theta)+1
if
\Theta\neq\emptyset
, and
\beta=0
else.
Also each ordinal
\beta=\{\gamma:\gamma<\beta\}\subset C_{\alpha}(\beta)
as well as
a set
C_{\gamma}(\delta)
is
C
-bounded.

, then
\rho\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.

Proof
. This seen from Proposition
3.39
.

Definition 3.47

The set
stm(\alpha)
of immediate subterms of
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is defined as follows.

stm(\alpha)=\emptyset
if
\alpha\in\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
.

stm(\alpha)=\{\alpha_{i}:i\leq m\}
if
\alpha=_{NF}\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0}
.

stm(\alpha)=\{\beta,\gamma\}
if
\alpha=_{NF}\varphi\beta\gamma
.

stm(\alpha)=\{\sigma,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)
if
\alpha=\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(a)
.

stm(\alpha)=\{\mathbb{S}\}
if
\alpha=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}
.

stm(\alpha)=\{\tau\}
if
\alpha=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]
.

stm(\alpha)=\{\tau\}
if
\alpha=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\tau/\mathbb{T}]
.

Proposition 3.48

1.

If
\beta\leq\alpha\in C_{b}(\beta)
, then
stm(\alpha)\subset C_{b}(\beta)
.

2.

Let
\alpha\not\in\Psi
. Then

, and

.

4.

.

, then
K_{\delta}(\alpha)\subset C_{b}(\beta)
holds.

Proof
.

.

.

.

. By induction on

Lemma 3.51

Let
\sigma\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
and
\alpha\in M_{\sigma}
.
Assume
\sigma\in C_{b}(\delta)
for
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\sigma)\leq b
and
\delta\leq\sigma
.
Then

\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\in C_{b}(\delta)\Leftrightarrow\alpha\in C_{b}(\delta)
.

In particular, if
\tau\in\Psi_{\mathbb{T}}
with
\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}
and

\sigma\in M_{\tau}
, then

\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\in M_{\tau}\Leftrightarrow\alpha\in M_{\tau}
holds.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\alpha
.
We may assume that
\alpha\geq\mathbb{S}
and
\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\in\Psi
by Proposition
3.48
.
2
.

We obtain
\beta\in M_{\sigma}
for each
\beta\in\{\kappa,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)
.
IH yields
\beta[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\in C_{b}(\delta)\Leftrightarrow\beta\in C_{b}(\delta)
.
Moreover if
\delta\leq\sigma\leq\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\in C_{b}(\delta)
, then
\forall\beta\in\{\kappa,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)(\beta[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\in C_{b}(\delta))
,
and if
\alpha\in C_{b}(\delta)
, then
\{\kappa,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{b}(\delta)
.

Let
M_{\sigma}=C_{d}(\sigma)
with
d=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\sigma)\leq b
.
We have
a<d
by
\sigma<\mathbb{S}\leq\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\in C_{d}(\sigma)
.
We obtain
a[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\leq a<b
.
Hence
\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]\in C_{b}(\delta)\Leftrightarrow\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\in C_{b}(\delta)
.

Let
M_{\tau}=C_{b}(\tau)
with
b=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\tau)
.
We see
d<b
from
\sigma\in C_{b}(\tau)
and
\tau<\mathbb{T}<\sigma
.

Definition 3.52

For
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, a finite set

of subterms of
\alpha
is defined recursively as follows.

1.

E_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=E_{\mathbb{S}}(SC(\alpha)):=\bigcup\{E_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta):\beta\in SC(\alpha)\}

if
\alpha\not\in SC(\alpha)
.

2.

E_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=\bigcup\{E_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta):\beta\in stm(\alpha)\}

if
\mathbb{S}<\alpha\in SC(\alpha)
.

3.

E_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathbb{S})=\emptyset
.

4.

E_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=\{\alpha\}
if
\mathbb{S}>\alpha\in SC(\alpha)
.

Proposition 3.53

\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}

Proposition 3.54

Lemma 3.55

Let
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
with
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
and
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, and
\max\{\mathbb{S},\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\leq\gamma
.

.

.

.

by induction on
\ell\alpha
.
We may assume that

by IH and Proposition
3.48
.
2
.

, then

.

3.38
we obtain

with
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\leq\gamma<a
.

, then we obtain
\mathbb{S}\in C_{a}(\beta)
.

. Then

yields

Next let

. We have

by IH.

.

and

.

, and

.
Therefore

follows from Proposition
3.54
.

Proposition 3.56

Let
\alpha
be a strongly critical number such that
\Omega<\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
There exists a unique sequence

(\alpha_{n})_{n\leq m}
such that
\alpha_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)
for an
a
,
\alpha_{m}=\alpha
and

\alpha_{n+1}

\psi_{\alpha_{n}}^{f}(b)
,

\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\alpha_{n}/\mathbb{S}]

and
\mathbb{S}
.
The sequence
(\alpha_{n})_{n\leq m}
is said to be the

to
\alpha
, and denoted by
