.

1.

For a term
\alpha_{n}
in the trail to
\alpha
, assume
\alpha_{n}<\alpha
.
Then

\alpha_{n}<\alpha_{k}
for
n<k\leq m
,

\alpha\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\Rightarrow\alpha_{n}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
, and
E_{\mathbb{T}}(\alpha_{n})\subset E_{\mathbb{T}}(\alpha)
for every
SSt\ni\mathbb{T}\leq\alpha_{n}
.

2.

\alpha_{0}\leq\alpha
,
\alpha\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\Rightarrow\alpha_{0}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
,
and
E_{\mathbb{T}}(\alpha_{0})\subset E_{\mathbb{T}}(\alpha)
holds for every

SSt\ni\mathbb{T}\leq\alpha_{0}
.

Proof
. This is seen by inspection of Definitions
3.31
and
3.33
.
If
\alpha_{n}>\alpha_{n+1}
, then we would have
\alpha_{n+1}\prec\alpha_{n}
and
\alpha<\alpha_{n}
by
Definition
3.35
.

In Proposition
3.56
.
1
,
\alpha\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\Rightarrow\alpha_{n}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
follows from
Proposition
3.49
.
6
.

\Box

Proposition 3.57

Let
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
with a successor stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
.
Assume
k_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=\max(\{0\}\cup K_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha))<\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
and

E_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)\subset\rho
.
Then
\alpha\in M_{\rho}=C_{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)}(\rho)
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\alpha
. Note that

k_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)<\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
iff
\alpha\in C_{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)}(\mathbb{S})
.
We have
\mathbb{S}\in M_{\rho}
by Proposition
3.38
.
By IH we may assume that
\mathbb{S}<\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}
and
\alpha\in\Psi
by Proposition
3.48
.
2
.
Let
\alpha=\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(a)
. For
stm(\alpha)=\{\sigma,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)
, we obtain
E_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=E_{\mathbb{S}}(stm(\alpha))
and

stm(\alpha)\subset C_{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)}(\mathbb{S})
with
a<\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
.
IH yields
stm(\alpha)\subset M_{\rho}
, and
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
by
a<\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
.

\Box

The axiom (
2
) states that each
(i+1)
-stable ordinal is a limit of
i
-stable ordinals.
In proving the axiom (
2
)
in Lemma
4.15

we need the fact that if both of an
(i+1)
-stable ordinal
\mathbb{T}
and an ordinal
\alpha<\mathbb{T}
are ‘captured’
in
{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
, then so is a successor
i
-stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
such that
\alpha<\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}
.
Or in other words, such an
\mathbb{S}
should be constructed from data included in ordinals
\mathbb{T}
and
\alpha
.
The data we need are trails.

Proposition 3.58

Let
\mathbb{T}\in SSt_{i+1}
be a successor
(i+1)
-stable ordinal, and
\alpha<\mathbb{T}
an ordinal.
Assume
\{\alpha,\mathbb{T}\}\subset{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.
Then there exists a successor
i
-stable ordinal
\alpha<\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}
such that

\mathbb{S}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.

Proof
. By induction on the lengths
\ell\alpha
of ordinal terms
\alpha
.
By IH we may assume that
\Omega<\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}
and
\alpha\in SC(\alpha)
.
Let
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{U}^{\dagger(i+1)}
with
\mathbb{U}\in St\cup\{\Omega\}
.

Case 1
. There exists a
k>0
such that
\alpha<\mathbb{U}^{\dagger i^{(k)}}
, where

\mathbb{U}^{\dagger i^{(0)}}=\mathbb{U}
and
\mathbb{U}^{\dagger i^{(k+1)}}=(\mathbb{U}^{\dagger i^{(k)}})^{\dagger i}
:
Pick a
k>0
such that
\alpha<\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{U}^{\dagger i^{(k)}}
.
We obtain
\mathbb{S}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
by
\mathbb{T}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
and Proposition
3.49
.
5
.

\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}
is seen from
\mathbb{T}\in LSt_{i}
.

Case 2
. Otherwise:
Then we see from Definition
3.35
that there exists a
\rho\leq\alpha
such that
\rho\prec\mathbb{T}
, and
\rho\in{\rm trail}(\alpha)
if
\rho<\alpha
.
From
\alpha\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
and Proposition
3.56
.
1
we see
\rho\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.
Pick a
k>0
such that
\alpha<\mathbb{S}=\rho^{\dagger i^{(k)}}<\mathbb{T}
.
We obtain
\mathbb{S}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
by Proposition
3.49
.
5
.

\Box

The following Definition
3.59
is needed in subsection
5.2
.

Definition 3.59

Let

s(f)=\max(\{0\}\cup{\rm supp}(f))
for finite function
f
, and

s(\rho)=s(m(\rho))
.

Let
\Lambda<\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a strongly critical number, which is a base for
\tilde{\theta}
-function.
Let
f:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
be a non-empty and irreducible finite function.
Then
f
is said to be a
special
finite function with base
\Lambda
if there exists an ordinal
\alpha

such that
f(s(f))=\Lambda(\alpha+1)
.
For a special finite function
f
,
f^{\prime}
denotes a finite function such that

{\rm supp}(f^{\prime})={\rm supp}(f)
,

f^{\prime}(c)=f(c)
for
c\neq s(f)
, and

f^{\prime}(s(f))=\Lambda\cdot\alpha
with
f(s(f))=\Lambda(\alpha+1)
.

A special function
h^{b}(g;a)
is defined from ordinals
a,b
and a finite function
g

as in
[
5
]
.

The following Definition
3.60
is mainly needed in subsection
5.2
, but
also in Definition
4.29
, cf. the beginning of subsection
5.2
.

Definition 3.60

Let
f,g:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
be special finite functions.

1.

For
ordinals
a\leq\Lambda
,
b\leq s(g)
,
let us define a special finite function
h=h^{b}(g;a)

as follows.

s(h)=b
, and

h_{b}=g_{b}
.
To define
h(b)
,
let
\{b=b_{0}<b_{1}<\cdots<b_{n}=s(g)\}=\{b,s(g)\}\cup\left((b,s(g))\cap{\rm supp}(g)\right)
.
Define recursively ordinals
\alpha_{i}
by

\alpha_{n}=\Lambda\cdot\alpha+a
with
g(s(g))=\Lambda(\alpha+1)
.

\alpha_{i}=g(b_{i})+\tilde{\theta}_{c_{i}}(\alpha_{i+1})
for

c_{i}=b_{i+1}-b_{i}
.
Finally let
h(b)=\alpha_{0}+\Lambda
.

Note that
h=h^{b}(g;a)=g
when
b=s(g)
.

2.

f\leq g:\Leftrightarrow\forall i(f^{\prime}(i)\leq g^{\prime}(i))
.

3.

f_{b}*g^{b}
denotes a special function
h
such that

{\rm supp}(h)={\rm supp}(f_{b})\cup{\rm supp}(g^{b})
,

h^{\prime}(c)=f^{\prime}(c)
for
c<b
, and

h^{\prime}(c)=g^{\prime}(c)
for
c\geq b
.

The following Proposition
3.61
is seen as in
[
5
]
.

Proposition 3.61

Let

f,g:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
be special finite functions such that

f_{d}=g_{d}
and

f<_{\Lambda}^{d}g^{\prime}(d)
for a
d\in{\rm supp}(g)
.

1.

For
b<d
and
a<\Lambda
,

f_{b}=(h^{b}(g;a))_{b}
and

f<_{\Lambda}^{b}(h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b)
.

2.

Let
b\leq e<d
,

a_{0}<a\leq\Lambda
, and

h=(h^{e}(g;a_{0}))*f^{e+1}
.
Then

h_{b}=(h^{b}(g;a))_{b}
,

f\leq h
, and

h<_{\Lambda}^{b}(h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b)
.

Proof
.
3.61
.
1
.
Let
h=h^{b}(g;a)
.
We have
h_{b}=g_{b}=f_{b}
.
Let
b+x\in{\rm supp}(f)\cap d\subset{\rm supp}(g)\cap d
.
Then
f(b+x)=g(b+x)<\tilde{\theta}_{-x}(h^{\prime}(b))
and

g^{\prime}(d)<\tilde{\theta}_{-(d-b)}(h^{\prime}(b))
.
Proposition
3.6
yields the proposition.

3.61
.
2
.
Note that
h=(h^{e}(g;a_{0}))^{\prime}*f^{e+1}
.
We have
h_{b}=g_{b}=(h^{b}(g;a))_{b}
.
For
b+x\in{\rm supp}(g)\cap e
,
h(b+x)=(h^{e}(g;a_{0}))(b+x)=g(b+x)<\tilde{\theta}_{-x}((h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b))
, and

h(e)=(h^{e}(g;a_{0}))(e)<\tilde{\theta}_{-(e-b)}((h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b))
by
a_{0}<a
.
For
e<e+x\in{\rm supp}(f)\cap d
, we obtain

h(e+x)=f(e+x)=g(e+x)<\tilde{\theta}_{-(e+x-b)}((h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b))
.
We obtain

h(d+x)=f(d+x)<\tilde{\theta}_{-x}(g^{\prime}(d))\leq\tilde{\theta}_{-(d+x-b)}((h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b))
for
d+x\in{\rm supp}(f)
.
Therefore

h<_{\Lambda}^{b}(h^{b}(g;a))^{\prime}(b)
.

Finally
f(e)=g(e)\leq h(e)
yields
f\leq h
.

\Box

4
Operator controlled derivations

We prove Theorem
1.1
assuming that the notation system
(OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}),<)
is a well ordering.
Operator controlled derivations are introduced by W. Buchholz
[
9
]
, which we follow.
In this section except otherwise stated,

\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\ldots,a,b,c,d,\ldots
and
\xi,\zeta,\nu,\mu,\ldots
range over ordinal terms in
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

f,g,h,\ldots
range over finite functions.

4.1
Classes of sentences

Following Buchholz
[
9
]
let us introduce a language of ramified set theory
RS
.

Definition 4.1

RS
-terms
and their
levels
are inductively defined.

1.

For each
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
,
{\sf L}_{\alpha}
is an
RS
-term of level
\alpha
.

2.

Let
\phi(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})
be a set-theoretic formula in the language
\{\in\}
, and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}

RS
-terms of levels
<\!\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

Then

[x\in{\sf L}_{\alpha}:\phi^{{\sf L}_{\alpha}}(x,a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})]

is an
RS
-term of level
\alpha
.

|u|
denotes the level of
RS
-terms
u
, and
Tm(\alpha)
the set of
RS
-terms of level
<\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)
.

Tm=Tm(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is then the set of
RS
-terms, which are denoted by
u,v,w,\ldots

Let us identify the individual constant
\mathsf{M}_{0}
in the language of
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}

with the
RS
-term
{\sf L}_{\Omega}
.

Definition 4.2

1.

Let
\rho_{i}\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}
be ordinals with successor stable ordinals
\mathbb{S}_{i}
for
i<n
.
For
\bar{\rho}=(\rho_{0},\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{n-1})
,

[\bar{\rho}]:=[\rho_{0}/\mathbb{S}_{0}][\rho_{1}/\mathbb{S}_{1}]\cdots[\rho_{n-1}/\mathbb{S}_{n-1}]
is said to be a
collapsing list
denoting
the list of Mostowski collapsings

[\rho_{i}/\mathbb{S}_{i}]
in Definition
3.33
.

[\bar{\rho}]
applies collapsings repeatedly to ordinals
\alpha
:

\alpha[\bar{\rho}]=(\cdots((\alpha[\rho_{0}/\mathbb{S}_{0}])[\rho_{1}/\mathbb{S}_{1}])\cdots)[\rho_{n-1}/\mathbb{S}_{n-1}]

for ordinals
\alpha

such that
\alpha\in M_{[\bar{\rho}]}:\Leftrightarrow\forall i<n((\cdots((\alpha[\rho_{0}/\mathbb{S}_{0}])[\rho_{1}/\mathbb{S}_{1}])\cdots)[\rho_{i-1}/\mathbb{S}_{i-1}]\in M_{\rho_{i}})
.

The empty list
\bar{\rho}
with
n=0
is denoted by
\emptyset
.
Let
M_{[\emptyset]}=OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and
\alpha[\emptyset]=\alpha
.

2.

For an ordinal
\tau\in\Psi_{\mathbb{T}}
let

[\bar{\rho}]*[\tau]:=[\rho_{0}/\mathbb{S}_{0}][\rho_{1}/\mathbb{S}_{1}]\cdots[\rho_{n-1}/\mathbb{S}_{n-1}][\tau/\mathbb{T}]
.

Definition 4.3

1.

RS
-
literals

are

u\in v,u\not\in v

and

st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u),\lnot st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u)

for
RS
-terms
u,v
,
0<i\leq N
and collapsing lists
[\bar{\rho}]
.
When
\bar{\rho}=\emptyset
, we write
st_{i}(u)
for
st_{i}^{[\emptyset]}(u)
.

RS
-
formulas

are constructed from
RS
-
literals

by propositional connectives
\lor,\land
, bounded quantifiers
\exists x\in u,\forall x\in u
and
unbounded quantifiers
\exists x,\forall x
.
Unbounded quantifiers
\exists x,\forall x
are denoted by
\exists x\in{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}},\forall x\in{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
, resp.

It is convenient for us not to restrict propositional connectives
\lor,\land
to binary ones.
Specifically when
A_{i}
are
RS
-formulas for
i<n<\omega
,

A_{0}\lor\cdots\lor A_{n-1}
and
A_{0}\land\cdots\land A_{n-1}
are
RS
-formulas.
Even when
n=1
,
A_{0}\lor\cdots\lor A_{0}
is understood to be different from the formula
A_{0}
.
For
\Gamma=\{A_{i}:i<n\}
we write

\bigvee\Gamma\equiv(A_{0}\lor\cdots\lor A_{n-1})
and

\bigwedge\Gamma\equiv(A_{0}\land\cdots\land A_{n-1})
.

2.

For
RS
-terms and
RS
-formulas
\iota
,

{\sf k}(\iota)
denotes the set of ordinal terms
\alpha
such that the constant
{\sf L}_{\alpha}
occurs in
\iota
.
Specifically
\mathsf{k}(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))=\mathsf{k}(u)
.

Let
|\iota|=\max({\sf k}(\iota)\cup\{0\})
and
{\sf k}(n)=\emptyset
and
|n|=0
for natural numbers
n
.

3.

\mathcal{L}_{i}=\{\in\}\cup\{st_{j}:0<j<i\}
, where
st_{j}\equiv st_{j}^{[\emptyset]}
.

4.

\Delta_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formulas,
\Sigma_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formulas and
\Sigma(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formulas are defined as in
[
6
]
.
Specifically if
\psi
is a
\Sigma(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formula, then so is the formula
\forall y\in z\,\psi
.

\theta^{(u)}
denotes a
\Delta_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formula obtained from a
\Sigma(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formula
\theta

by restricting each unbounded existential quantifier to
u
.

5.

For a
\Sigma_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})
-formula
\psi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})

and

u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}\in Tm(\kappa)
with
\kappa\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
,

\psi^{({\sf L}_{\kappa})}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})

is a
\Sigma_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i}:\kappa)
-formula
.

\Delta_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{i}:\kappa)
-formulas and
\Sigma(\mathcal{L}_{i}:\kappa)
-formulas are defined similarly

6.

For
\theta\equiv\psi^{({\sf L}_{\kappa})}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})\in\Sigma(\mathcal{L}_{i}:\kappa)

and
\lambda<\kappa
,
with
u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}\in Tm(\lambda)
,

\theta^{(\lambda,\kappa)}:\equiv\psi^{({\sf L}_{\lambda})}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})
.

In what follows we consider only
sentences
without free variables.
Sentences are denoted
A,C
possibly with indices.

For each sentence
A
, either a disjunction is assigned as
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
, or
a conjunction is assigned as
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
.
In the former case
A
is said to be a
\bigvee
-formula
, and in the latter

A
is a
\bigwedge
-formula
.
By
st_{i}(u)
we understand that
there is a
successor

i
-stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
such that
\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}=u
.
It is convenient for us, cf. Recapping
5.6
, to modify
the assignment of disjunctions and conjunctions to sentences from
[
9
]

such that
if
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
is a
\bigvee
-formula, then each

A_{\iota}
is a
\bigwedge
-formula, and similarly for
\bigwedge
-formulas
A
.

Definition 4.4

If
A
is a
\bigvee
-formula, then let
A^{\lor}:\equiv A
.
Otherwise let

A^{\lor}:\equiv\left(\bigvee_{i\leq 0}B_{i}\right)
with
B_{0}\equiv A
.
If
A
is a
\bigwedge
-formula, then let
A^{\land}:\equiv A
.
Otherwise let

A^{\land}:\equiv\left(\bigwedge_{i\leq 0}B_{i}\right)
with
B_{0}\equiv A
.

Definition 4.5

1.

For
v,u\in Tm(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with
|v|<|u|
, let

(v\dot{\in}u):\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A(v)&\mbox{{\rm if }}u\equiv[x\in{\sf L}_{\alpha}:A(x)]\\
v\not\in{\sf L}_{0}&\mbox{{\rm if }}u\equiv{\sf L}_{\alpha}\end{array}\right.

and

(u=v):\equiv(\forall x\in u(x\in v)\land\forall x\in v(x\in u))
.

2.

When
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
,
let
\lnot A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
.

3.

For
RS
-terms
v,u
,
let

(v\in u)\simeq\bigvee(A_{w,0}\land A_{w,1}\land A_{w,2})_{w\in J}
,
and

(v\not\in u)\simeq\bigwedge(\lnot A_{w,0}\lor\lnot A_{w,1}\lor\lnot A_{w,2})_{w\in J}
,
where
J=Tm(|u|)
,

A_{w,0}\equiv(w\dot{\in}u)^{\lor}
,
A_{w,1}\equiv(\forall x\in w(x\in v))^{\lor}
and

A_{w,2}\equiv(\forall x\in v(x\in w))^{\lor}
.

4.

(A_{0}\lor\cdots\lor A_{n-1})\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota}^{\land})_{\iota\in J}

and

(A_{0}\land\cdots\land A_{n-1})\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota}^{\lor})_{\iota\in J}
for

J:=n
.

5.

For
u\in Tm(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cup\{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\}
,

\exists x\in u\,A(x)\simeq\bigvee(A_{v})_{v\in J}

for

A_{v}:\equiv((v\dot{\in}u)^{\lor}\land(A(v))^{\lor})
and

J=Tm(|u|)
, where

Tm(|{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}|)=Tm(\mathbb{I}_{N})=Tm
and

(v\dot{\in}{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}):\equiv(v\not\in{\sf L}_{0})
.

6.

For a collapsing list
[\bar{\rho}]
, let

st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u)\simeq\bigvee({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}=u)_{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}\in J_{i}}

with

J_{i}=\{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}:\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}\cap M_{[\bar{\rho}]},\,|u|\geq\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]\}
, where

st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}
denotes a predicate symbol,
while

SSt_{i}\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
in the definition of
J_{i}
.

7.

For
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
let

[\rho]J=\{\iota\in J:{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}\}
.

It is clear that

{\sf k}(A_{\iota})\subset{\cal H}_{0}[{\sf k}(A)\cup{\sf k}(\iota)]
.

The rank
\mathrm{rk}(\iota)
of
sentences or terms
\iota

is defined as in
[
9
]
so that the following Proposition
4.7
holds.
For completeness let us reproduce it.

Definition 4.6

\mathrm{rk}(\lnot A):=\mathrm{rk}(A)
.

\mathrm{rk}(\mathsf{L}_{\alpha})=\omega\alpha
.

\mathrm{rk}([x\in\mathsf{L}_{\alpha}:A(x)])=\max\{\omega\alpha+1,\mathrm{rk}(A(\mathsf{L}_{0}))+2\}
.

\mathrm{rk}(v\in u)=\max\{\mathrm{rk}(v)+6,\mathrm{rk}(u)+1\}
.

\mathrm{rk}(A_{0}\lor\cdots\lor A_{n-1})=\max(\{0\}\cup\{\mathrm{rk}(A_{i})+1:i<n\})
.

\mathrm{rk}(\exists x\in u\,A(x))=\max\{\mathrm{rk}(u),\mathrm{rk}(A(\mathsf{L}_{0}))+2\}
for
u\in Tm(\mathbb{K})\cup\{\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{K}}\}
.
And

\mbox{{\rm rk}}(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))=\mbox{{\rm rk}}(u)+5
.

For finite sets
\Delta
of sentences, let

\mathrm{rk}(\Delta)=\max(\{0\}\cup\{\mathrm{rk}(\delta):\delta\in\Delta\})
.

We see the following Proposition
4.7
as in
[
9
]
.

Proposition 4.7

Let
A
be a sentence with

A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
or
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
.

1.

\mbox{{\rm rk}}(A)<\mathbb{I}_{N}+\omega
.

2.

\omega|u|\leq\mbox{{\rm rk}}(u)\in\{\omega|u|+i:i\in\omega\}
, and

\omega|A|\leq\mbox{{\rm rk}}(A)\in\{\omega|A|+i:i\in\omega\}
.

3.

\mbox{{\rm rk}}(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))\in\{\mbox{{\rm rk}}(u)+i:i<\omega\}
.

4.

\forall\iota\in J(\mbox{{\rm rk}}(A_{\iota})<\mbox{{\rm rk}}(A))
.

5.

\forall\iota\in J(\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\subset\mathsf{k}(A)\cup\mathsf{k}(\iota))
.

6.

If
\mathrm{rk}(A)=\omega\cdot\alpha
, then either
A\equiv(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\alpha}B(x))
or

A\equiv(\forall x\in\mathsf{L}_{\alpha}B(x))
.

Definition 4.8

Let
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
for a successor stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
, and

{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}
for
RS
-terms and
RS
-formulas
\iota
.
Then

\iota^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
denotes the result of replacing each unbounded quantifier

Qx
by
Qx\in{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}
,
and each ordinal term
\alpha\in{\sf k}(\iota)

by
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
for the Mostowski collapse in
Definition
3.33
.

\iota^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
is defined recursively as follows.

1.

({\sf L}_{\alpha})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv{\sf L}_{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
with
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
.
When
\{\alpha\}\cup\bigcup\{{\sf k}(u_{i}):i\leq n\}\subset M_{\rho}
,

\left([x\in{\sf L}_{\alpha}:\phi^{{\sf L}_{\alpha}}(x,u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})]\right)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}

is defined to be the
RS
-term

[x\in{\sf L}_{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}:\phi^{L_{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]]}}(x,(u_{1})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]},\ldots,(u_{n})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})]
.

2.

(\lnot A)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\lnot A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
.

(u\in v)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\left(u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\in v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\right)
.

(A_{0}\lor\cdots\lor A_{n-1})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\left((A_{0})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\lor\cdots\lor(A_{n-1})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\right)
.

(\exists x\in u\,A)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv(\exists x\in u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})
.

(\exists xA)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv(\exists x\in{\sf L}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})
.

3.

(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]*[\rho]}(u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})&\mbox{if }|u|\geq\mathbb{S}\\
st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u)&\mbox{if }|u|<\mathbb{S}\end{array}\right.

The following Propositions
4.9
,
4.10

and
4.11
are seen from Lemma
3.41
.

Proposition 4.9

Let
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
.

1.

Let
v
be an
RS
-term with
{\sf k}(v)\subset M_{\rho}
,
and
\alpha=|v|
.
Then

v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
is an
RS
-term of level
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
,

\left|v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\right|=\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
and

{\sf k}(v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})=\left({\sf k}(v)\right)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
.

2.

Let
\alpha\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
be such that
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
. Then

\left(Tm(\alpha)\right)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}:=\{v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}:v\in Tm(\alpha),{\sf k}(v)\subset M_{\rho}\}=Tm(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
.

3.

Let
A
be an
RS
-formula with
{\sf k}(A)\subset M_{\rho}
.
Then
A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
is an
RS
-formula such that

{\sf k}(A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset\{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\alpha\in{\sf k}(A)\}\cup\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\}\subset{\cal H}_{\mathbb{S}}[{\sf k}(A)\cup\{\rho\}]
.

Proof
.
4.9
.
1
.
We see easily that

v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
is an
RS
-term of level
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.

4.9
.
2
.
We see

\left(Tm(\alpha)\right)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\subset Tm(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
from
Proposition
4.9
.
1
.
Conversely let
u
be an
RS
-term with
{\sf k}(u)=\{\beta_{i}:i<n\}

and
\max\{\beta_{i}:i<n\}=|u|<\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.
By
Lemma
3.41

there are ordinal terms
\gamma_{i}\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
such that

\gamma_{i}\in M_{\rho}
and

\gamma_{i}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\beta_{i}
.
Let
v
be an
RS
-term obtained from
u
by replacing each constant
{\sf L}_{\beta_{i}}
by

{\sf L}_{\gamma_{i}}
.
We obtain
v^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv u
,
v\in Tm(\alpha)
, and
{\sf k}(v)=\{\gamma_{i}:i<n\}\subset M_{\rho}
.
This means
v\in\left(Tm(\alpha)\right)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
.

4.9
.
3
.
We see readily that
{\sf k}(A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset\{\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\alpha\in{\sf k}(A)\}\cup\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\}
.
From this and Proposition
3.53
.
4

{\sf k}(A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset{\cal H}_{\mathbb{S}}[{\sf k}(A)\cup\{\rho\}]
follows.

\Box

Proposition 4.10

For
RS
-formulas
A
, let

A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
and assume

{\sf k}(A)\subset M_{\rho}
with
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
.
Then

A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigvee\left((A_{\iota})^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\right)_{\iota\in[\rho]J}

for
[\rho]J=\{\iota\in J:{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}\}
.

Proof
. This is seen from Proposition
4.9
.
2
.
Let
A
be a formula
st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u)
such that
\mathsf{k}(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))=\mathsf{k}(u)\subset M_{\rho}
.
Then
st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u)\simeq\bigvee({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}=u)_{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}\in J_{i}}

with

J_{i}=\{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}:\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}\cap M_{[\bar{\rho}]},\,|u|\geq\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]\}
.
If
|u|<\mathbb{S}
, then
|u|<\rho
,
(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u)
,
[\rho]J_{i}=J_{i}
and

({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}=u)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}=u)
by
u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}=u
.
Let
|u|\geq\mathbb{S}
. We have

(st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u))^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]*[\rho]}(u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\simeq\bigvee({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}([\bar{\rho}]*[\rho])}=u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})_{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}([\bar{\rho}]*[\rho])}\in I}

with

I=\{{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}([\bar{\rho}]*[\rho])}:\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}\cap M_{[\bar{\rho}]*[\rho]},\,|u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}|\geq\mathbb{S}([\bar{\rho}]*[\rho])\}
.
For
{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}\in J_{i}
,
{\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}\in[\rho]J_{i}
iff
\mathsf{k}({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]})=\{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]\}\subset M_{\rho}

iff
\mathbb{S}\in M_{[\bar{\rho}]*[\rho]}
,
and
({\sf L}_{\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]}=u)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv({\sf L}_{(\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}])[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}=u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})
.
On the other hand we have
\mathbb{S}([\bar{\rho}]*[\rho])=(\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}])[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
, and
|u|\geq\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}]
iff

|u^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}|=|u|[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\geq(\mathbb{S}[\bar{\rho}])[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\mathbb{S}([\bar{\rho}]*[\rho])
for
\mathbb{S}\in M_{[\bar{\rho}]*[\rho]}
.

\Box

Proposition 4.11

Let
{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}
with
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
.
Then

\mbox{{\rm rk}}(\iota^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})=\left(\mbox{{\rm rk}}(\iota)\right)[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.

Proof
. We see that
\mbox{{\rm rk}}(\iota)\in M_{\rho}

from Proposition
4.7
.
2
.
The proposition is seen from
the facts

(\omega\alpha)[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\omega(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}])
and
(\alpha+1)[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]+1
when
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
.

\Box

4.2
A preview of elimination procedures of stable ordinals

Let us explain briefly our elimination procedures of stable ordinals in this section and
section
5
.
We define two derivability relations

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma

and

({\cal H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma

in subsections
4.3
and
4.4
, resp.
In the former relation,

c
is a bound on ranks of the formulas for stability and of cut formulas as well as
successor stable ordinals collected in
\mathtt{D}
.

The rôle of the former calculus
\vdash^{*a}_{c}
is twofold:
first finite proof figures are embedded in the calculus.
Here is the
axiom
for the stability
L_{\mathbb{S}}\prec_{\Sigma_{1}}L
of
\mathbb{S}

\lnot B(u),\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x)

(14)

Each formula in it is of rank
<\mathbb{I}_{N}
, and
the cut rank
c
in
\vdash^{*a}_{c}
is lowered to
\mathbb{I}_{N}+1
.
Then the derivation is collapsed down to a
\beta<\mathbb{I}_{N}
using the collapsing function

\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\alpha)
.
For Collapsing
4.22
we need
a standard requirement (
17
)
in operator controlled derivations.
Namely
\{\gamma,a,c\}\cup\mathsf{k}(\Gamma)\cup\mathtt{D}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
when
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma
.
Moreover
\mathbb{S}\in\mathtt{D}
when
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\lnot B(u),\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x)
.

The axiom (
14
) says that

\mathbb{S}
‘reflects’
\Pi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
-formulas in transfinite levels for a bigger ordinal
\mathbb{I}_{N}>\mathbb{S}

such that
L=L_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
.
In order to analyze the reflections,
Mahlo classes
Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)
are introduced in Definition
3.8
.
2
.

\pi\in Mh^{a}_{i,c}(\xi)

reflects every fact

\pi\in Mh_{i,0}^{a}(g_{c})=\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,d}(g(d)):c>d\in{\rm supp}(g)\}

on the ordinals
\pi\in Mh_{i,c}^{a}(\xi)
in lower level,
down to
‘smaller’ Mahlo classes
Mh_{i,c}^{a}(f)=\bigcap\{Mh^{a}_{i,d}(f(d)):c\leq d\in{\rm supp}(f)\}
.

This apparatus would suffice to analyze reflections in transfinite levels.
We need another for the axiom
L_{\mathbb{S}}\prec_{\Sigma_{1}}L
, i.e.,
a (formal)
Mostowski collapsing
:
Assume that
B(u,v)
with
v\in L_{\mathbb{S}}
for a
\Delta_{0}
-formula
B
.
We need to find a substitute
u^{\prime}\in L_{\mathbb{S}}
for
u\in L
such that

B(u^{\prime},v)
.
For simplicity let us assume that
v=\beta<\mathbb{S}
and
u=\alpha
are ordinals.
We may assume that
\alpha\geq\mathbb{S}
.
Let
\rho<\mathbb{S}
be an ordinal, which is bigger than every ordinal
<\mathbb{S}

occurring in the ‘context’ of
B(\alpha,\beta)
.
This means that
\delta<\rho
holds for every ordinal
\delta<\mathbb{S}
occurring in a ‘relevant’ branch of a
derivation of
B(\alpha,\beta)
.
Then we can define a Mostwosiki collapsing
\alpha\mapsto\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
for
ordinal terms
\alpha
such that

\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\beta
for each relevant
\beta<\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{S}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=\rho
, cf. Definition
3.33
.
Then we see that
B(\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}],\beta)
holds.

Let
M_{\rho}
be the set in (
10
) of Definition
3.30
.
1
.
Roughly
\alpha\in M_{\rho}
if
every subterm
\beta<\mathbb{S}
of
\alpha
is smaller than
\rho
.
It is shown in Lemma
3.50
.
1
that

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[M_{\rho}]\subset M_{\rho}

if
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\rho]\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\rho
.
Let
{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\vdash^{a}_{c}\Gamma
, and
assume that
\{\gamma,a,c\}\cup{\sf k}(\Gamma)\subset{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.
Moreover let us assume that
\Theta\subset M_{\rho}
holds.
Then we obtain

\{\gamma,a,c\}\cup{\sf k}(\Gamma)\subset{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\subset{\cal H}_{\gamma}[M_{\rho}]\subset M_{\rho}
.
This means that
{\sf k}(\Gamma)\subset M_{\rho}
holds
as long as
\Theta\subset M_{\rho}
holds, i.e., as long as
we are concerned with branches for
{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}

in, e.g., inferences
(\bigwedge)
:
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}

{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\vdash^{a}_{c}\Gamma,A\{{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c}\Gamma,A,A_{\iota}\}_{\iota\in J}\leadsto{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\vdash^{a}_{c}\Gamma,A\{{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c}\Gamma,A,A_{\iota}\}_{\iota\in J,\,{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}}

(15)

and dually
{\sf k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}
for a minor formula
A_{\iota}

of a
(\bigvee)
with the main formula
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
,

provided that

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\rho]\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\rho
.
The proviso means that
\gamma_{1}>\gamma
when
\rho=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{f}(\gamma_{1})
.
Such a
\rho
is in
{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
only when
\rho\in\Theta
.

Let us try to replace the axiom (
14
)
by inferences for reflection of
\rho
with
\Theta\subset M_{\rho}
:
If
B(u)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
holds, then
B(u)^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
holds for some
\sigma<\rho
.

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta\cup\{\rho\}]\vdash\Gamma^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\lx@proof@logical@and\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta\cup\{\rho\}]\vdash\Gamma^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]},B(u)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta\cup\{\rho,\sigma\}]\vdash\Gamma^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]},\lnot B(u)^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\}_{\Theta\subset M_{\sigma},\sigma<\rho}

In analyzing the inferences for reflections in transfinite levels,
formulas
\Gamma^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
are replaced by
\Gamma^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
.
This means that
\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]
is substituted for each
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.
Namely a composition of uncollapsing and collapsing
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\mapsto\alpha\mapsto\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]

arises.
Hence we need
\alpha\in M_{\sigma}\subsetneq M_{\rho}
for
\sigma<\rho
.
However we have
\sigma\not\in M_{\sigma}
although
\sigma\in M_{\rho}
, and we cannot replace
[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
by
[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]

in the upper part of
\Gamma^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]},B(u)^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}
.
The schema seems to be broken.

Instead of an explicit collapsing
[ρ/S]
, formulas could put on
caps

\rho,\sigma,\ldots

in such a way that
{\sf k}(A^{(\sigma)})={\sf k}(A)
and
\mathrm{rk}(A^{(\sigma)})=\mathrm{rk}(A)
.
This means that the cap
\sigma
does not ‘occur’ in a
capped
formula
A^{(\sigma)}
.
Caps are temporary ones.
A capped formula
A^{(\rho)}
might put on another cap
\sigma
as
A^{(\sigma)}
as long as

\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\rho}\cap M_{\sigma}
.

If we choose an ordinal
\gamma_{0}
big enough (depending on a given finite proof figure),
every ordinal ‘occurring’ in derivations (including the subscript
\gamma<\gamma_{0}
in
the operators
{\cal H}_{\gamma}
)
is in
C_{\gamma_{0}}(0)

for the ordinal
\gamma_{0}
, while
each cap
\rho
exceeds a
threshold

\gamma_{1}\leq\gamma_{0}
in the sense that

\rho\not\in{\cal H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\rho]\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\rho
.
Then every ordinal ‘occurring’ in derivations is in the domain
M_{\rho}

of the Mostowski collapsing
\alpha\mapsto\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.

The ordinal
\gamma_{1}
is a threshold, which means that
every ordinal occurring in the relevant branches for
\rho
is in
C_{\gamma_{1}}(0)
and
the subscript
\gamma<\gamma_{1}
in
{\cal H}_{\gamma}
, while
each
\rho\in\mathtt{Q}
for a finite set
\mathtt{Q}
of ordinals, exceeds
\gamma_{1}
in such a way that

{\tt p}_{0}(\rho)\geq\gamma_{1}
for the ordinal
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
in Definition
3.30
.
2
.
This ensures us that
{\cal H}_{\gamma}[M_{\rho}]\subset M_{\rho}
.
In the end, inferences for reflections are removed
by moving outside
C_{\gamma_{1}}(0)
.

If we have a single stable ordinal, then
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{0}
suffices.
Now we have several (successor) stable ordinals
\mathbb{S},\mathbb{T},\ldots\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})

for a
finite
collection
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
of successor stable ordinals, cf. Definition
4.23
.
1a
.
Axioms for stability and their children for reflections are eliminated
first for bigger
\mathbb{S}>\mathbb{T}
, and then smaller ones
\mathbb{T}
.
Therefore we need an assignment
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\ni\mathbb{S}\mapsto\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}
for
thresholds so that

\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}<\gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathtt{Q}}<\gamma_{0}
if
\mathbb{S}>\mathbb{T}
in Definition
4.23
.
2
.

Furthermore each formula
A
puts on a list
\bar{\rho}=(\rho_{0},\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{n-1})
of ordinals
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S}_{i})=\rho_{i}\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}

for each
\mathbb{S}_{i}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
, which we call a
cap
over
\mathtt{Q}
since a single formula might be related to several collapsing
processes
[\rho_{i}/\mathbb{S}_{i}]
.
A capped formula is formally a pair
(A,\bar{\rho})
of a formula
A
and a cap
\bar{\rho}
, denoted by
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
We assume that
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}=\bigcap_{i<n}M_{\rho_{i}}
.

In the second derivability relation

({\cal H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma

of subsection
4.4
,
an operator
{\cal H}_{\gamma}
together with a finite set
\Theta
of ordinals and
a finite family
\mathtt{Q}\subset\coprod_{\mathbb{S}}\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
controls
ordinals occurring in derivations, where

\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
is a finite set of successor stable ordinals
\mathbb{S}
and

\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
is a finite set of ordinals
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
for each
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
Furthermore
\mathtt{Q}
carries thresholds.
The first subscript
c
is a bound on ranks of cut formulas, while
the second one
\xi
is a bound on stable ordinals.
We have an inference
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho))
in the second calculus to derive the axiom (
14
):

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma\lx@proof@logical@and\{(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c,\xi}\Gamma,\lnot\delta^{(\bar{\rho})}\}_{\delta\in\Delta}\{(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c,\xi}\Delta^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},\Gamma\}_{\sigma<\rho,\Theta\cap M_{\rho}\subset M_{\sigma}}

where
\mathbb{S}
is a successor
i
-stable ordinal

\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cap\xi
,
\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
,

a_{0}<a
,

\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\{(\mathbb{S},\sigma)\}
,

\Delta
is
a set of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
-formulas
\delta
, and

\bar{\rho}_{\sigma}
is obtained from the list
\bar{\rho}
by replacing a component
\rho=\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})
by
\sigma
.

In Lemma
5.1
(Capping), the first calculus is embedded in the second in such a way that
if
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma
, then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{c+a}_{c,c}\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}
, where each formula
A\in\Gamma
puts on the same cap
\bar{\rho}
.

The axiom (
14
) is derived in the second calculus as follows.

\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})},(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}\lx@proof@logical@and\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})},B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})}\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},(B(u)^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}

(16)

where
D_{0}
denotes a derivation of a tautology

\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho})},A^{(\bar{\rho})}
with
A\equiv B(u)
, while
D_{1}
is a derivation of another ‘tautology’

\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},(A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
assuming

\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}
, and
\sigma<\rho
varies through ordinals such that
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\sigma}
.
Let
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
.
The branches of inferences
(\bigwedge)
with the capped major formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}

are restricted to
\iota\in J
such that
\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}
in
(
15
),
cf. Tautology
4.33
.
1
.

Since the stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
is arbitrary, this means that the formula
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
is derived from
formulas
A_{\iota}
such that
\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\bigcap\{M_{\tau}:\tau\in\bigcup_{\mathbb{T}}\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})\}
.

M_{\sigma}\subset M_{\rho}
holds for
\{\sigma<\rho\}\subset\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
, cf. Definition
4.23
.
1b
, and
this amounts to

\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}
for each least
\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}=\min\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.

Next let us examine a derivation of

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},(A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
, where

\sigma<\rho
and

\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})=\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cup\{\sigma\}
.
We obtain

A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})_{\iota\in[\sigma]J}
by Proposition
4.10
.
Let
B\equiv A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
with
B\simeq\bigwedge(B_{\nu})_{\nu\in I}
and
B_{\nu}\equiv A_{\iota}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}

for
I=\{\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}:\iota\in[\sigma]J\}
and
\nu=\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
.
Let
\iota\in[\sigma]J
be such that
|\iota|\geq\mathbb{S}
.
Then
\nu=\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\neq\iota
,
\mathbb{S}>|\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}|\geq\sigma=\mathbb{S}[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]
and

\mathsf{k}(\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})\not\subset M_{\sigma}
for
\sigma=\min\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})
.
Therefore the capped formula
(A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
needs to be derived from formulas

B_{\nu}
with
\mathsf{k}(\nu)\not\subset M_{\sigma}
when the cap
\rho
is not the smallest one in
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})

even if
\rho
is the least one in
\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
, i.e.,
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}>\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})}=\sigma
.

This leads us to the
inverse

\mathrm{inv}((A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv A
of
a capped formula

Next let us consider a cut-elimination procedure in the second calculus.
We need to lower the ranks
\mathrm{rk}(A^{(\bar{\rho})})=\mathrm{rk}(A)
of capped formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
down to

\mathrm{rk}(A^{(\bar{\rho})})\leq\mathbb{S}
in eliminating the largest stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
in the domain
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
We have an inference
(\mathtt{D})
in the first calculus
\vdash^{*}
at which a successor stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
is eliminated from the collection
\mathtt{D}
:

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*a_{0}}_{c}\Gamma

where
\mathbb{S}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\cap c
and
a_{0}<a
.
Corresponding to it, there is an inference
(\mathrm{dom})
in the second calculus
\vdash
:

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{P})\vdash\Gamma(\mathtt{P})

where
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{P})=\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cup\{\mathbb{S}\}
and the cap
\bar{\rho}
of each
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma

is extended to
\bar{\rho}*(\rho_{\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})})
in
\Gamma(\mathtt{P})
.

Due to this inference
(\mathrm{dom})
, an inversion lemma seems not to hold.
An inversion lemma for the calculus
\vdash
would state that ’if

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma,A^{(\bar{\rho})}
with
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
, then
for each
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J
,

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q});\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma,A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})}
holds’.
Consider the following trunk of a derivation:

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash\Gamma,A^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{P})\vdash\Gamma(\mathtt{P}),A^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathtt{P})\{(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{P}),\mathtt{P})\vdash\Gamma(\mathtt{P}),A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathtt{P})\}_{\iota\in[\bar{\rho}(\mathtt{P})\mathtt{P}]J}

where
\bar{\rho}(\mathtt{P})=\bar{\rho}*(\rho_{\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})})
.

[\bar{\rho}(\mathtt{P})\mathtt{P}]J\subsetneq[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J
may be the case.
For example let
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\{\mathbb{T}\}
with
\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})=\{\rho\}
,
and
\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})=\{\sigma\}
for an ordinal
\sigma\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
and
\mathbb{T}>\mathbb{S}
.
Even
if
\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset M_{\rho}
,

\mathsf{k}(\iota)\not\subset M_{\sigma}
is possible, e.g., when
\sigma\leq|\iota|<\mathbb{S}<\rho
.

The lack of an inversion lemma is not a serious problem to prove a cut-elimination theorem
5.4
, and
we see in Lemma
5.2
that inferences
(cut)
are interchangeable with

:

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash\Gamma\lx@proof@logical@and(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash\Gamma,\lnot C^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash C^{(\bar{\rho})},\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{P})\vdash C^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathtt{P}),\Gamma(\mathtt{P})

turns to

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{P})\vdash\Gamma(\mathtt{P})\lx@proof@logical@and(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{P})\vdash\Gamma(\mathtt{P}),\lnot C^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathtt{P})(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{P})\vdash C^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathtt{P}),\Gamma(\mathtt{P})

where a derivation

is obtained from the derivation

by

\mathtt{Q}
to
\mathtt{P}
.

However we cannot interchange inferences

In Lemma

stable ordinals are removed from derivations as follows.

,

\mathtt{Q}
to
\mathbb{W}
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}},\Theta_{\mathbb{W}*},\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{W})\vdash_{\mathbb{W},\mathbb{W}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{W}*]}

.

In particular let
\mathbb{S}_{0}=\Omega^{\dagger}
be the least stable ordinal.

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}},\Theta_{\mathbb{S}_{0}*},\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}_{0})\vdash_{\mathbb{S}_{0},\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{S}_{0}*]}

\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}_{0})=\emptyset
.
This means that every formula occurring in the derivation puts on the empty cap
\bar{\rho}
, and is uncapped.
Finally by eliminating uncapped cut formulas and collapsing the derivations down to
<\Omega
,
we obtain Theorem

4.3
Operator controlled
*
-derivations

Let

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}:=\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\emptyset]
.
By a
successor stable ordinal
we mean ordinals in
SSt=\bigcup_{0<i\leq N}SSt_{i}
,
and
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}:=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger 1}
.
In this section and the next section
5

let us fix an additive principal number
\gamma_{0}=\omega_{m+2}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)\in{\cal H}_{0}
.
The ordinal
\gamma_{0}
depends on a given finite proof figure in
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
,
and is specified in the end of section
5
.

In what follows by ordinals we mean ordinal terms in
C_{\gamma_{0}}(0)
.

We define a derivability relation

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma

where
\mathtt{D}
is a collection of successor stable ordinals, and

c
is a bound of ranks of the formulas as well as stable ordinals
\mathbb{S}
in the axioms
(i{\rm-stbl}(\mathbb{S}))
, and one of ranks of cut formulas.
The relation depends on an ordinal
\gamma_{0}
, and
should be written as
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c,\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
.
However the ordinal
\gamma_{0}
will be fixed.
So let us omit it.

Definition 4.12

Let
\Theta\subset C_{\gamma_{0}}(0)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a finite set of ordinals,

(\bigvee)

Lemma 4.13

(Tautology)

(\mathcal{H}_{0},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A);\emptyset)\vdash^{*2d}_{0}\lnot A,A

holds for

d=\mathrm{rk}(A)
.

Proof
. By induction on
d
.

\Box

Lemma 4.14

(Equality)

(\mathcal{H}_{0},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A,u,v);\emptyset)\vdash^{*\omega(|u|\#|v|)\#2d}_{0}u\neq v,\lnot A(u),A(v)
holds for
d=\mathrm{rk}(A(\mathsf{L}_{0}))
.

Proof
. By induction on
d
, cf.
[
9
,
3
]
.

Let
\Theta=\mathsf{k}(u,v,w)
.
First show that

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\emptyset)\vdash^{*\alpha}_{0}u\neq v,u\not\in w,v\in w
,

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\emptyset)\vdash^{*\alpha}_{0}u\neq v,u\neq w,v=w

and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\emptyset)\vdash^{*\alpha}_{0}u\neq v,w\not\in u,w\in v

simultaneously by induction on the natural sum
|u|\#|v|\#|w|
, where

\alpha=\omega(|u|\#|v|\#|w|)
.
Then the lemma is seen by induction on
d=\mbox{{\rm rk}}(A(\mathsf{L}_{0}))
.

\Box

Lemma 4.15

(Embedding of Axioms)

For each axiom
A
in
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}

there is an
m<\omega
such that

(\mathcal{H}_{0},\mathsf{k}(\Gamma);\emptyset)\vdash^{*\mathbb{I}_{N}\cdot 2+m}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+m,\gamma_{0}}A,\Gamma

holds for every sequent
\Gamma
.

Proof
. In the proof, let us suppress the operator
{\cal H}_{0}
, and
write
\vdash^{*}
for

Lemma 4.16

(Embedding)

If
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\vdash\Gamma
for sets
\Gamma
of sentences,

4.15

as in

,
3
]
.

Lemma 4.17

(Inversion)

Let
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma,A
.

1.

Let
\mathrm{rk}(A)\geq\mathbb{I}_{N}
, and
\iota\in J
be such that
\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma,A_{\iota}
holds.

2.

Let
A\equiv(\forall x\,B)\in\Pi_{1}(\mathbb{I}_{N})
, and
\beta\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
. Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma,A^{(\beta,\mathbb{I}_{N})}
holds, where
A^{(\beta,\mathbb{I}_{N})}\equiv(\forall x\in\mathsf{L}_{\beta}B)
.

Proof
. By induction on
a
.
Since
\mathrm{rk}(A)\geq\mathbb{I}_{N}
,
A
is not a major formula of any of
(i{\rm-stbl}(\mathbb{S}))
.

\Box

Lemma 4.18

Let
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma
and
\mathbb{S}<c
be a successor stable ordinal.
Then
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\{\mathbb{S}\};\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma
holds.

Proof
. By induction on
a
.

\Box

Lemma 4.19

(Reduction)

Let
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\lnot C
and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*b}_{c}C,\Gamma_{1}
for

C\simeq\bigvee(C_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
with
\mathbb{I}_{N}\leq\mathrm{rk}(C)\leq c
,
c>\mathbb{I}_{N}
and
a\geq b
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a+b}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
holds.

Proof
. By induction on
b
.
Since
\mathrm{rk}(C)\geq\mathbb{I}_{N}
,
C
is not a major formula of any
(i{\rm-stbl}(\mathbb{S}))
.

Case 1
.
b=0
. Then
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*b}_{c}C,\Gamma_{1}
follows either from a void
(\bigwedge)

with a major formula in
\Gamma_{1}
or from an
(i{\rm-stbl}(\mathbb{S}))
. In each case we have

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*0}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
.
Let
b>0
.

Case 2
.
\mathrm{rk}(C)<c
: Then a
(cut)
yields the lemma by
b\leq a<a+b
.
In what follows assume
\mathrm{rk}(C)=c>\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

Case 3
.
The last inference in
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*b}_{c}C,\Gamma_{1}
is a
(\bigvee)
with the
major formula
C
.
We have
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*b_{0}}_{c}C_{\iota},C,\Gamma_{1}
for
\iota\in J
and
b_{0}<b
.
IH yields
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a+b_{0}}_{c}C_{\iota},\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
.
Assuming
\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset\mathsf{k}(C_{\iota})
, we obtain
\mathsf{k}(\iota)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
by (
17
), and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\lnot C_{\iota}
by Inversion
4.17
.
1
.
We obtain
\mathrm{rk}(C_{\iota})<\mathrm{rk}(C)
by Proposition
4.7
.
4
, and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a+b}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
by a
(cut)
.

Case 4
.
The last inference in
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*b}_{c}C,\Gamma_{1}
is a
(\mathtt{D})
:
We have
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*b_{0}}_{c}C,\Gamma_{1}
for a
b_{0}<b
and

\mathbb{S}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.
We obtain

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\{\mathbb{S}\};\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\lnot C
by Lemma
4.18
, and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*a}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\lnot C
.
IH yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*a+b_{0}}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
, and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a+b}_{c}\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
by a
(\mathtt{D})
.

Other cases are seen from IH.

4.19
.

(
17
) follows from the assumption.

4.4
Operator controlled derivations with caps

Let
\Lambda_{0}=\beta
be the ordinal in Collapsing
4.22
, and

\Lambda:=\Gamma(\Lambda_{0})
.

\Lambda_{0}
is a bound on the ordinals
s(\rho)
in inferences
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,x,f))
of Definition
4.30
, and

\Lambda
is the base of the
\tilde{\theta}
-function

\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi)=\tilde{\theta}_{b}(\xi;\Lambda)
in Definition
3.1
.
Definitions

4.23
,

4.29
and
4.30
depend on the ordinals
\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}
.

Definition 4.23

\Lambda_{0}
is the ordinal
\beta
in Collapsing
4.22
such that

\Lambda_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\gamma+\omega^{a})
,

\gamma+\omega^{a}<\gamma_{0}
and
\{\gamma,a\}\subset C_{0}(0)
.

A pair
(\mathtt{Q},\gamma_{\cdot}^{\mathtt{Q}})

is said to be a
finite family for pairs
(\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0})
of ordinals

if the following conditions are met.

1.

\mathtt{Q}

is a finite function
\mathtt{Q}\subset\coprod_{\mathbb{S}}\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
for which the following hold:

(a)

Its domain
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
is a finite set of successor stable ordinals
\mathbb{S}<\Lambda_{0}
.

(b)

\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
is a non-empty finite set of ordinals
\kappa
in
\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
for each
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})

such that

\forall\{\tau<\kappa\}\subset\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})(M_{\tau}\subset M_{\kappa})
,

\forall\mathbb{U}<\mathbb{S}(\kappa\in M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{U})})

and
m(\kappa)
is a special finite function
with base
\Lambda
such that

\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
.

Let

\bigcup\mathtt{Q}=\bigcup\{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S}):\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\}
and

M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\bigcap\{M_{\rho}:\rho\in\bigcup\mathtt{Q}\}
.
Let
\sigma,\rho\in\{\mathtt{u}\}\cup\bigcup\mathtt{Q}
.
We say that
\sigma
is a
predecessor
of
\rho
in
\mathtt{Q}
, denoted by
\sigma=pd(\rho;\mathtt{Q})

if there exists an
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
such that
\sigma,\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
and

\sigma=\max\{\tau\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S}):\tau<\rho\}
.
When there are no such
\mathbb{S}
and
\sigma
, let
pd(\rho;\mathtt{Q})=\rho
.

2.

\gamma_{\cdot}^{\mathtt{Q}}
is a
threshold function

\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\ni\mathbb{S}\mapsto\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}<\gamma_{0}
such that

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}
holds for every
\{\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}\}\subset\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
,
and the following
(
20
) is met.

\forall\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})(\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq b(\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S}))<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S})

(20)

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq b(\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S}))<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}
means that

\forall\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})(\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq b(\rho)<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S})
, where

b(\rho)=a
for
\rho=\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(a)
.

Let

\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}:=\min\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
for
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.

A pair
(\mathtt{Q},\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot})
is simply denoted by
\mathtt{Q}

when the function
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}

is irrelevant.

Definition 4.24

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family for
(\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0})
with thresholds
\gamma_{\cdot}^{\mathtt{Q}}
.

1.

The threshold function
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
is uniquely extended for

\mathbb{S}\in\{\Omega\}\cup St
by

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}:=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}

for
\mathbb{T}=\min\{\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}):\mathbb{T}\geq\mathbb{S}\}
if such a
\mathbb{T}
exists.
Otherwise let
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}=0
.

2.

The threshold function
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
with an ordinal
\gamma
is said to have
gaps

\eta
if
for every
\{\alpha<\beta\}\subset\{0,\gamma\}\cup\{\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}:\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\}
,

\alpha+\eta<\beta
holds.

3.

For a finite set
\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})\subset\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
,

\mathtt{Q}\cup\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})=\mathtt{P}

denotes a pair
(\mathtt{P},\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\cdot})
enjoying the following:

(a)

\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{P})=\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cup\{\mathbb{S}\}
,

\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{T})=\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})
for
\mathbb{T}\neq\mathbb{S}
.

\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})=\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cup\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})
.

(b)

\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\cdot}
extends
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
in such a way that

\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{T}}=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}
for
\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}>\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}+\mathbb{T}
for every
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}>\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}
for every
\mathbb{S}>\mathbb{U}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.

4.

For an ordinal
\xi
,
\mathtt{R}=\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\xi
denotes the restriction of
\mathtt{Q}
to
\xi
defined as follows.

(a)

\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{R})=\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cap\xi
,

\mathtt{R}(\mathbb{T})=\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})
for
\mathbb{T}<\xi
.

(b)

\gamma^{\mathtt{R}}_{\cdot}
is the restriction of
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
to
\xi
in such a way that

\gamma^{\mathtt{R}}_{\mathbb{T}}=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}
for
\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cap\xi
.

Definition 4.25

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family.

1.

A function
\bar{\rho}\in\prod_{\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})}\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
is said to be a
cap
over
\mathtt{Q}
.
For each
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
,
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
holds.

Let
M_{\bar{\rho}}:=\bigcap\{M_{\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})}:\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\}
, and

\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
denotes the least cap such that
\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}(\mathbb{S})=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
for each
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
The cap
\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
over the empty family
\mathtt{Q}
, i.e.,
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\emptyset
, is
the empty function
\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}=\emptyset
. Then let

M_{\emptyset}=OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

2.

Let
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
and
\sigma
be an ordinal such that
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\{(\mathbb{S},\sigma)\}
is a finite family, where

\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})=\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cup\{\sigma\}
.

For a cap
\bar{\rho}
over
\mathtt{Q}
,
\bar{\rho}[\sigma/\rho]
denotes a cap
\bar{\tau}
over
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}

such that
\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{T})=\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T})
for
\mathbb{T}\neq\mathbb{S}
.

\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})
is defined as follows.

\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})=\sigma
if
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})=\rho
, and
\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})=\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})
else.

3.

Let
\bar{\rho}
be a cap over
\mathtt{Q}
, and
\mathtt{P}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})
be a finite family with

\mathbb{S}\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
For
\rho\in\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})
,
\bar{\tau}=\bar{\rho}*(\rho)
denote a cap over
\mathtt{P}
such that

\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})=\rho
, and
\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{T})=\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T})
for
\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.

4.

By a
capped formula
over
\mathtt{Q}
we mean a pair
(A,\bar{\rho})
of
RS
-sentence
A

and a cap
\bar{\rho}=(\rho_{i})_{i<n}
over
\mathtt{Q}

such that

\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}
.
Such a pair is denoted by
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
A capped formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
is said to be a
\Sigma(\pi)
-formula if

A\in\Sigma(\pi)
.

Let
{\sf k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})}):={\sf k}(A)
and
\mathrm{rk}(A^{(\bar{\rho})}):=\mathrm{rk}(A)
.

5.

A
sequent
is a finite set of capped formulas, denoted by

\Gamma_{0}^{(\bar{\rho}_{0})},\ldots,\Gamma_{m}^{(\bar{\rho}_{m})}
,
where each formula in the set
\Gamma_{i}^{(\bar{\rho}_{i})}
puts on the cap

\bar{\rho}_{i}
.
When we write
\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}
, we tacitly assume that

\mathsf{k}(\Gamma)\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}
.

6.

Let
\Gamma
be a sequent.

\Gamma(\rho)=\{A^{(\bar{\rho})}:A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma,\,\rho\in\bar{\rho}\}
denotes the set of capped formulas with a cap
\rho\in\bar{\rho}
in
\Gamma
.

Actually we need caps
\bar{\rho}\in\prod_{\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})}\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
which are reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
in the following sense.

Definition 4.26

Let
\bar{\rho}
be a cap over a finite family
\mathtt{Q}
, and
\mathbb{S}
an ordinal.

1.

A cap
\bar{\rho}
is said to be
reduced
for
\mathtt{Q}
if there is at most one
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})

such that
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})>\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
.
Such an
\mathbb{S}
is said to be the
collapsing point
of the cap
\bar{\rho}
, and
denoted by

\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})
if it exists.
Otherwise let
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})=\Lambda_{0}
.

2.

A capped formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
is
reduced
for
\mathtt{Q}
if its cap
\bar{\rho}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
,
and
A\in\Sigma_{1}(\mathbb{S})\cup\Delta_{0}(\mathbb{S})
for
\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})
.

3.

A set
\Gamma
of capped formulas is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
if every formula in
\Gamma
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
.

It is clear that if
\bar{\rho}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
, then so is
\bar{\rho}[\sigma/\rho]
for
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}
, and

\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho}[\sigma/\rho];\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})
.

For the least cap
\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
, it is clear that
A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
.
Next let
\bar{\rho}
be a reduced cap for
\mathtt{Q}
.
Let
\sigma
be an ordinal such that
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\{(\mathbb{S},\sigma)\}
is a finite family and

\sigma=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})}
with an
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
Then
\bar{\rho}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}
iff either
\bar{\rho}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
or

\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})
.
In each case we obtain
\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})
.

Finally let
\mathtt{P}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})
be a finite family for
\mathbb{S}\not\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
Then the cap
\bar{\rho}*(\rho_{\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})})
over
\mathtt{P}
is reduced for
\mathtt{P}

if
\bar{\rho}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
.

Definition 4.27

1.

Let
\sigma\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
, and
A
an uncapped formula.
Then a formula
A[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}
is obtained from
A
by replacing each
\alpha\in\mathsf{k}(A)
by
the uncollapsing
\alpha[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}
of
\alpha
, cf. Definition
3.44
,
and each
st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]*[\sigma]}(u)
is replaced by
st_{i}^{[\bar{\rho}]}(u[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1})
if

|u[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}|\geq\mathbb{S}
.

2.

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family, and
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
a reduced formula for
\mathtt{Q}
.
If

\mathrm{rk}(A)<\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})<\Lambda_{0}
,

\sigma=pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S});\mathtt{Q})<\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})

and
A[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}\not\equiv A

such that
\mathsf{k}(A[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1})\subset M_{\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}}
,
then
the
inverse

\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
of
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
for
\mathtt{Q}
is defined to be the formula

(A[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1})^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
Let
\mathrm{cp}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})
in this case.

Otherwise let

\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\rho)};\mathtt{Q}):\equiv A^{(\bar{\rho})}
and
\mathrm{cp}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\Lambda_{0}
.

By definition, the inverse
\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
of
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
puts on the same cap
(\bar{\rho})
as
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
Let
\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}):\equiv B
when
\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv B^{(\bar{\rho})}
.

Let
A\equiv B^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\not\equiv B
.
Then
\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv B^{(\bar{\rho})}
only when
\sigma=pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S});\mathtt{Q})<\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})
.
Let
\kappa
be an ordinal such that
\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\{(\mathbb{S},\kappa)\}
is a finite family and

\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})>\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}>\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{S})}=\kappa
.
Assuming
\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
, we see

\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})
, cf. (
47
)
in the proof of Recapping
5.6
.

Definition 4.28

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family,
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
a reduced formula for
\mathtt{Q}
, and
\Theta
a finite set of ordinals.

1.

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}):=\mathsf{k}(\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}))
.

2.

For
\iota\in J
with either
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
or
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
,
let

\iota\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J:\Leftrightarrow\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}\,\&\,\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}.

3.

For finite sets
\Theta
of ordinals, let

\Theta(\mathtt{Q}):=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}.

Definition 4.29

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with thresholds
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
.
For
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
,

H_{\rho}(f,\mathtt{Q},\Theta)

denotes the
resolvent class
defined by

\kappa\in H_{\rho}(f,\mathtt{Q},\Theta)
iff

\kappa\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}\cap\rho
,

\kappa\in\bigcap_{\mathbb{U}<\mathbb{S}}M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{U})}
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq b(\kappa)<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}
,

(\Theta\cap M_{\rho})\cup(\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cap\rho)\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset M_{\kappa}
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\kappa)\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
,
and

m(\kappa)
is special such that

f\leq m(\kappa)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a sequent,

\Theta
a finite set of ordinals in
C_{\gamma_{0}}(0)\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
,

\{\gamma,a,c,\xi\}\subset C_{\gamma_{0}}(0)
, and

\mathtt{Q}
a finite family for
(\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0})
with thresholds.

We define another derivability relation
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi,\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
, where

c
is a bound on ranks of cut formulas, and

\xi
a bound on ordinals in
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
Although the relation depends on ordinals
\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}
,
the ordinals will be fixed.
So let us omit these.

Definition 4.30

Let

\Lambda_{0}
be a fixed ordinal in Collapsing
4.22
, and

\Lambda=\Gamma(\Lambda_{0})

(\mathtt{Q},\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot})

(23)

.

.
3
,

(25)

for which

holds for an ordinal

, and

\Gamma(\mathtt{P})=\{A^{(\bar{\rho}*(\rho_{\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})}))}:A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma\}
.

(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,x,f))

There exist a successor
i
-stable ordinal

\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
,
a special finite function

such that
\min\{s(\rho),s(f)\}\geq\mathbb{S}+1
,

and
