,
and
a
set
\Delta
of

\mathcal{L}_{i}
-
formulas
\delta
, for which the following
(r1), (r2), (r3) and (r4) hold:

(r1)

\Delta\subset\bigvee(x):=\{\delta:\mathrm{rk}(\delta)<x,\delta\mbox{ {\rm is a}}\bigvee\mbox{{\rm-formula}}\}
and

\mathrm{rk}(\Delta)<\Lambda_{0}
.

(r2)

\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
,
and

f_{x}=g_{x}\,\&\,f<_{\Lambda}^{x}g^{\prime}(x)
,
cf. Definition
3.31
.
6
.

(r3)

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta_{\iota},\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c,\xi}\Gamma,\lnot\delta_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})}
holds for
each
\delta\in\Delta
and
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J_{\delta}
with
\delta\simeq\bigvee(\delta_{\iota})_{\iota\in J_{\delta}}
,
where
\Theta_{\iota}=\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(\delta_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})
.

(r4)

There exists a reduced set
\Xi\subset\Gamma
for
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}

such that
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\tau};\mathtt{Q})=\mathbb{S}
for every
A^{(\bar{\tau})}\in\Xi

for which

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c,\xi}\Delta^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},\Xi
holds for each
\sigma\in H_{\rho}(f,\mathtt{Q},\Theta)
,
where

\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\{(\mathbb{S},\sigma)\}
denotes
a finite family with thresholds obtained from
\mathtt{Q}
by adding
\sigma
to
\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
, and

\bar{\rho}_{\sigma}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}[\rho/\sigma]
.

For the condition (
21
), let

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma\{(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta_{\iota},\mathtt{P})\vdash^{a_{\iota}}_{c,\xi}\Gamma_{\iota}\}_{\iota}

be an inference.
We see that each upper sequent
\Gamma_{\iota}
is reduced for
\mathtt{P}
if the lower sequent
\Gamma
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
,
cf. the comments after Definition
4.26
.

An inversion lemma for the calculus
\vdash
seems not to hold due to the inference
(\mathrm{dom})
, cf. subsection
4.2
.
In this reason, the major formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
need not to be in the upper sequents
\Gamma_{0}\cup\{A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})}\}

in inferences
(\bigwedge)
.
Furthermore
we perform in advance an inversion step
in inferences
(\bigvee)
,
(cut)
and (r3) of
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,x,f))
.

Lemma 4.31

Let

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma
.
Then

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\subset M_{\rho}\cap\bigcap\{M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}:\mathbb{S}>\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\}
for

\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
with
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cap\xi
.

Proof
. Let
\xi>\mathbb{S}>\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
We have

\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq\min\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho),\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}\}
by
(
24
) and Definition
4.23
.
1b
.
This yields

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[M_{\rho}]\subset M_{\rho}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}]\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}

by Lemma
3.50
.
1
.
On the other hand we have
\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\rho}\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}
and

\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{U})\subset M_{\rho}\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}
for
\mathbb{U}\geq\xi>\mathbb{S}>\mathbb{T}
and
\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
by Definition
4.23
.
1b
.
We obtain
\Theta(\mathtt{Q})=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\rho}\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}
.

\Box

Lemma 4.32

(Weakening)

Let

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma
and

A^{(\bar{\tau})}
be reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
such that

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi}\Gamma,A^{(\bar{\tau})}
holds.

Proof
. By induction on
a
. By the assumption (
22
) is enjoyed.
In inferences
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,x,f))
, add the formula
A^{(\bar{\tau})}
to the lower sequents, and to left upper sequents.

\Box

Lemma 4.33

(Tautology)

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with thresholds
and
\Theta
a finite set of ordinals
such that
\gamma\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
and
(
24
) is enjoyed for an ordinal
\xi
such that
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\subset\xi
.

Let
\sigma=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
for an
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cap\xi
,
and

A
be a formula such that
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
.
Let
d=\mathrm{rk}(A)
.

1.

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d}_{0,\xi}\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})}
holds.
Also

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}+1}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})}
holds for each
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J
when

A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
and
d_{\iota}=\mathrm{rk}(A_{\iota})
.

2.

Let
\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
be such that
\sigma=pd(\rho;\mathtt{Q})<\rho
, and

\bar{\rho}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}[\rho/\sigma]
.
Let
\gamma\geq\mathbb{S}
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d}_{0,\xi}\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},(A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
holds.

Proof
. By induction on
d=\mathrm{rk}(A)
.
By
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
, we obtain

\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
by Lemma
4.31
.

4.33
.
1
.
We have

\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv A
for the least cap
\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}

since there is no
\tau\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})
such that
\tau<\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}(\mathbb{T})=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}
.
Hence

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(A)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
We obtain

d\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
by Proposition
4.7
.
2
for (
23
).

Let
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
and
A_{\iota}\simeq\bigwedge(B_{\nu})_{\nu\in J_{\iota}}
for each
\iota\in J
.
Let
\iota\in[\sigma\mathtt{Q}\xi]J
and
\nu\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J_{\iota}
.
Let
d_{\iota}=\mathrm{rk}(A_{\iota})
and
d_{\nu}=\mathrm{rk}(B_{\nu})
.
We have
d_{\nu}<d_{\iota}<d
.
We obtain

\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
, and

\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[(\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}))(\mathtt{Q})]
.

IH yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu},A_{\iota}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})},B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
A
(\bigvee)
yields
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}+1}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})},A^{(\bar{\rho})}
, and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d}_{0,\xi}\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho})},A^{(\bar{\rho})}
by a
(\bigwedge)
.

4.33
.
2
.
Let
B\equiv A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
. We have
\mathrm{rk}(B)<\mathbb{S}
.
We claim that
\mathrm{inv}(B^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv A
.
We have
\mathrm{inv}(B^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv B[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}\equiv A

if
B\not\equiv A
.
Otherwise
\mathrm{inv}(B^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv A
since

B[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}\equiv B\equiv A
and
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T})=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}
for
\mathbb{T}\neq\mathbb{S}
.
In each case we obtain
\mathsf{k}(B^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(A)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Hence (
22
)
and (
23
)
are enjoyed.

Let
A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
or
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
.
By Proposition
4.10
we obtain

A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigvee(A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}_{\iota})_{\iota\in[\sigma]J}
or

A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigwedge(A^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}_{\iota})_{\iota\in[\sigma]J}
.
Let
I=\{\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}:\iota\in[\sigma]J\}
.

We claim that for
\nu=\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
and
B_{\nu}\equiv A_{\iota}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\mbox{ and }\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J\Leftrightarrow\nu=\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]I

(26)

where

\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J

iff
\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
, while

\iota^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]I
iff

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\subset M_{\sigma}
and

\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Hence the claim (
26
) follows.

For each
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J
, we have
d_{\iota}=\mathrm{rk}(A_{\iota})<d
by Proposition
4.7
.
4
.
IH yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota},C_{\mu}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}}_{0,\xi}\lnot C_{\mu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})}
if
A_{\iota}\simeq\bigvee(C_{\mu})_{\mu\in J_{\iota}}
.
When
A_{\iota}\simeq\bigwedge(C_{\mu})_{\mu\in J_{\iota}}
, we have

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota},C_{\mu}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},(C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
In each case

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}+1}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})}
.

Let
A\simeq\bigvee_{\iota\in J}\bigwedge_{\mu\in J_{\iota}}C_{\mu}
with
C_{\mu}\simeq\bigvee_{\alpha\in J_{\mu}}D_{\alpha}
, and
d_{\mu}=\mathrm{rk}(C_{\mu})
.
IH yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha},C_{\mu}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\mu}}_{0,\xi}\lnot D_{\alpha}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},(C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
for

\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J
,
\mu\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J_{\iota}
and
\alpha\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}\xi]J_{\mu}
.
We see

\mathsf{k}(C_{\mu})=\mathsf{k}(C_{\mu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})

as in (
26
).

We obtain

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha})\cup\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\mu}}_{0,\xi}\lnot D_{\alpha}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},(C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
.
Let
\Theta_{0}=\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\cup\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
.
We have

\mathsf{k}(C_{\mu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(C_{\mu})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta_{0}(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
On the other hand we have

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})=\mathsf{k}((A_{\iota}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta_{0}(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Hence

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d+1}_{0,\xi}\lnot A^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},B^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},B^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\cup\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}}_{0,\xi}\lnot A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},(C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota},D_{\alpha})\cup\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\mu}}_{0,\xi}\lnot D_{\alpha}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}})},(C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}

where
\iota
ranges over
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}\mathtt{Q}]J
by
(
26
).
The case
A\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
is similarly seen.

\Box

5
Elimination of stable ordinals

5.1
Capping and recapping

In this subsection the relation
\vdash^{*}
is embedded in
\vdash
by putting caps on formulas,
and then caps are changed to smaller caps.

Lemma 5.1

(Capping)

Let

\Gamma\subset\Delta_{0}(\mathcal{L}_{N+1})
be a set of uncapped formulas
with
\mbox{{\rm rk}}(\Gamma)<\Lambda_{0}
, where

\Lambda_{0}=\beta<\mathbb{I}_{N}

denotes a fixed ordinal in Collapsing
4.22

such that

a<\Lambda=\Gamma(\Lambda_{0})
, cf. (
19
).

Let

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
, where
\Theta\cup\{\gamma,a\}\subset C_{\gamma_{0}}(0)
,

\mathbb{I}_{N}\leq\gamma<\gamma_{0}
.

Let

g_{0}:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
be a special finite function with base
\Lambda

such that

\mathrm{supp}(g_{0})=\{\Lambda_{0}\}
with
g_{0}(\Lambda_{0})=\Lambda\cdot 3
.

Let
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
be a threshold function
\mathtt{D}\ni\mathbb{S}\mapsto\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}<\gamma_{0}

such that
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}=\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\Lambda\cdot\beta_{\mathbb{S}}
for an
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}
and
an additive principal number
\Lambda>\beta_{\mathbb{S}}\geq F(a):=f(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}+a)=\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}\cdot 2}(\Lambda_{0}+a)
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
together with
\gamma
has gaps
\Lambda\cdot(F(a))
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}}[\Theta]
,
and

\mathrm{fld}(g_{0})=\{\Lambda_{0},\Lambda\cdot 3\}\subset C_{0}(SC(\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}))
,
cf. (
11
).
Let

\rho_{\mathbb{S}}=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g_{0}}(\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}})
, and

\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with thresholds
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
such that

\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})=\{\rho_{\mathbb{S}}\}
for each
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\mathtt{D}\subset\Lambda_{0}
, cf. (
19
).
Let
\bar{\rho}\in\prod_{\mathbb{S}\in\mathtt{D}}\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
be the cap with
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})=\rho_{\mathbb{S}}
for every
\mathbb{S}\in\mathtt{D}
.
Assume

\Theta\subset M_{\bar{\rho}}=\bigcap_{\mathbb{S}\in\mathtt{D}}M_{\rho_{\mathbb{S}}}
.

Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{\Lambda_{0}+a}_{\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}
holds for
c=\xi=\Lambda_{0}

and

\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}=\{A^{(\bar{\rho})}:A\in\Gamma\}
.

Proof
. By induction on
a
.
Let us
write
\vdash^{a}_{\Lambda_{0}}
for
\vdash^{a}_{\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}
in the proof.

For each
\mathbb{S}\in\mathtt{D}
we have
\rho_{\mathbb{S}}=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
and

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\rho_{\mathbb{S}})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(A)
.
On the other hand we have
\Theta\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
by the assumption.
Let
A\in\Gamma
.
We obtain
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
by (
17
) and

\Theta\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
, where
\Theta=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
(
22
) and (
23
) in

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{\Lambda_{0}+a}_{c}\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}

follow from (
17
) in
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c,\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
.
We have
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\mathtt{D}\subset\Lambda_{0}
.
Let
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
. Then by (
17
) we obtain
\mathbb{S}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.

For
\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}
,

\rho_{\mathbb{S}}\in M_{\rho_{\mathbb{T}}}

follows from
\{\mathbb{S},\Lambda_{0},\Lambda,\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\}\subset{\cal H}_{\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}}[\Theta]\subset M_{\rho_{\mathbb{T}}}

and
\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}<\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}+\mathbb{S}<\gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathtt{Q}}=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho_{\mathbb{T}})
.

Case 1
.
First consider the case when the last inference is an
(i{\rm-stbl}(\mathbb{S}))
:
We have
\mathbb{S}\in\mathtt{D}\cap\Lambda_{0}
,
a
\bigwedge
-formula

B(\mathsf{L}_{0})

with
\mathrm{rk}(B(\mathsf{L}_{0}))<\mathbb{S}
, and
a term
u\in Tm(\mathbb{I}_{N})

such that
\mathbb{S}\leq\mathrm{rk}(B(u))<\Lambda_{0}
,

\{\lnot B(u),\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x)\}\subset\Gamma
,
and
\mathsf{k}(B(u))\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
.
We have
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\subset\Lambda_{0}
and

\{\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})},(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}\}\subset\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}
.

Let
f
be a special finite function such that
\mathrm{supp}(f)=\{\Lambda_{0}\}
and

f(\Lambda_{0})=\Lambda
.
Then
f_{\Lambda_{0}}=(g_{0})_{\Lambda_{0}}=\emptyset
and
f<^{\Lambda_{0}}(g_{0})^{\prime}(\Lambda_{0})

by
f(\Lambda_{0})=\Lambda<\Lambda\cdot 2=(g_{0})^{\prime}(\Lambda_{0})
.

Let
d=\mathrm{rk}(B(u))
and
B(u)\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in I}
.
We obtain for
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]I

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B(u))\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d_{\iota}+1}_{0}\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})},A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})}

(27)

by Tautology
4.33
.
1
, where
d_{\iota}=\mathrm{rk}(A_{\iota})
.

On the other hand we have

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B(u)),\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})\vdash^{2d}_{0}\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},(B(u)^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})}
by Tautology
4.33
.
2
,
where

B(u^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})\equiv B(u)^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
.
For
u^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\in Tm(\mathbb{S})
, we see

\mathrm{inv}((B(u^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}))^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})=B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})}
and

\mathsf{k}((B(u^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}))^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})=\mathsf{k}(B(u))\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[(\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B(u)))\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})}\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}}
,
and
u^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}]J
.
A
(\bigvee)
yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B(u)),\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})\vdash^{2d+1}_{\Lambda_{0}}\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho}_{\sigma})},(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}

(28)

We have
\bar{\rho}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
and
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})=\mathbb{S}
by
\bar{\rho}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{S})}=\sigma<\rho=\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})
.
Hence

\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
with
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})=\Lambda_{0}>\mathbb{S}
, and
(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}

for
\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma}
with
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q}^{\sigma})=\mathbb{S}
and
\mathrm{rk}(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))=\mathbb{S}
.
An inference
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,\Lambda_{0},f))
with

\mathrm{rk}(B(u))<\Lambda_{0}\in\mathrm{supp}(m(\rho))
, (
27
) and (
28
)
yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B(u)),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{2d+2}_{\Lambda_{0}}\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})},(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}
, where
2d+2<\Lambda_{0}
and
\{\lnot B(u)^{(\bar{\rho})},(\exists x\in\mathsf{L}_{\mathbb{S}}B(x))^{(\bar{\rho})}\}\subset\Gamma^{(\bar{\rho})}
.

Case 2
. The last inference is a
(\mathtt{D})
:
There exists a successor stable ordinal

\mathbb{S}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]\cap\Lambda_{0}
such that
\mathbb{S}\not\in\mathtt{D}
and

({\cal H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{\Lambda_{0}}\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D}\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\vdash^{*a_{0}}_{\Lambda_{0}}\Gamma

for

Lemma 5.2

\varphi_{b_{1}}(\varphi_{b}(a^{\prime})\#\varphi_{b}(e))<\varphi_{b}(a)
by
b_{1}<b
.

\Box

Lemma 5.4

(Cut-elimination)

Let

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c+b,\xi}\Gamma

with
\forall\mathbb{S}\in St\cap\xi(\mathbb{S}<c)
and

c\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{\varphi_{b}(a)}_{c,\xi}\Gamma

holds.

Proof
. By induction on
a
. We may assume
b>0
. Let
\bar{\rho}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Consider the case when the last inference is a
(cut)
.
We have an
a_{0}<a
and a
\bigvee
-formula
C^{(\bar{\rho})}
with
C\simeq\bigvee(C_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
such that

\mathrm{rk}(C)<c+b
,

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta_{\iota},\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c+b,\xi}\Gamma,\lnot C_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})}

for each
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J
,
and
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{c+b,\xi}C^{(\bar{\rho})},\Gamma
.
IH yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{\varphi_{b}(a_{0})}_{c,\xi}\Gamma,\lnot C_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})}

and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{\varphi_{b}(a_{0})}_{c,\xi}C^{(\rho)},\Gamma
.
Let
c+b_{1}=\max\{c,\mathrm{rk}(C)\}<c+b
.
Then
b_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
by (
22
).
We obtain

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{\varphi_{b_{1}}(\varphi_{b}(a_{0})\cdot 2)}_{c,\xi}\Gamma
by
Reduction
5.3
, where

\varphi_{b_{1}}(\varphi_{b}(a_{0})\cdot 2)<\varphi_{b}(a)
.

\Box

Definition 5.5

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
and
\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.
Let
\rho>\kappa
be an ordinal such that
\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cap\rho\subset\kappa
and

\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}=\mathtt{Q}\cup\{(\mathbb{S},\kappa)\}

is a finite family.
Let
\bar{\rho}
be a cap over
\mathtt{Q}
, and
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
be a reduced formula for
\mathtt{Q}

such that
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\kappa}
.
Let
\Gamma
be a set of formulas such that
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\kappa}
for each
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma
.
Then let
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{(\kappa/\rho)}:\equiv A^{(\bar{\rho}[\kappa/\rho])}
, and

\Gamma^{(\kappa/\rho)}=\{(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{(\kappa/\rho)}:A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma\}
.

We see that
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{(\kappa/\rho)}
is reduced
for
\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}
if
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
is reduced for
\mathtt{Q}
and
\mathsf{k}(A)\subset M_{\kappa}
.

Lemma 5.6

(Recapping)

Let
a<\Lambda
, and

(\mathtt{Q},\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot})
be
a finite family with thresholds,
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
and

\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.
Suppose

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{\mathbb{S}+1,\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Pi,\Gamma
, where
\Pi(\rho)=\emptyset
and
\Gamma=\Gamma(\rho)
, cf. Definition
4.25
.
6
.

Let
b
be an ordinal
such that
\mathbb{S}+1\leq b<s(\rho)\leq\Lambda_{0}<\Lambda
and

\Gamma\subset\bigvee(b)

Assume that
b\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
and

\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
for
g=m(\rho)
.
Let

\kappa\in H_{\rho}(g_{1},\mathtt{Q},\Theta)
with
g_{1}=h^{b}(g;\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(a))
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\vdash^{\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(a)}_{\mathbb{S}+1,\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Pi,\Gamma^{(\kappa/\rho)}

(36)

holds.

Proof
. We show the lemma by induction on
a
.
We have
\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(a)<\Lambda
and
g_{1}:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
.
Let

\kappa\in H_{\rho}(g_{1},\mathtt{Q},\Theta)
.
By (
24
) and Definition
4.29
we have

\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\kappa)\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
,

\Theta\cap M_{\rho}\subset M_{\kappa}
and
\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cap\rho\subset\kappa
.
On the other hand we have

\{a,\Lambda_{0},b\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

by (
23
).

If
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
, then

\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{S})}=\kappa
.
Otherwise
\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{S})}=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
.
In each case we obtain

pd(\kappa;\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})<\kappa
iff
pd(\rho;\mathtt{Q})<\rho
, and

pd(\kappa;\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})=pd(\rho;\mathtt{Q})
in this case.
Moreover
pd(\tau;\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})=pd(\tau;\mathtt{Q})
for
\rho<\tau\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.
Also

\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}

by
\Theta\cap M_{\rho}\subset M_{\kappa}
.

Let
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma(\rho)
.
We obtain

\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho}[\kappa/\rho])};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
and

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho}[\kappa/\rho])};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})=\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
.
We obtain
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\subset M_{\kappa}
by
Lemma
3.50
.
1
.

\Theta(\mathtt{Q})=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}=\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})

yields

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho}[\kappa/\rho])};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})=\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
by (
23
).

Let
A^{(\bar{\tau})}\in\Pi
with
\tau=\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})\neq\rho
.
Then

\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})
.
We obtain

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})=\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
.
Therefore each of (
22
) and (
23
)
is enjoyed in (
36
).

Also
\{b,a,\Lambda_{0}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset M_{\kappa}
by
\{b,a,\Lambda_{0}\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

and
\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\kappa)
.
Hence

\mathrm{fld}(h^{b}(g;\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(a)))\subset M_{\kappa}
, cf. (
12
).
In the proof let us suppress the subscripts
\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}
.

) by (
46
) and (
45
)
with

\kappa=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{S})}
and
\mathrm{rk}(\Gamma)<b<\Lambda_{0}
.

Case 2
.
Second consider the case when the last inference
(\bigvee)
introduces a
\bigvee
-formula

A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma
:
Let
A\simeq\bigvee\left(A_{\iota}\right)_{\iota\in J}
.
We have an
\iota\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J
such that

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
,
and
for
A_{\iota}\simeq\bigwedge(B_{\nu})_{\nu\in I}
and
\nu\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]I
,

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{\mathbb{S}+1,\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}}\Pi,\Gamma,B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})}
holds.

Let
\bar{\rho}_{\kappa}=\bar{\rho}[\kappa/\rho]
and
\nu\in[\bar{\rho}_{\kappa}\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}]I
.
We obtain

\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\kappa})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})
.
This yields

\nu\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]I
and

\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\kappa})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\subset M_{\kappa}
.
We obtain

\kappa\in H_{\rho}(g_{1},\mathtt{Q},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q}))
.

Likewise we see that

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\kappa})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})=\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
.
IH
yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\kappa})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}),\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\vdash^{\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(a_{0})}_{\mathbb{S}+1,\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}}\Pi,\Gamma^{(\kappa/\rho)},B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho}_{\kappa})}
for
\nu\in[\bar{\rho}_{\kappa}\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}]I
and
\Gamma\cup\{B_{\nu}\}\subset\bigvee(b)
.
We obtain (
36
)
by a
(\bigvee)
.

Case 3
.
Third consider the case when the last inference
(\bigvee)
introduces a
\bigvee
-formula

A^{(\bar{\tau})}\in\Pi
with
\tau=\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})\neq\rho
:
Let
A\simeq\bigvee\left(A_{\iota}\right)_{\iota\in J}
.
We have an
\iota\in[\bar{\tau}\mathtt{Q}]J
such that

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}=\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
,
and
for
A_{\iota}\simeq\bigwedge(B_{\nu})_{\nu\in I}
and
\nu\in[\bar{\tau}\mathtt{Q}]I
,

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}),\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{\mathbb{S}+1,\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}}B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})},\Pi,\Gamma
holds.

We claim that

\mathrm{inv}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv\mathrm{inv}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})

(47)

We have
\rho\neq\tau=\bar{\tau}(\mathbb{S})
.
If
\tau<\rho
, then
\tau\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cap\rho\subset\kappa
, and

\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cap\tau=\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{S})\cap\tau
.
Hence

\mathrm{inv}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv\mathrm{inv}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})
.

Let
\tau>\rho
.
Then
\kappa<\rho\leq\lambda=pd(\tau;\mathtt{Q})
.
We may assume that

A_{\iota}\equiv C^{[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]}\not\equiv C
for a
C
.
Suppose

\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv A_{\iota}\equiv C^{[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]}
. Then
we would have

\mathsf{k}(C^{[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]})=\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\lambda}\cap\mathbb{S}=\lambda
.
Hence
\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv C
, and

\mathsf{k}(C)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
.
This yields
\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\equiv C
,

\iota\in[\bar{\tau}\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}]J

and

\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}}
.

Let
\nu\in[\bar{\tau}\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa}]I
.
We see as in (
47
) that

\mathrm{inv}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathrm{inv}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})
,

\nu\in[\bar{\tau}\mathtt{Q}]I
,

\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}^{\kappa})\subset M_{\kappa}
, and

\kappa\in H_{\rho}(g_{1},\mathtt{Q},\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(B_{\nu}^{(\bar{\tau})};\mathtt{Q}))
.

IH with (
47
) followed by a
(\bigvee)
yields (
36
).

Other cases are seen from IH.

\Box

5.2
Eliminations of inferences (rfl)

In this subsection, inferences
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,x,f))

are removed from operator controlled derivations of sequents of formulas in
\Sigma(\Omega)\cup\Pi(\Omega)
.

Lemma 5.7

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with thresholds
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
and
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
.
Assume that

\{\rho,\nu\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
for

\nu=b(\rho)
and
\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}
.
Let
g=m(\rho)
and

f:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
be a special finite function with
\Lambda\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

for which one of the following holds:

1.

\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
for a
\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}
, and if
f\neq\emptyset
, then
there exists
x\in\mathrm{supp}(g)
and
y<x
such that

(y,x)\cap\mathrm{supp}(g)=(y,x)\cap\mathrm{supp}(f)=\emptyset
,
f_{y}=g_{y}
,

f(y)=g(y)<g(y)+\tilde{\theta}_{x-y}(g(x))\cdot\omega
and
f<_{\Lambda}^{x}g(x)
, cf. Definition
3.31
.
6
.

2.

\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
for a
\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}
, and

f_{x}=g_{x}
and
f<_{\Lambda}^{x}g^{\prime}(x)
for an
x\in\mathrm{supp}(g)

3.

f=h^{b}(g;a)
for some

such that

and

.

Then there exists a
\sigma
such that
\sigma\in H_{\rho}(f,\mathtt{Q},\Theta)
,

m(\sigma)=f
,
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\sigma)=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
and

\{\sigma,b(\sigma)\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
.

Proof
.

.

.

.

, and

with

.

.
2
.
On the other hand we have

Finally let

and

.

5.7
.
2
.
If

and

for an

, then let

.
It is clear that

,

.

for

.

We have
\mathrm{fld}(h_{i})\subset\mathcal{H}_{0}[\{b,a,\Lambda\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g)]
.
We see

\mathrm{fld}(h_{i})\cup\{\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

from the assumption
\{b,a,\Lambda,\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(g)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

By Lemma
5.7
.
1
pick ordinals
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i\leq n}
recursively as follows.

\sigma_{n}=\rho
, and for
i<n
,
\sigma_{i}

,

,

,

and

.

,

and

.
Therefore

Definition 5.8

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family such that
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}
.

\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
denotes the restriction of
\mathtt{Q}
to
\mathbb{S}
.

1.

For a cap
\bar{\rho}\in\prod_{\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})}\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})
over
\mathtt{Q}
,

\bar{\rho}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}\in\prod_{\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})}\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})
denotes the restriction of
\bar{\rho}

to
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S})
.

2.

For a capped formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
over
\mathtt{Q}
such that
\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
,

(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q})
denotes a capped formula over

\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
defined as follows.
Let
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
.

(a)

If
\mathrm{cp}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathbb{S}
, then

A\equiv B^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\not\equiv B\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
and
\mathsf{k}(B)\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\rho}
for

\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})>pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S});\mathtt{Q})=\sigma\geq\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}=\rho
.
In this case let

(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q}):\equiv(B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})}
.

(b)

If
\mathrm{cp}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}
, then

A\equiv B^{[\sigma/\mathbb{T}]}\not\equiv B\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
and
\mathsf{k}(B)\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\rho}
for

\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T})>pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T});\mathtt{Q})=\sigma\geq\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}
.
In this case let

(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q}):\equiv((B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{[\sigma/\mathbb{T}]})^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})}
.

(c)

Otherwise let

(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q}):\equiv(A^{[\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})}
.

3.

For a set
\Gamma
of capped formulas over
\mathtt{Q}
such that
\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
for each
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma
, let

\Gamma^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q})=\{(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q}):A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma\}
.

We have
\rho\in\bigcap_{\mathbb{U}<\mathbb{S}}M_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{U})}
for
\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.
We obtain
\mathsf{k}(A^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S}}
in Definition
5.8
.
2c
.
In Definition
5.8
.
2b
we have
\mathsf{k}(B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset M_{\sigma}
.

Main Lemma 5.9

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with thresholds
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
.
Let

\Gamma
be a set of reduced
\bigvee
-formulas.
Let

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
for
a<\Lambda
.
Assume that

s(\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})})\leq\mathbb{S}+1
and

\{\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})},b(\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})})\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

for
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}+\mathbb{S}
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}},\Theta\cup\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S}),\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S})\vdash^{\hat{a}}_{\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q})

(48)

holds for
\hat{a}=f(\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})},a)=\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}+\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}}(a)
.

Proof
. By main induction on
\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
with subsidiary induction on
a
.
Let
\mathtt{R}
denote the restriction
\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
of
\mathtt{Q}
to
\mathbb{S}
, and

\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}=\Theta\cup\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.
We write
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}
for
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}(\mathtt{Q})
when
\mathtt{Q}
is fixed.

\hat{a}<\Lambda
holds.
We have

\{a,\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{S}\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
by (
23
), and
\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}

by (
24
).
On the other hand we have

\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

by the assumption.
Hence

\hat{a}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]
for (
23
).
We have
\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
by (
24
).
We obtain
\gamma_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]
for (
23
).

We have

\gamma_{\mathbb{U}}^{\mathtt{Q}}<\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}+\mathbb{S}<\gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathtt{Q}}
for every

\{\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}\leq\mathbb{U}\}\subset\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})

by Definition
4.23
.
2
.
Hence
(
24
) is enjoyed in (
48
).

Claim 5.10

Each of (
21
),
(
22
) and (
23
) is enjoyed in (
48
).

Proof
of Claim
5.10
.
Let
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma
.
We show first
\mathsf{k}((A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]};\mathtt{R})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta(\mathtt{R})]\cap M_{\mathtt{R}}
.
We have
\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Let
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
.

Consider first the case in Definition
5.8
.
2a
.
Let
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}\leq\sigma=pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S});\mathtt{Q})<\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})
.
Then we have
A\equiv B^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\not\equiv B
and

B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}((A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]};\mathtt{R})\not\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv B
.
By
\mathsf{k}(B)=\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}
,
we obtain
\mathsf{k}(B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})=\mathsf{k}((B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})};\mathtt{R})=\mathsf{k}((A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]};\mathtt{R})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]

by Proposition
3.53
.
4
,
\gamma_{1}\geq\mathbb{S}
and

\rho\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]
.
On the other hand we have
\mathsf{k}(B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset M_{\mathtt{R}}
by
\rho\in M_{\mathtt{R}}
and Lemma
3.51
.

Next consider the case in Definition
5.8
.
2b
.
Let
\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\sigma=pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T});\mathtt{Q})<\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{T})
with
\mathbb{T}<\mathbb{S}
.
Then we have
A\equiv B^{[\sigma/\mathbb{T}]}\not\equiv B
and

B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}((A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]};\mathtt{R})\not\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\equiv B
.
As in the first case we see that

\mathsf{k}(B^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]\cap M_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Finally let
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}\equiv(A^{[\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})}
in Definition
5.8
.
2c
.
Then
A^{[\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})/\mathbb{S}]}\equiv\mathrm{inv}^{(\mathtt{u})}((A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]};\mathtt{R})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]\cap M_{\mathtt{R}}
is seen as in the first case from
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})\in\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})
.

Let
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}\equiv B^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{S})}
.
Obviously the cap
\bar{\rho}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
is reduced for
\mathtt{R}
.
We have

\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S};\mathtt{R})=\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})
and

\mathrm{rk}(B)\leq\mathrm{rk}(A)
if
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})<\mathbb{S}
.
If
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})\geq\mathbb{S}
, then
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S};\mathtt{R})=\Lambda_{0}
.
Hence

(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}
is reduced for
\mathtt{R}
.

\Box
of Claim
5.10
.

In the proof let us omit the subscripts
\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}
in
\vdash^{a}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}
.

Case 1
. First the last inference is a
(\bigvee)
introducing an
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma
:
Let

A\simeq\bigvee(B_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
and
B_{\iota}\simeq\bigwedge(A_{\nu})_{\nu\in J_{\iota}}
.
Let

\iota\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J
be such that

\mathsf{k}(B_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}

for which there is an
a_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap a
such that

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta_{\nu},\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a_{0}}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}}\Pi,\Gamma,A_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})}
holds for every

, where

\Theta_{\nu}=\Theta\cup\mathsf{k}(A_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})
and

\mathsf{k}(A_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Let
\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}}=\bar{\rho}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
.
Let
\widehat{a_{0}}=\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}+\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}}(a_{0})
.

There are three cases for the formula
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}
.
Let
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
.

Case 1.1
. The case in Definition
5.8
.
2a
:
Let
\mathrm{cp}(\bar{\rho};\mathtt{Q})=\mathbb{S}
and
\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S})>pd(\bar{\rho}(\mathbb{S});\mathtt{Q})=\sigma\geq\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}=\rho
.
We have
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}\equiv(C^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}})}
for a
C
such that

A\equiv C^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
and
\mathsf{k}(C)\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
.
Let
C\simeq\bigvee(C_{\mu})_{\mu\in I}
. Then
A\equiv C^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigvee(C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})_{\mu\in[\sigma]I}
and

C^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigvee(C_{\mu}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})_{\mu\in[\rho]I}
.
Let
\mu\in I
be such that
B_{\iota}\equiv C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
for
\iota=\mu^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
.
We have

\mathsf{k}(C_{\mu})=\mathsf{k}(B_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\rho}
.
We see

\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}})};\mathtt{R})=\mathsf{k}(C_{\mu}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]\cap M_{\mathtt{R}}
from
\rho\in M_{\mathtt{R}}
as in Claim
5.10
.

Let
C_{\mu}\simeq\bigwedge(D_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in K}
.
We obtain
(B_{\iota}^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}\equiv(C_{\mu}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}})}
,

C_{\mu}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigwedge(D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})_{\alpha\in[\rho]K}
,
and
B_{\iota}\equiv C_{\mu}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}\simeq\bigwedge(D_{\alpha}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})_{\alpha\in[\sigma]K}
.
Moreover
\mathsf{k}((D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}})};\mathtt{R})=\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})
and

\mathsf{k}((D_{\alpha}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha})
.

Claim 5.11

Let
\alpha\in[\rho]K
be such that
\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset M_{\mathtt{R}}
.
Let
\nu=\alpha^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
,
A_{\nu}\equiv D_{\alpha}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]}
,
and
\Theta_{\mathbb{S},\nu}=\Theta\cup\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})\cup\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})
.
Then
\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}.\nu}(\mathtt{R})]\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}

and

\nu\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J_{\iota}
.

Proof
of Claim
5.11
.
We have
\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha})\subset M_{\rho}\subset M_{\sigma}
.
We obtain
(A_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}\equiv(D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}})}
and

\mathsf{k}(A_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha})\subset M_{\mathtt{R}}\cap M_{\rho}=M_{\mathtt{Q}}

by
\rho=\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{S})}
and Lemma
3.51
.
Hence
\nu\in[\bar{\rho}\mathtt{Q}]J_{\iota}
.
On the other hand
we have
\max\{\mathbb{S},\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)\}\leq\gamma_{1}

\{\rho\}\cup\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S},\nu}(\mathtt{R})]
.
Lemma
3.55
yields

\mathsf{k}(A_{\nu}^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})=\mathsf{k}(D_{\alpha})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S},\nu}(\mathtt{R})]
.

\Box
of Claim
5.11
.

SIH with Claim

yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}},\Theta_{\mathbb{S},\nu},\mathtt{R})\vdash^{\widehat{a_{0}}}_{\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{S}]},((D_{\alpha}^{[\sigma/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{S}]}

such that

, where

On the other hand we have

\mathsf{k}((C_{\mu}^{[\rho/\mathbb{S}]})^{(\bar{\rho}^{\mathbb{S}})};\mathtt{R})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathtt{R})]\cap M_{\mathtt{R}}

A
(\bigvee)
yields (

.
Moreover if
\mathbb{W}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
and
\gamma<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{W}}
, then

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{W}}\leq\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)
holds.

Proof
. Let
\mathtt{s}=\mathtt{s}(\gamma,\mathtt{Q})
.
If
[\mathbb{W},\mathtt{s})\cap\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\emptyset
, then
\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)=\delta_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)=\gamma
, and

\mathtt{s}\leq\mathbb{V}
. Then

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{V}}\leq\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathtt{s}}\leq\gamma
by Definition
4.23
.
2
.

Otherwise
\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)=\max\{\gamma,\gamma_{\mathbb{U}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\}
for
the least
\mathbb{U}\in[\mathbb{W},\mathtt{s})\cap\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
If
\mathbb{S}\leq\mathbb{U}
, then
\delta_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)=\max\{\gamma,\gamma_{\mathbb{U}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\}
and

\gamma_{\mathbb{V}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\leq\gamma_{\mathbb{U}}^{\mathtt{Q}}
by
\mathbb{U}\leq\mathbb{V}
.
Assume
\mathbb{U}<\mathbb{S}
.
If
[\mathbb{S},\mathtt{s})\cap\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\emptyset
, then
\mathtt{s}\leq\mathbb{V}
and

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{V}}\leq\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathtt{s}}\leq\delta_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)=\gamma\leq\max\{\gamma,\gamma_{\mathbb{U}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\}
.
Otherwise let
\delta_{\mathbb{S}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)=\max\{\gamma,\gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\}
for
the least
\mathbb{T}\in[\mathbb{S},\mathtt{s})\cap\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
Then
\mathbb{U}<\mathbb{T}\leq\mathbb{V}
, and

\gamma_{\mathbb{V}}^{\mathtt{Q}}\leq\gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathtt{Q}}<\gamma_{\mathbb{U}}^{\mathtt{Q}}
.

\Box

Definition 5.18

Let
\mathtt{Q}
be a finite family with thresholds
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
,
\mathbb{W}
a successor stable ordinal, and

\gamma<\gamma_{0}
and
\alpha
ordinals.

\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathtt{Q},\alpha)
denotes an ordinal defined by

\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathtt{Q},\alpha)=\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)+\Lambda\cdot\alpha
.

Lemma 5.19

(Elimination of stable ordinals)

Let
\xi\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
be a stable ordinal, and

\mathtt{Q}

a finite family with thresholds
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
with
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\subset\xi
.
Suppose

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{\xi,\xi,\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
,
where
a<\Lambda
,
\xi\leq\Lambda_{0}
,
\Gamma
is a set of reduced
\bigvee
-formulas and

\mathbb{W}\leq\xi
is a successor stable ordinal
such that

\mathbb{W}\in{\cal H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
.

Let
f(\xi,a)=\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}+\xi}(a)
and

\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}=\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathtt{Q},f(\xi,a))
denote the ordinal in Definition
5.18
.
Assume that
\mathrm{fld}(m(\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})}))\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
,

\{\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})},b(\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T})})\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathtt{Q}}+\mathbb{T}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
for every

\mathbb{W}\leq\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
, and

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
and
\gamma
have gaps
\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a))
.

Let
\Theta_{\mathbb{W}*}=\Theta\cup\bigcup\{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T}):\mathbb{W}\leq\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\}
,

\{\mathbb{T}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}):\mathbb{T}\geq\mathbb{W}\}=\{\mathbb{T}_{0}>\mathbb{T}_{1}>\cdots>\mathbb{T}_{n-1}\}
, and

\mathbb{T}_{-1}=\Lambda_{0}
.

Let
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
be a formula such that
\mathsf{k}(A^{(\bar{\rho})};\mathtt{Q})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}}
,
and
\kappa_{i}\leq\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T}_{i})}\,(0\leq i<n)
be ordinals such that

\mathtt{p}_{0}(\kappa_{i})=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho_{\mathtt{Q}(\mathbb{T}_{i})})
,

\{\kappa_{i},b(\kappa_{i})\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}_{i}}+\mathbb{T}_{i}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]

s(\kappa_{i})\leq\mathbb{T}_{i}+1
,
and

.
Let
\mathtt{Q}_{i}=(\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{T}_{i}^{\dagger})\cup\{(\mathbb{T}_{i},\kappa_{i})\}

A_{i}\equiv B_{i}^{(\bar{\rho}\,\!\upharpoonright\!\,\mathbb{T}_{i-1})}
denote capped formulas defined recursively as follows:

and
A_{i+1}\equiv A_{i}^{[\mathbb{T}_{i}]}(\mathtt{Q}_{i})
.
Then
let
(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{W}*]}:\equiv A_{n}
.
Let

\Gamma^{[\mathbb{W}*]}=\{(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{W}*]}:A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma\}
.

Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}},\Theta_{\mathbb{W}*},\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{W})\vdash^{f(\xi,a)}_{\mathbb{W},\mathbb{W},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{W}*]}

(52)

holds.

Proof
. By main induction on
\xi
with subsidiary induction on
a
.
In the proof let us omit the subscripts
\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}
.

By the assumption, (
23
) and (
24
) we have

\{\mathbb{W},\gamma,a,\xi,\Lambda_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
,

\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q})]
for
\mathbb{W}\leq\mathbb{U}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})

and
\max\{\gamma,\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}\}\leq\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}
by Proposition
5.17
.
On the other hand we have
\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}}\subset M_{\rho(\mathbb{T})}
for
\mathbb{T}\geq\mathbb{W}
.
Hence

\{\mathbb{W},f(\xi,a),\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}}[\Theta(\mathtt{Q}_{\mathbb{W}})]
follows, and
(
23
) is enjoyed in
(
52
).

We see
\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}

for every
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\cap\mathbb{W}
as follows.
We have
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{W}<\xi
. We obtain
\gamma+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a))<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}
by (
24
)
with gaps, and
we are done if
\delta^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{W}}(\gamma)=\gamma
.
Let
\delta^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{W}}(\gamma)=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}
for
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{W}\leq\mathbb{U}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
.
Then
\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a))<\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{S}}

since
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\cdot}
has gaps
\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a))
.
On the other hand we have
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)<\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}

for

by Proposition
5.17
.
Hence (
24
) is enjoyed.

Let
A^{(\bar{\rho})}\in\Gamma
.

(A^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{W}*]}
is a capped formula over

.
We see as in Claim

Claim 5.21

Case 3.1
.

) follows
for
\gamma_{2}=\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathtt{P},f(\xi,a_{0}))=\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{P}}(\gamma)+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a_{0}))<\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}
by Claim
5.21
.

Case 3.2
.
\xi>\mathbb{S}\geq\mathbb{W}
:
We have

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{2}},\Theta_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}*},\mathtt{P}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}^{\dagger})\vdash^{a_{1}}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger},\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}*]}(\mathtt{P})

by SIH.

Lemma
5.14

yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}},\Theta_{\mathbb{S}*},\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S})\vdash^{a_{2}}_{\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{S}*]}
, where
\mathtt{P}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}=\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
,

\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}
and
\Theta_{\mathbb{S}*}=\Theta_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}*}

with
\mathtt{P}(\mathbb{S})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{1}}[\Theta_{\mathbb{S}*}(\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S})]
.

Claim 5.22

\gamma_{3}=\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{R}}(\gamma_{1})+\Lambda\cdot f(\mathbb{S},a_{2})<\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)+\Lambda\cdot f(\xi,a)=\gamma_{\mathbb{W}}
for
\mathtt{R}=\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{S}
.

Proof
of Claim
5.22
.
Let
\mathtt{s}=\mathtt{s}(\gamma,\mathtt{Q})
,
\delta=\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{Q}}(\gamma)
,

\mathtt{s}_{1}=\mathtt{s}(\gamma_{1},\mathtt{R})
and
\delta_{1}=\delta_{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathtt{R}}(\gamma_{1})

with
\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}

By

we obtain
\mathtt{s}_{1}\leq\mathbb{S}
.

We have
\mathbb{S}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{P})
,
and

by (

).
Hence
\mathbb{S}<\mathtt{s}
.

First let

with the least

.

.

.
If
\mathbb{V}<\mathbb{S}

\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}\leq\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{V}}
.

f(\mathbb{S},a_{2})<f(\xi,a)
with
\mathbb{S}<\xi<\Lambda
yields the claim.
Let
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{V}

\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{V}}+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a_{0})+1)
.
We obtain

by
\mathbb{S}<\xi
and
a_{2}<f(\xi,a)
.

Next let
\delta=\gamma
with
[\mathbb{W},\mathtt{s})\cap\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\emptyset

Then
[\mathbb{W},\mathtt{s}_{1})\cap\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{P})=\emptyset
by

, and

\delta_{1}=\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{\mathtt{P}}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathbb{S}=\max\{\gamma,\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}_{1}}\}+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a_{0})+1)+\mathbb{S}
,
where
\mathbb{U}_{1}=\min\{\mathbb{U}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}):\mathbb{U}>\mathbb{S}\}
if such a
\mathbb{U}
exists, and
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}_{1}}=0
else.
Let
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{U}\in\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})
. Then
\mathtt{s}\leq\mathbb{U}
and
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{U}}=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathtt{s}}\leq\gamma
.
We obtain

\delta_{1}=\gamma+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a_{0})+1)+\mathbb{S}
, and

\delta_{1}+\Lambda\cdot f(\mathbb{S},a_{2})=\gamma+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a_{0})+1)+\mathbb{S}+\Lambda\cdot f(\mathbb{S},a_{2})=\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}}_{\mathbb{V}}+\Lambda\cdot(f(\xi,a_{0})+f(\mathbb{S},a_{2}))<\gamma+\Lambda\cdot f(\xi,a)=\delta+\Lambda\cdot f(\xi,a)
.

\Box
of Claim

.

MIH with
\mathbb{S}<\xi
and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{3}},\Theta_{\mathbb{W}*},\mathtt{Q}\!\upharpoonright\!\mathbb{W})\vdash^{f(\mathbb{S},a_{2})}_{\mathbb{W},\mathbb{W}}\Gamma^{[\mathbb{W}*]}
, where

by Claim

.
(

) follows.

Case 4
. Fourth the last inference is a
(cut)

\bar{\rho}=\bar{\rho}_{\mathtt{Q}}
.

and a

.

\mathrm{rk}((C^{(\bar{\rho})})^{[\mathbb{W}*]})<\xi\leq\Lambda_{0}
.

5.3
yields (

) for
\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(a_{1})<\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}+\xi}(a)=f(\xi,a)
.

The case when the last inference is a

(\Sigma(\Omega){\rm-rfl})

is seen from SIH.

\Box

5.3
Uncapped calculus

Let
\mathbb{S}_{0}=\Omega^{\dagger}
be the least stable ordinal.
Since there is no stable ordinal below
\mathbb{S}_{0}
,
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})\subset\mathbb{S}_{0}
means that

\mathtt{Q}
is the empty family with
\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q})=\emptyset
.
Let
\mathtt{Q}_{0}
denote the empty family.
In a witnessed derivation of the fact
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q}_{0})\vdash^{a}_{\mathbb{S}_{0},\mathbb{S}_{0},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
,
there occurs no
inference
(i{\rm-rfl}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho,x,f))

nor
(\mathrm{dom})
,
cf. Definition
4.30
.
Hence each formula
A^{(\bar{\rho})}
occurring in it is uncapped with the empty list
\bar{\rho}=\emptyset
.
Let us write
A^{(\mathtt{u})}
for
A^{(\emptyset)}
in this subsection.

We see from Definition
4.28
.
1
that
\mathrm{inv}(A^{(\mathtt{u})};\mathtt{Q}_{0})\equiv A^{(\mathtt{u})}
,

\mathsf{k}(A^{(\mathtt{u})};\mathtt{Q}_{0})=\mathsf{k}(A)
and

\iota\in[\mathtt{u}\mathtt{Q}_{0}]J
iff
\mathsf{k}(A_{\iota})\subset M_{\mathtt{Q}_{0}}
for

A\simeq\bigvee(A_{\iota})_{\iota\in J}
.
By (
22
)
we have
\mathsf{k}(\Gamma)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta]
, where
\Theta=\Theta\cap M_{\mathtt{Q}_{0}}=\Theta(\mathtt{Q}_{0})
, and

\{\gamma,a,c,\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}[\Theta)]

by (
23
).

In this subsection we write

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta)\vdash^{a}_{c}\Gamma^{(\mathtt{u})}

for
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q}_{0})\vdash^{a}_{c,\mathbb{S}_{0},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma^{(\mathtt{u})}

for the empty family
\mathtt{Q}_{0}
.

Lemma 5.23

(Cut-elimination)

1.

Assume
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta)\vdash^{a}_{\Omega+1+b}\Gamma^{(\mathtt{u})}
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta)\vdash^{\varphi_{b}(a)}_{\Omega+1}\Gamma^{(\mathtt{u})}
.

2.

Assume
(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta)\vdash^{a}_{b}\Gamma^{(\mathtt{u})}
with
b<\Omega
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta)\vdash^{\varphi_{b}(a)}_{0}\Gamma^{(\mathtt{u})}

holds.

Proof
. Each is seen by main induction on
b
with subsidiary induction on
a
.

\Box

Lemma 5.24

(Collapsing)

Let

\Gamma\subset\Sigma(\mathcal{L}_{0}:\Omega)
be a set of formulas.
Suppose

\Theta\subset C_{\gamma+1}(\psi_{\Omega}(\gamma+1))

and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta)\vdash^{a}_{\Omega+1}\Gamma
.
Let

\beta=\psi_{\Omega}(\hat{a})
with
\hat{a}=\gamma+\omega^{a}
.
Then

(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{a}},\Theta)\vdash^{\beta}_{\beta}\Gamma^{(\beta,\Omega)}
holds.

Proof
. By induction on
a
as in
[
9
]
.

\Box

Let us prove Theorem
1.1
.
Let

S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\vdash\theta^{L_{\Omega}}
for a
\Sigma
-sentence
\theta
.
By Embedding
4.16
pick an
m>0
so that

(\mathcal{H}_{0},\emptyset;\emptyset)\vdash_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1+m,\gamma_{0}}^{*\mathbb{I}_{N}\cdot 2+m}\theta^{L_{\Omega}}
.
Cut-elimination
4.20
yields

(\mathcal{H}_{0},\emptyset;\emptyset)\vdash_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1,\gamma_{0}}^{*a}\theta^{L_{\Omega}}

for
a=\omega_{m}(\mathbb{I}_{N}\cdot 2+m)<\omega_{m+1}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)
.
Then Collapsing
4.22
yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{a}},\emptyset;\emptyset)\vdash_{\beta,\gamma_{0}}^{*\beta}\theta^{L_{\Omega}}

for
\beta=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\hat{a})\in LSt_{N}
with

\hat{a}=\omega^{a}=\omega_{m+1}(\mathbb{I}_{N}\cdot 2+m)>\beta
.

Now let
\gamma_{0}=\omega_{m+2}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)
, and

\Lambda_{0}:=\beta<\Lambda=\Gamma(\Lambda_{0})<\mathbb{I}_{N}
be the next strongly critical number
as the base of the
\tilde{\theta}
-function.
Capping
5.1
then yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{a}},\emptyset,\mathtt{Q}_{0})\vdash_{\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}^{\Lambda_{0}\cdot 2}(\theta^{L_{\Omega}})^{(\mathtt{u})}

where
c=\xi=\Lambda_{0}
,
\mathtt{u}
denotes the empty cap,

\mathtt{Q}_{0}
is an empty family for
(\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0})
with thresholds such that

\mathrm{dom}(\mathtt{Q}_{0})=\emptyset
, and
\gamma^{\mathtt{Q}_{0}}_{\cdot}
together with
\hat{a}
have gaps

\Lambda\cdot F(\Lambda_{0})<\Lambda^{2}<\mathbb{I}_{N}
with

F(\Lambda_{0})=f(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}\cdot 2)=\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}\cdot 2}(\Lambda_{0}\cdot 2)<\Lambda
.
For the empty family
\mathtt{Q}_{0}
this means that

\hat{a}>\Lambda\cdot F(\Lambda_{0})
.

In what follows each finite function is an
f:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
.
Let

\alpha=F(\Lambda_{0})
and the least stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}_{0}=\Omega^{\dagger}
.

By Lemma
5.19
we obtain

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}},\emptyset,\mathtt{Q}_{0})\vdash_{\mathbb{S}_{0},\mathbb{S}_{0},\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}^{\alpha}(\theta^{L_{\Omega}})^{(\mathtt{u})}
, i.e.,

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}},\emptyset)\vdash_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\alpha}(\theta^{L_{\Omega}})^{(\mathtt{u})}
, where

\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}=\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\mathtt{Q}_{0},\alpha)=\delta_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\mathtt{Q}_{0}}(\hat{a})+\Lambda\cdot F(\Lambda_{0})=\hat{a}+\Lambda\cdot\alpha<\hat{a}+\Lambda^{2}<\gamma_{0}
.

Cut-elimination
5.23
.
1
yields

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}},\emptyset)\vdash_{\Omega+1}^{\alpha}(\theta^{L_{\Omega}})^{(\mathtt{u})}
for
\alpha=\varphi_{\Lambda_{0}}(\alpha)
.

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\emptyset)\vdash_{\delta}^{\delta}(\theta^{L_{\delta}})^{(\mathtt{u})}
follows from
Collapsing
5.24

for

\gamma=\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}+\alpha
and

\delta=\psi_{\Omega}(\gamma_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}+\alpha)
with the epsilon
number
\alpha
.
We obtain

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\emptyset)\vdash_{0}^{\varphi_{\delta}(\delta)}(\theta^{L_{\delta}})^{(\mathtt{u})}

by Cut-elimination
5.23
.
2
.

We see that
\theta^{L_{\delta}}
is true by induction up to
\varphi_{\delta}(\delta)
,
where
\delta<\psi_{\Omega}(\omega_{m+2}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1))<\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
.

This completes a proof of Theorem
1.1
assuming the well-foundedness of
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

6
Some ordinals in well-foundedness proof

In this section we introduce some ordinals needed in our well-foundedness proof.

In
[
4
]
the following Lemmas
6.2
and
6.3
are shown.
Lemma
6.2
is used in showing the finiteness of the sequence

\rho_{0}\succ\rho_{1}\succ\rho_{2}\succ\cdots
,
cf. Definition
3.28
and Lemma
6.15
.
Lemma
6.3
is needed in showing
Corollary
7.38
.

Definition 6.1

Let
\Lambda\leq\mathbb{I}_{N}
be a strongly critical number.

1.

For
\xi<\Gamma(\Lambda)
,

a_{\Lambda}(\xi)
denotes an ordinal defined recursively by

a_{\Lambda}(0)=0
, and

a_{\Lambda}(\xi)=\sum_{i\leq m}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{i}}(\omega\cdot a_{\Lambda}(\xi_{i});\Lambda)

when
\xi=_{NF}\sum_{i\leq m}\tilde{\theta}_{b_{i}}(\xi_{i};\Lambda)
in (
6
).

2.

For irreducible functions
f:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
with base
\Lambda

let us associate ordinals
o_{\Lambda}(f)<\Gamma(\Lambda)
as follows.

o_{\Lambda}(\emptyset)=0
for the empty function
f=\emptyset
.
Let
\{0\}\cup{\rm supp}(f)=\{0=c_{0}<c_{1}<\cdots<c_{n}\}
,

f(c_{i})=\xi_{i}<\varphi_{\Lambda}(0)
for
i>0
, and
\xi_{0}=0
.
Define ordinals
\zeta_{i}=o_{\Lambda}(f;c_{i})
by

\zeta_{n}=\omega\cdot a_{\Lambda}(\xi_{n})
, and

\zeta_{i}=\omega\cdot a_{\Lambda}(\xi_{i})+\tilde{\theta}_{c_{i+1}-c_{i}}(\zeta_{i+1}+1;\Lambda)
.
Finally let
o_{\Lambda}(f)=\zeta_{0}=o_{\Lambda}(f;c_{0})
.

3.

For
d\not\in\{0\}\cup{\rm supp}(f)
, let

o_{\Lambda}(f;d)=0
if
f^{d}=\emptyset
.
Otherwise
o_{\Lambda}(f;d)=\tilde{\theta}_{c-d}(o_{\Lambda}(f;c)+1;\Lambda)
for

c=\min({\rm supp}(f^{d}))
.

Lemma 6.2

Let
f:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
be an irreducible finite function with base
\Lambda

defined from an irreducible function
g:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
and ordinals
c,d

as follows.

f_{c}=g_{c}
,

c<d\in{\rm supp}(g)
with

(c,d)\cap{\rm supp}(g)=(c,d)\cap{\rm supp}(f)=\emptyset
,

f(c)<g(c)+\tilde{\theta}_{d-c}(g(d);\Lambda)\cdot\omega
, and

f<_{\Lambda}^{d}g(d)
, cf. Definition
3.31
.
6
.
Then
o_{\Lambda}(f)<o_{\Lambda}(g)
holds.

Lemma 6.3

For irreducible finite functions
f,g:\Lambda\to\Gamma(\Lambda)
with base
\Lambda
,
assume

f<_{lx}^{0}g
.
Then
o_{\Lambda}(f)<o_{\Lambda}(g)
holds.

6.1
A preview of well-foundedness proof

To prove the well-foundedness of a computable notation system,
we utilize
the distinguished class introduced by W. Buchholz
[
7
]
.
Also cf.
[
11
]
for a well-foundedness in terms of a maximal distinguished class.

Let
OT
be a computable notation system of ordinals with an ordinal term
\Omega_{1}
.

\Omega_{1}
denotes the least recursively regular ordinal
\omega_{1}^{CK}
.
Assume that
the well-founded part
W(OT)
of
OT
exists as a set.
A parameter-free
\Pi^{1-}_{1}\mathrm{+CA}
suffices to show the existence.
Then the well-foundedness of such a notation system
OT
is provable.
When the next recursively regular ordinal
\Omega_{2}
is in
OT
,
we further assume that
a well-founded part
W(\mathcal{C}^{\Omega_{1}}(W_{0}))
of a set
\mathcal{C}^{\Omega_{1}}(W_{0})
exists,
where
W_{0}=W(OT)\cap\Omega_{1}
, and

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\Omega_{1}}(W_{0})
iff
each component
<\!\!\Omega_{1}
of
\alpha
is in
W_{0}
.
Likewise when
OT
contains
\alpha
-many terms denoting increasing sequence of
recursively regular ordinals,
we need to iterate the process of defining the well-founded parts
\alpha
-times.

Let us consider a notation system
OT
for recursively inaccessible universes.
There are
\alpha
-many ordinal terms denoting recursively regular ordinals in
OT

with the order type
\alpha
of
OT
.
The whole process then should be internalized.
We need to specify a feature of sets arising in the process.
Then
distinguished sets
emerge.
D[P]
denotes the fact that
P
is a distinguished class and
defined by

D[P]:\Leftrightarrow\forall\alpha\left(\alpha\leq P\to W(C^{\alpha}(P))\cap\alpha^{+}=P\cap\alpha^{+}\right)

where
\alpha\leq P\Leftrightarrow\exists\beta\in P(\alpha\leq\beta)
and

\alpha^{+}
denotes the next recursively regular ordinal above
\alpha
if such an ordinal exists.

W_{0}=W(OT)\cap\Omega_{1}
is the smallest distinguished set, and
W_{1}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\Omega_{1}}(W_{0}))\cap\Omega_{2}

is the next one.
Given two distinguished sets, it turns out that one is an initial segment of the other,
and the union
\mathcal{W}_{0}=\bigcup\{P\subset OT:D[P]\}
of all distinguished sets is distinguished, the
maximal distinguished class
.
The maximal distinguished class
\mathcal{W}_{0}
is
\Sigma^{1-}_{2}
-definable, and a proper class
without assuming
\Sigma^{1-}_{2}\mathrm{-CA}
.

Assuming the maximal distinguished class
\mathcal{W}_{0}
exists as a set,
the well-foundedness of
OT
for a single stable ordinal is provable in
[
4
]
.
Consider now a notation system
OT
for several stable ordinals
\mathbb{S}_{0},\mathbb{S}_{1},\ldots
.
We then need several maximal distinguished sets

\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{1},\ldots
to prove the well-foundedness.

\mathcal{W}_{0}
is the maximal distinguished set in an absolute sense.

A moment reflection on the emergence of distinguished sets shows that

\mathcal{W}_{1}
could be a
maximal distinguished set relative to

\mathcal{W}_{0}

and
\mathbb{S}_{0}
.
Specifically cf. (
56
),
a set
P
is said to be a
0
-
distinguished set
for
\gamma
and
X
, denoted by

D^{\gamma}[P;X]
, iff
P
is well-founded and

P\cap\gamma^{-\dagger}=X\cap\gamma^{-\dagger}\,\&\,\forall\alpha\geq\gamma^{-\dagger}\left(\alpha\leq P\to W(C^{\alpha}(P))\cap\alpha^{+}=P\cap\alpha^{+}\right)

where

\gamma^{-\dagger}=\max\{\mathbb{S}\in St\cup\{0\}:\mathbb{S}\leq\gamma\}
.
Then let, cf. (
57
)

W_{1}^{\gamma}(X):=\bigcup\{P\subset OT:D^{\gamma}[P;X]\}.

Observe that
W_{1}^{\gamma}(X)
is a
\Sigma^{1}_{2}
-definable class, and hence a set
assuming
\Sigma^{1}_{2}\mathrm{-CA}
.
We see in Lemma
7.8
.
2
that
W_{1}^{\gamma}(X)
is the maximal

0
-distinguished class for
\gamma
and
X
provided that
X\cap\gamma^{-\dagger}
is well-founded.

Assume that there are
\alpha
-many stable ordinals with the order type
\alpha
of
a notation system
OT
of ordinals.
Then we have to introduce distinguished sets in the next level.
In the higher level the recursive regularity is replaced by the stability, and
the
\Pi^{1}_{1}
-sets
W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P))
by
\Sigma^{1}_{2}
-sets
\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\gamma}(X)
.

A set
X
is a
1
-
distinguished set
, denoted by
D_{1}[X]
iff
X
is well-founded and

\forall\gamma\left(\gamma\leq X\to\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger}\right).

where
\alpha^{\dagger}=\min\{\mathbb{S}\in St:\alpha<\mathbb{S}\}
if such a stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
exists.
We see that
\mathcal{W}_{0}=W_{1}^{0}(\emptyset)
is the smallest
1
-distinguished set, and

\mathcal{W}_{1}=W_{1}^{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\mathcal{W}_{0})
is the next
1
-distinguished set, and so forth.
In Lemma
7.10
it is shown that if
D_{1}[X]
and
\gamma\in X
, then

X
is a
0
-distinguished set for
\gamma
and
X
, i.e.,
D^{\gamma}[X;X]
, and

\gamma\in W(\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(X))\cap\gamma^{+}=X\cap\gamma^{+}
, where

\gamma\in W_{1}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger}
.
This crucial lemma allows us to prove facts by going down to the lowest level, i.e., to the well-foundedness.

\mathcal{W}:=\bigcup\{X\subset OT:D_{1}[X]\}
is the
1
-maximal distinguished class, which is a

\Sigma^{1-}_{3}
-definable class.
Although
\mathcal{W}
is a proper class in a set theory with
\Pi_{1}
-Collection or
equivalently in
\Sigma^{1}_{3}\mathrm{-DC+BI}
, the theories proves that
if
\mathbb{S}\in\mathcal{W}
for
\mathbb{S}\in St\cup\{0\}
, then

\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}\in\mathcal{W}
, cf. Lemma
7.20
.
In showing that a limit of stable ordinals is in
\mathcal{W}
, we invoke

\Sigma^{1}_{3}\mathrm{-DC}
in Lemma
7.22
:
if
\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{W}}
, then
there exists a
1
-distinguished
set

Z
such that
Z
is closed under

\mathbb{S}\mapsto\mathbb{S}^{\dagger}
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{Z}
for a
\Pi^{1}_{0}
-set
\mathcal{G}^{Z}

in Definition
7.14
of subsection
7.2
.

By iterating this ‘jump’ operators, we arrive at a
\Sigma_{N+1}
-formula
D_{N}[X]
for
N
-distinguished sets
for positive integers
N
, cf. Definition
7.4
.
The maximal
N
-distinguished class
\bigcup\{X\subset OT:D_{N}[X]\}
is
\Sigma^{1-}_{N+2}
-definable proper class
in
\Pi_{N}
-Collection or in
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mathrm{-DC+BI}
.

Up to this, everything seems to go well.
But as long as we have an infinite increasing sequence
\{\mathbb{S}_{n}\}_{n}
of successor stable ordinals,
a technical difficulty is hidden as follows.
Above a successor stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}_{0}
, there are increasing sequence

\mathbb{S}_{1}=\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\dagger},\mathbb{S}_{2}=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\dagger},\ldots
of successor stable ordinals.
Let
\rho_{n}\prec\mathbb{S}_{n}
.
Let us define ordinals
\kappa_{n,i}
and
\sigma_{n,i}
for
i\leq n
recursively by

\kappa_{n,n}=\mathbb{S}_{n}
,
\kappa_{n,i}=\kappa_{n,i+1}[\rho_{i}/\mathbb{S}_{i}]
,

\sigma_{n,n}=\rho_{n}
and

\sigma_{i}=\sigma_{n,i+1}[\rho_{i}/\mathbb{S}_{i}]
.
Let
\kappa_{n}=\kappa_{n,0}
and
\sigma_{n}=\sigma_{n,0}
.
Then we see that

\sigma_{0}<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<\cdots<\kappa_{2}<\kappa_{1}<\kappa_{0}
.
This might yield an infinite decreasing chain
\{\kappa_{n}\}_{n}
of collapsed ordinals.

For simplicity let
\rho_{i}=\psi_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}^{f_{i}}(\alpha_{i})
.
Then
M_{\rho_{i}}=C_{\alpha_{i}}(\rho_{i})
.
In order to collapse
\kappa_{n,i+1}
by
\rho_{i}
,
\rho_{j}\in M_{\rho_{i}}
has to be enjoyed for
j>i
.
Since
\rho_{j}>\rho_{i}
, this means that
\alpha_{j}<\alpha_{i}
.
Namely there must exist an infinite decreasing chain
\alpha_{0}>\alpha_{1}>\alpha_{2}>\cdots
in advance
to have another chain
\kappa_{0}>\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{2}>\cdots
.
Here
\alpha_{i}
is the ordinal
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho_{i})
in Definition
3.30
.
2
.
Let
\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})
be an ordinal in the layer
L(\mathbb{S})
of a successor stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
,
cf. Definition
3.34
.
A pair
(\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta),\mathtt{g}_{2}(\eta))
of ordinals is associated with such an ordinal
\eta

in Definitions
6.7
and
6.14
, and we show in
Lemma
6.15
that

(\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma),\mathtt{g}_{2}(\gamma))<_{lx}(\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta),\mathtt{g}_{2}(\eta))

when
\gamma\in R(\eta)
for the set
R(\eta)
in Definition
6.12
.
It turns out that this suffices to prove the well-foundedness in Lemma
7.32
.

6.2
Props

In this subsection an ordinal
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
and
a pair
\mathtt{g}(\alpha)=(\mathtt{g}_{1}(\alpha),\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha))

are introduced for ordinal terms
\alpha
.
These are needed to show that there is no infinite sequence
\{\rho_{n},\kappa_{n}\}_{n}

such that
\rho_{0}\prec\mathbb{S}_{0}
,

\kappa_{n}\in\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho_{n}]\}\cup\{\rho_{n}^{\dagger\vec{i}_{n}},\mathbb{S}_{n}^{\dagger\vec{i}_{n}}[\rho_{n}/\mathbb{S}_{n}]\}
and
either
\rho_{n+1}\prec\mathbb{S}_{n}^{\dagger\vec{i}_{n}}[\rho_{n}/\mathbb{S}_{n}]=\kappa_{n}
or
\rho_{n+1}\prec\tau^{\dagger\vec{i}_{n}}

for
\tau\prec\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho_{n}]=\kappa_{n}
,
cf. Proposition
6.10
,
Lemmas
6.15
and
7.32
.

Recall that
\alpha\in SSt^{M}
iff either
\alpha
is a successor stable ordinal in
SSt
or

\alpha=\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
for a
\beta\in SSt^{M}
and a successor stable ordinal
\mathbb{S}
,
cf. Definition
3.31
.
8
.

Definition 6.4

For
\rho\in\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}
with
\mathbb{S}\in SSt^{M}
, let

N(\rho)=\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\}\cup\{\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}},\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\vec{i}\neq\emptyset\}\cap OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
if
\mathbb{S}\not\in SSt
.
Otherwise

N(\rho)=\{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\}\cup\{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]:\vec{i}\neq\emptyset\}\cap OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

Note that
\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}}\in SSt
when
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
, and
N(\rho)\cap\Psi=\emptyset
.
Recall that, cf. Definition
3.34
,
L(\mathbb{S})
denotes the layer of
\mathbb{S}
, and

\alpha\in L(\mathbb{S})
iff
\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}
iff there are ordinals
\{\rho_{i},\kappa_{i}\}_{i}
such that

\kappa_{0}=\mathbb{S}
,
\rho_{i}\prec\kappa_{i}
,
\kappa_{i+1}\in N(\rho_{i})
, and

\alpha\in\{\rho_{0}\}\cup\{\rho_{i},\kappa_{i}\}_{i>0}
.

Definition 6.5

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt_{i}
and
\mathbb{T}\in St\cup\{\Omega\}
be the least such that

\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger i}
.
For
a\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
the
prop

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)
of
a

denotes an ordinal term defined recursively as follows.

1.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathbb{I}_{N})=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)=0
if
a\leq\mathbb{T}

In what follows assume
\mathbb{I}_{N}\neq a>\mathbb{T}
.

2.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)=\max_{i\leq m}\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a_{i})
if

a=a_{0}+\cdots+a_{m}
.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)=\max\{\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(b),\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(c)\}
if
a=\varphi bc
.

3.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\kappa)
if

a\in N(\kappa)

for a
\kappa\in L(\mathbb{U})\cap\Psi
with a
\mathbb{U}>\mathbb{T}
.

4.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathbb{U}^{\dagger k})=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathbb{U})
for
\mathbb{T}\leq\mathbb{U}\in St
.

5.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)
.

6.

For
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{fld}(f))=\max\{\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(b):b\in\mathrm{fld}(f)\}
, let

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\max\{\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\kappa),\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a),\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{fld}(f))\}&\mbox{if }\kappa>\mathbb{S}\\
\max\{a,\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)\}&\mbox{if }\kappa=\mathbb{S}\\
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\kappa)&\mbox{if }\kappa<\mathbb{S}\end{array}\right.

Proposition 6.6

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
and
\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)
,

\beta=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b)
with

\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

1.

Let
c\in C_{b}(\beta)
with

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(c)\neq 0
.
Then there exists a subterm

\gamma\in C_{b}(\beta)
of
c
such that

\gamma\prec\mathbb{S}
and
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\gamma)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(c)
.

2.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{fld}(f))\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=\max\{a,\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)\}
holds.

3.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)

if
\beta<\alpha
.

4.

Let
\delta<\alpha<\beta
with
\delta\prec\beta
.
Then

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
.

5.

Let
\{\gamma,\delta\}\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.
Then
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\gamma)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\delta)
if
\gamma<\delta
.

Proof
.
6.6
.
1
.
By induction on
\ell c
.

6.6
.
2
.
By (
11
) in
Definition
3.31
.
5
we obtain

\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset C_{a}(SC(a))
.

By induction on
\ell b
,
we see

b\in C_{a}(SC(a))\Rightarrow\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(b)\leq\max\{a,\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)\}
.

We show Propositions
6.6
.
3
and
6.6
.
4

simultaneously by induction on
\ell\beta+\ell\alpha
.

6.6
.
3
.
If
a=b
, then
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
.
Let
b<a
. We can assume
a<c=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(b)
.
By Proposition
6.6
.
1
pick a shortest subterm

\gamma\in C_{b}(\beta)\cap\mathbb{S}\subset\beta
of
b

such that
\gamma\prec\mathbb{S}
and
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\gamma)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(b)=c

for

.
Then
\gamma\preceq\delta=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{h}(c)
for some
h
and
\gamma<\beta
.
If
\delta\leq\alpha
, then IH yields

.
Assume
\gamma<\alpha<\delta
with
\gamma\prec\delta
.
IH for Proposition
6.6
.
4
then yields

.

Next let
a<b
.
Pick a subterm

of a term in

such that

and

.
Let
\eta\preceq\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{h}(d)=\sigma
for some

and

.
Then we obtain

, and IH yields

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\sigma)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\eta)
.
On the other hand we have

.

.
Hence
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
.

6.6
.

.
Pick a subterm
\eta
of a term in
\{a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)
such that

,

and

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\eta)\leq\max\{a,\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(a)\}
by Proposition
6.6
.
2
.
Let
\eta\preceq\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{h}(d)=\sigma
for some
h
and
d
.
Then we obtain
\delta\leq\sigma
.
If
\beta\leq\sigma
, then IH for Proposition

.

yields

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\sigma)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\eta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
.
Otherwise we obtain
\delta\leq\sigma<\beta
with
\delta\prec\beta
.
IH yields
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\sigma)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
.

6.6
.

.
This is seen by induction on

using Definition

, and Propositions

.

and

.

The set
Cr
of strongly critical numbers in

is divided to

Cr=LSt_{N}\cup SSt\cup\bigcup\{L(\mathbb{S}):\mathbb{S}\in SSt\}\cup(Cr\cap(\Omega+1))

,
cf. Definition
3.34
.

Definition 6.7

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
and
\alpha\in L(\mathbb{S})
.
Let
\alpha\in\Psi^{M}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}:\Leftrightarrow\exists\rho,b(\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(b))

Let us define ordinals
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha),\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)
and
\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha)

1.

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha)=0
for

.

2.

If
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
,

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(b))=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(b))=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)

for every

.
Also
\mathtt{g}_{2}(\rho)=o_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(m(\rho))+1
for

with base
\mathbb{I}_{N}
, and

\mathtt{g}_{2}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(b))=0
.

Let
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
and

,
where

if

.

\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)
.
Let

be such that
\alpha=\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.
If
\alpha\in\Psi\cup\Psi^{M}_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}

\mathtt{g}_{i}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{i}(\beta)
for

.

Proposition 6.8

Let
b=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\alpha)
for
\alpha\in L(\mathbb{S})\cap\Psi
with
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
.
Then

SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha))\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
.
Moreover

\mathtt{p}_{0}(\alpha)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\alpha
.
Cf. Definition
3.30
.
2
for
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\alpha)
.

Case 1
.
First let
\alpha\preceq\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b)
with an
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
and
f=m(\alpha)
.
By Proposition
3.32
.
2
let
\mathbb{T}\in LSt\cup\{\Omega\}
be such that

for a sequence
\vec{i}
.
We obtain

for

.
By
(

) in Definition

.
On the other hand we have
\mathtt{p}_{0}(\alpha)=b\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)
.

We claim that
\alpha<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
.

SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha))\subset C_{b}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b))\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)

follows from the claim.
For the claim it suffices to show
\mathbb{S}<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
.
Let
\{(\mathbb{T}_{m},\mathbb{S}_{m},\vec{i}_{m})\}_{m\leq n}
be the sequence
such that

\mathbb{T}_{0}\in LSt_{N}\cup\{\Omega\}
,

\mathbb{S}_{m}=\mathbb{T}_{m}^{\dagger\vec{i}_{m}}
and
\mathbb{T}_{m+1}\prec\mathbb{S}_{m}\,(m<n)
, and

\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}_{n}
, cf. the trail to
\mathbb{S}
in Proposition
3.56
.
If
\mathbb{T}_{0}=\Omega
, then
\mathbb{S}\leq\mathbb{S}_{0}<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)\in LSt_{N}
.
Let
\mathbb{T}_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(c)
.
Proposition
3.32
.
3
yields
c<b
, and

.
Hence
\mathbb{S}\leq\mathbb{S}_{0}<\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)\in LSt_{N}
.

Case 2
.
Next let
LSt_{i}\ni\rho\prec\mathbb{S}\in SSt
,

\alpha\prec^{R}\tau\in N(\rho)
and

\alpha=\beta[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
for a

.
Then

, and

SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha))=SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\beta))\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)

for

\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{2}(\beta)
, and

\mathtt{p}_{0}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)
.

On the other hand we have
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)\geq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)=c
with
M_{\rho}=C_{c}(\rho)
.
Thus it suffices to show
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\delta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\rho)
for

\delta\in C_{c}(\rho)
by induction on
\ell\delta
.
If
\delta\preceq\psi_{\mathbb{T}}^{f}(d)
with a
\mathbb{T}\in SSt
, then

.
We obtain

and
d\in C_{c}(\rho)
.

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{T}}(d)<c
.

Next let

with a

.
Then

and

.

.
