Proposition 6.9

Let
\tau\in L(\mathbb{S})\cup\{\mathbb{S}\}

and
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
.

For
\rho,\eta\prec\tau
,
if
\rho<\eta
, then

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)\leq\mathtt{g}_{0}(\eta)
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\rho
.

Case 1
.
\tau=\mathbb{S}
:
Let
\eta\preceq\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{f}(a)
and
\rho\preceq\beta=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b)
.
Then
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\eta)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\eta)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
and

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)
.
If
\beta\leq\alpha
, then Proposition
6.6
.
3
yields

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)
.
Suppose
\rho<\alpha<\beta
with
\rho\prec\beta
.
We obtain
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\alpha)

by Proposition
6.6
.
4
.

Case 2
.
\tau\neq\mathbb{S}
:

\tau\prec^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\kappa]

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho_{1})
and

3.44
for uncollapsing.

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho_{1})\leq\mathtt{g}_{0}(\eta_{1})
.

Proposition 6.10

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
,

\rho\prec\tau\in(L(\mathbb{S})\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\cap SSt^{M}
, and

\alpha\prec\sigma\in SSt^{M}
, where

\sigma\preceq^{R}\kappa\in N(\rho)
.
Then

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha)<\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)
.

Proof
. We may assume that either
\sigma=\kappa=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
or

\kappa=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]\,\&\,\sigma=(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(\gamma))^{\dagger\vec{i}}
for a
\gamma
and
an
\vec{i}
.
By induction on
\ell\alpha
we show
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha)<\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)
.

Case 1
.
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
:
Let
\rho\preceq\beta=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b)
.
Then
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b))
.
From
b\in\mathcal{H}_{b}(\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b))

we see
\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{T}}(b)<\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{T}}(\psi_{\mathbb{T}}(b))=b\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b))=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)

for any
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}\in SSt
.

Case 1.1
.
\sigma=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
:
Let

\alpha\preceq\psi_{\sigma}^{h_{1}}(c_{1})=\left(\psi_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}^{h}(c)\right)[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
, where

h_{1}=h[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\neq\emptyset
,
c_{1}=c[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
and

\sigma=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]=(\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}})[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.
Then
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}(\psi_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}^{h}(c))
.
We have

\rho<\psi_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}^{h}(c)\in M_{\rho}=C_{b}(\rho)
,
and hence
c<b
.
We obtain

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}(c)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}(b)

by Proposition
6.6
.
5
.

Case 1.2
.
\sigma=(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(\gamma_{1}))^{\dagger\vec{i}}
for a
\gamma_{1}
:
Let
\alpha\preceq\psi_{\sigma}^{h_{1}}(c_{1})=\left(\psi_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}^{h}(c)\right)[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
, where

h_{1}=h[\rho/\mathbb{S}]\neq\emptyset
,
c_{1}=c[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
and

\sigma=\mathbb{T}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
with
\mathbb{T}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\gamma)
and

\gamma_{1}=\gamma[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
.
Then
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}(\psi_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}^{h}(c))
.
We have

\psi_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}^{h}(c)\in M_{\rho}
.
As in
Case 1.1
we see

c<b
and

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}(c)\leq\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}}(b)
from
\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}
, i.e., from

\mathbb{S}<\mathbb{T}=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\gamma)\in LSt_{N}
.

Case 2
.
\rho\prec\tau\neq\mathbb{S}
:
Let
\lambda\prec\mathbb{S}
be such that either
\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{j}}[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]
or

\alpha\prec^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\lambda]
.
Then
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha_{1})
with
\alpha=\alpha_{1}[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]

and
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho_{1})
with
\rho=\rho_{1}[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]
.
We have
\rho_{1}\prec\tau[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}
and

\alpha_{1}\prec\sigma[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}

with
\sigma=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}]
or
\sigma=(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(\gamma))^{\dagger\vec{i}}
for a
\gamma
.
If
\tau[\lambda/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}\in SSt
, then
we obtain
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\alpha_{1})<\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho_{1})
by
Case 1
.
Otherwise IH with
\ell\alpha_{1}<\ell\alpha
yields the proposition.

\Box

Proposition 6.11

Let
\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset L(\mathbb{S})
with an
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
.
If
\alpha<\beta
, then

\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)
.

Proof
. Let
\rho,\eta\prec\mathbb{S}
be such that
either
\alpha=\rho
or
\alpha\preceq^{R}\kappa\in N(\rho)
,
and either
\beta=\eta
or

\beta\preceq^{R}\sigma\in N(\eta)
.
Then
\rho\leq\eta
by
\alpha<\beta
.
Proposition
6.9
yields

\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)\leq\mathtt{g}_{0}(\eta)=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)
.

\Box

Definition 6.12

A
set
R(\eta)\subset\Psi
is defined.

1.

Let
\eta\prec\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

\gamma\in R(\eta)
holds iff
there exists an
SSt\ni\mathbb{S}<\eta
such that
\gamma\in L(\mathbb{S})\cap\Psi
.

2.

Let

\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})
with an
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
.

\gamma\in R(\eta)\cap L(\mathbb{S})
holds iff
\gamma\in\Psi
,
\gamma<\eta
and one of the following holds:

(a)

\gamma\prec\eta
.

(b)

There exist
\tau\in L(\mathbb{S})
and
\vec{j},\vec{i}
such that

\eta\preceq\tau^{\dagger\vec{j}}
and one of the following holds:

i.

\gamma\prec^{R}\tau^{\dagger\vec{i}}
and

\vec{i}<_{lx}\vec{j}
.

ii.

\gamma\prec^{R}\tau^{\dagger\vec{i}}
,
\eta=\tau^{\dagger\vec{j}}
and

\vec{i}=\vec{j}
.

iii.

\gamma\prec^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]

.

iv.

\gamma\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\tau/\mathbb{S}]
.

(c)

There exist
\tau\in L(\mathbb{S})
and
\vec{i}
such that

\eta\preceq\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]
, and and one of the following holds:

i.

\gamma\prec^{R}\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]
and
\eta=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]
.

ii.

\gamma\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\tau/\mathbb{S}]
.

(d)

There exist
\tau\in L(\mathbb{S})
and
\vec{j},\vec{i}
such that

\eta\preceq\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{j}}[\tau/\mathbb{S}]
, and and one of the following holds:

i.

\gamma\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\tau/\mathbb{S}]
and

\vec{i}<_{lx}\vec{j}
.

ii.

\gamma\prec^{R}\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\tau/\mathbb{S}]
,
\eta=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{j}}[\tau/\mathbb{S}]
and

\vec{i}=\vec{j}
.

(e)

There exist
\tau\in L(\mathbb{S})
,
\rho
and
\vec{i}
such that

\eta,\rho\prec\mathbb{I}_{N}[\tau]
,
\rho<\eta
and

\gamma\prec^{R}\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}}
.

(f)

There exist
\tau\in(L(\mathbb{S})\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})\cap SSt^{M}
,
\rho
and
\kappa
such that

\eta,\rho\prec\tau
,
\rho<\eta
,

\gamma\prec^{R}\kappa\in N(\rho)
.

Proposition 6.13

Let

\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})
for an
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger k}\in SSt
with
\mathbb{T}\in\{\Omega\}\cup(LSt\cap\Psi)
.
Assume
\gamma\not\in R(\eta)
, and let
\tau
be maximal such that
\gamma\prec\tau\leq\eta
.
Then

\eta>\tau\in\Psi
.

Proof
. This is seen by an inspection to Definition
3.35
.

\Box

Definition 6.14

Let
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
and
\alpha\in L(\mathbb{S})
.

Let
\vec{i}=(i_{0}\geq i_{1}\geq\cdots\geq i_{m})
be a weakly descending chain of positive
integers with
i_{0}\leq N
.
Then let

o(\vec{i}\,):=\omega^{i_{0}-1}+\omega^{i_{1}-1}+\cdots+\omega^{i_{m}-1}<\omega^{N}
.

Let us define ordinals
\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)
and

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\alpha)
as follows.
Let
\lambda=\omega^{N+1}
.

1.

Let
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
.
Then

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)=\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}

and

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\rho)=\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)+1}
.

2.

Let
\rho\in L(\mathbb{S})
be such that

\rho\prec\mathbb{T}\in SSt^{M}\cap(L(\mathbb{S})\cup\{\mathbb{S}\})
,

\alpha\prec\kappa\in N(\rho)\cup\{(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(a))^{\dagger\vec{i}}:\vec{i}\neq\emptyset\}
,
where
\alpha\neq\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(b)
for any
b
if
\kappa=\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]
.
Let
\mathtt{g}_{1}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}
.

(a)

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{T}])=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot(o(\vec{i}\,)+1)
.

(b)

\alpha\prec\mathbb{T}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{T}]
:

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot o(\vec{i}\,)
.

(c)

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot(\omega^{N}+1)
.

(d)

\alpha\prec\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]
:

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot\omega^{N}
.

(e)

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}((\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(a))^{\dagger\vec{i}})=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot(\omega^{N}+o(\vec{i}\,)+1)
.

(f)

\alpha\prec(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho]}(a))^{\dagger\vec{i}}
:

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot(\omega^{N}+o(\vec{i}\,))
.

(g)

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}})=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot(\omega^{N}+\omega^{N}+o(\vec{i}\,)+1)
.

(h)

\alpha\prec\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}}
:

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}\cdot(\omega^{N}+\omega^{N}+o(\vec{i}\,))
.

Let
\mathtt{g}(\alpha)=(\mathtt{g}_{1}(\alpha),\mathtt{g}_{2}(\alpha))
.

Lemma 6.15

Let
\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})

with
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
.
Then

\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\gamma)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\eta)
,

\mathtt{g}(\gamma)<_{lx}\mathtt{g}(\eta)
and

SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\gamma))\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)

for
\gamma\in R(\eta)
and
b=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\eta)
.

Proof
.

Case 1
.
\gamma\prec\eta
: We have
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\gamma)=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\eta)
.
If
\eta\in\Psi
, then

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta)=\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)
and

\mathtt{g}_{2}(\gamma)<\mathtt{g}_{2}(\eta)
by
Lemma
6.2
.
Otherwise
\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)<\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta)
.
In what follows assume
\gamma\not\prec\eta
.
We claim that
\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)<\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta)
.

Case 2
.
\eta\preceq\tau_{1}
,
\gamma\prec^{R}\tau_{2}
with

\{\tau_{2}\leq\tau_{1}\}\subset N(\tau)

for a
\tau\in L(\mathbb{S})
,
cf. Definitions
6.12
.
2b
,
6.12
.
2(b)iii
,
6.12
.
2(b)iv
,
6.12
.
2c
,
6.12
.
2d
: We have

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\tau)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}\cdot(\alpha+1)

for an
\alpha<\omega^{N+1}
.
If
\gamma\prec\tau_{2}
, then

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\tau)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}\cdot(\beta+1)

with
\beta<\alpha
.
Otherwise let
\sigma\prec\tau_{2}
be such that

\gamma\prec\sigma_{1}\in SSt^{M}
,

\sigma_{1}\preceq^{R}\kappa_{1}\in N(\sigma)
.
We obtain
\mathtt{g}_{0}(\sigma)<\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)
by
Proposition
6.10
, and

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\tau)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}\cdot\beta+\delta
with
\delta<\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\sigma)+1}\leq\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}
.

Case 3
.

\rho,\eta\prec\tau_{1}\in N(\tau)
,

\rho<\eta
and
\gamma\prec^{R}\kappa\in N(\rho)
,
cf. Definitions
6.12
.
2e
and
6.12
.
2f
:
We have

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\tau)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}\cdot\alpha

for an
\alpha<\omega^{N+1}
,

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}
, and

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\delta
for
\delta<\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\tau)}

by Proposition
6.10
.

Case 4
.
\rho,\eta\prec\mathbb{S}
,
\rho<\eta
and
\gamma\prec^{R}\kappa\in N(\rho)
,
cf. Definition
6.12
.
2f
:
We have

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\eta)=\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\eta)+1}
,
and

\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)=\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)}
,
where

\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)\leq\mathtt{g}_{0}(\eta)
by Proposition
6.9
.
On the other hand we have

\mathtt{g}_{1}(\gamma)=\mathtt{g}^{\prime}_{1}(\rho)+\delta
with
\delta<\lambda^{\mathtt{g}_{0}(\rho)+1}
.

Thus
\mathtt{g}(\gamma)<_{lx}\mathtt{g}(\eta)
is shown.
In each case
c=\mathtt{p}_{0}(\gamma)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\gamma)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\eta)=b
holds by Proposition
6.8
.
We obtain
\psi_{\mathbb{I}}(c)\leq\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
by

c\in C_{c}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(c))
and
b\in C_{b}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b))
.
On the other hand we have

SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\gamma))\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(c)
by Proposition
6.8
.
Hence
SC(\mathtt{g}_{2}(\gamma))\subset\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
.

\Box

Proposition 6.16

Let
\{\alpha_{1},\beta\}\subset L(\mathbb{S})
for an
\mathbb{S}\in SSt
,

\alpha_{1}=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\leq\psi_{\sigma}^{h}(c)=\beta
and

\beta\in C_{a}(\alpha_{1})
. Then

c<a
and

\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha_{1})
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\beta
.
We have
c\in K_{\alpha_{1}}(\beta)<a
, and
\{\sigma,c\}\subset C_{a}(\alpha_{1})
.
We show
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{g}_{0}^{*}(\alpha_{1})
.
First let
\beta\prec\mathbb{S}
.
We show
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\beta)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha_{1})
.
We can assume
\sigma=\mathbb{S}
by IH.
Let
\gamma=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}^{g}(b)
be a proper subterm of
\beta
.
If
\gamma\in K_{\alpha_{1}}(\beta)
, then
b<a
.
If
\gamma<\alpha_{1}
, then
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\gamma)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha_{1})
by
Proposition
6.11
.

Second let
\rho\prec\mathbb{S}
and
\beta\prec^{R}\kappa\in N(\rho)
.
Then
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)
.
If
\alpha_{1}\leq\rho
, then
\rho\in C_{a}(\alpha_{1})
and

\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)\leq\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha_{1})
by the first case.
Let
\rho<\alpha_{1}<\beta
.
Then we obtain
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)=\mathtt{p}_{\mathbb{S}}(\rho)=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\beta)
.

\Box

6.3
Coefficients

In this subsection we introduce coefficient sets
\mathcal{E}(\alpha),G_{\delta}(\alpha),F_{X}(\alpha),k_{X}(\alpha)
of

\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
for
X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
each of which is a finite set of subterms of
\alpha
.
These are utilized in our well-foundedness proof.
Roughly
\mathcal{E}(\alpha)
is the set of subterms of the form
\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
, and

F_{X}(\alpha)
[
k_{X}(\alpha)
] the set of subterms in
X
[subterms not in
X
], resp.

Let us write for
\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}
,

\alpha^{\dagger 0}=\min\{\sigma\in Reg:\sigma>\alpha\}
for the next regular ordinal
\alpha^{+}
above
\alpha
.
Let
\alpha^{\dagger i}:=\infty
if
\alpha\geq\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
For
0\leq i\leq N
, let

\alpha^{-i}:=\max\{\sigma\in St_{i}\cup\{0\}:\sigma\leq\alpha\}
when
\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}
, and

\alpha^{-i}:=\mathbb{I}_{N}
if
\alpha\geq\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

Although
\alpha^{-1}
is similar to the Mostowski uncollapsing
\alpha[\rho/\mathbb{S}]^{-1}
in
Definition
3.44
, no confusion likely occurs.

Since
St_{i+1}\subset St_{i}
, we obtain
\alpha^{\dagger i}\leq\alpha^{\dagger(i+1)}
and

\beta^{\dagger 0}<\sigma
if
\beta<\sigma\in St\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
since each
\sigma\in St
is a limit of regular ordinals.

Note that
R(\eta)\subset L(\mathbb{S})
if
\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})
, and

\gamma^{-N}=\eta^{-N}
for every
\gamma,\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})
.

Definition 6.17

For terms
\alpha,\delta\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and
X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
, finite sets

\mathcal{E}(\alpha)
,
G_{\delta}(\alpha)
,
F_{X}(\alpha)
,
k_{X}(\alpha)
of terms
are defined recursively as follows.

1.

\mathcal{E}(\alpha)=\emptyset
for
\alpha\in\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
.

\mathcal{E}(\alpha_{m}+\cdots+\alpha_{0})=\bigcup_{i\leq m}\mathcal{E}(\alpha_{i})
.

\mathcal{E}(\varphi\beta\gamma)=\mathcal{E}(\beta)\cup\mathcal{E}(\gamma)
.

\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{I}_{N}[\rho])=\mathcal{E}(\rho^{\dagger\vec{i}})=\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{S}^{\dagger\vec{i}}[\rho/\mathbb{S}])=\mathcal{E}(\rho)
.

\mathcal{E}(\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a))=\{\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\}
.

\mathcal{E}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=\{\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}(\alpha)=\bigcup\{\mathcal{A}(\beta):\beta\in\mathcal{E}(\alpha)\}

for
\mathcal{A}\in\{G_{\delta},F_{X},k_{X}\}
.

3.

G_{\delta}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=G_{\delta}(a)
.

F_{X}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=F_{X}(a)
if
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\not\in X
, and

F_{X}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=\{\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\}
if
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\in X
.

k_{X}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=\{\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\}\cup k_{X}(a)
if
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\not\in X
, and

k_{X}(\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a))=\emptyset
if
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)\in X
.

G_{\delta}(\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}G_{\delta}(\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))&\delta<\pi\\
\{\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\}&\pi\leq\delta\end{array}\right.

F_{X}(\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}F_{X}(\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))&\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\not\in X\\
\{\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\}&\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\in X\end{array}\right.

k_{X}(\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\}\cup k_{X}(\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))&\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\not\in X\\
\emptyset&\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)\in X\end{array}\right.

4.

For

\alpha\in N(\rho)

G_{\delta}(\alpha)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\alpha\}&\alpha<\delta\\
G_{\delta}(\rho)&\delta\leq\alpha\end{array}\right.

F_{X}(\alpha)=F_{X}(\rho)
and

k_{X}(\alpha)=k_{X}(\rho)
.

For
\mathcal{A}\in\{K_{\delta},G_{\delta},F_{X},k_{X}\}
and sets
Y\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

\mathcal{A}(Y):=\bigcup\{\mathcal{A}(\alpha):\alpha\in Y\}
.

Definition 6.18

S(\eta)
denotes a set of immediate subterms of
\eta
.
For example
S(\varphi\beta\gamma)=\{\beta,\gamma\}
.

S(\eta):=\emptyset
when
\eta\in\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
,

S(\alpha)=\{\rho\}
for
\alpha\in N(\rho)
,

S(\eta)=\{\eta\}
when
\eta\in\Psi
.

Proposition 6.19

For
\{\alpha,\delta,a,b,\rho\}\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

1.

G_{\delta}(\alpha)\leq\alpha
.

2.

\alpha\in C_{a}(b)\Rightarrow G_{\delta}(\alpha)\subset C_{a}(b)
.

Proof
. These are shown simultaneously by induction on
\ell\alpha
.
It is easy to see that

G_{\delta}(\alpha)\ni\beta\Rightarrow\beta<\delta\,\&\,\ell\beta\leq\ell\alpha

(53)

6.19
.
1
.
Consider the case
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
with
\delta<\pi
.
Then
G_{\delta}(\alpha)=G_{\delta}(\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,a\})
.
On the other hand we have

\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,a\}\subset C_{a}(\alpha)
.
Proposition
6.19
.
2
with (
53
)
yields

G_{\delta}(\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,a\})\subset C_{a}(\alpha)\cap\pi\subset\alpha
.
Hence
G_{\delta}(\alpha)<\alpha
.

Next let
\alpha\in N(\rho)

with
\delta\leq\alpha
.
Then
G_{\delta}(\alpha)=G_{\delta}(\rho)
.
By IH we have
G_{\delta}(\rho)\leq\rho<\alpha
.
Hence
G_{\delta}(\alpha)<\alpha
.

6.19
.
2
.
Since
G_{\delta}(\alpha)\leq\alpha
by Proposition
6.19
.
1
, we can assume
\alpha\geq b
.

Consider the case
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
with
\delta<\pi
.
Then
\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,a\}\subset C_{a}(b)
and

G_{\delta}(\alpha)=G_{\delta}(\mathrm{fld}(f)\cup\{\pi,a\})
.
IH yields the lemma.

Next let
\alpha\in N(\rho)

with
\delta\leq\alpha
.
Then
G_{\delta}(\alpha)=G_{\delta}(\rho)
and
\rho<\alpha
.

b\leq\alpha\in C_{a}(b)
yields

\rho\in C_{a}(b)
.
IH yields the lemma.

\Box

Proposition 6.20

If

\beta\not\in C_{a}(\alpha)
and
K_{\delta}(\beta)<a
, then there exists a
\gamma\in F_{\delta}(\beta)

such that
C_{a}(\alpha)\not\ni\gamma<\delta
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\beta
. Assume
\beta\not\in C_{a}(\alpha)
and

K_{\delta}(\beta)<a
.
By IH we can assume that
\beta=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(b)
.
If
\beta<\delta
, then
\beta\in F_{\delta}(\beta)
, and
\gamma=\beta
is a desired one.
Assume
\beta\geq\delta
. Then we obtain

K_{\delta}(\beta)=\{b\}\cup K_{\delta}(\{b,\kappa\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))<a
.
In particular
b<a
, and hence
\{b,\kappa\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\not\subset C_{a}(\alpha)
.
By IH there exists a
\gamma\in F_{\delta}(\{b,\kappa\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))=F_{\delta}(\beta)

such that
C_{a}(\alpha)\not\ni\gamma<\delta
.

\Box

7
Well-foundedness proof with the maximal distinguished sets

In this section working in the second order arithmetic

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC}}+\mbox{{\rm BI}}
,
we show the well-foundedness of the notation system
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
up to

each

\alpha<\Omega
.
The proof is based on distinguished classes, which was first
introduced by Buchholz
[
7
]
.
Each ordinal term
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
is identified with its code
\lceil\alpha\rceil\in\mathbb{N}
, cf. Lemma
3.36
.

7.1
Distinguished sets

In this subsection
we establish elementary facts on
distinguished classes.

X,Y,Z,\ldots
range over
subsets
of
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
while
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y},\ldots
range over
classes
, which are
definable by second-order formulas in the language of arithmetic.
Following
[
10
]
,
we define sets
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
for
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and

X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
as follows.

Definition 7.1

For
\alpha,\beta\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
and
X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
, let us define
a set

\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
recursively as follows.

1.

\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\cup(X\cap\alpha)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.

2.

Let
(\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{n})\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with

\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.
Then

(\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{n})\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.

3.

Let
\varphi\beta\gamma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with

\{\beta,\gamma\}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.
Then
\varphi\beta\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.

4.

Let
\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\beta)\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with

\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.
Then

\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\beta)\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
if
\mathbb{I}_{N}>\alpha
.

5.

Let
\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(\beta)\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
with

\{\sigma,\beta\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.
Then

\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(\beta)\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
if
\sigma>\alpha
.

6.

Let

\beta\in N(\rho)

with
\rho\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.
Then

\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
if
\beta\geq\alpha
.

Proposition 7.2

Assume
\forall\gamma\geq\alpha[\gamma\in P\Rightarrow\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(P)]
for a set
P\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

1.

\alpha\leq\beta\Rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
.

2.

\alpha\leq\beta<\alpha^{\dagger 0}\Rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
.

Proof
.
7.2
.
1
.
We see by induction on
\ell\gamma\,(\gamma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}))
that

\forall\beta\geq\alpha[\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)\Rightarrow\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)\cup(P\cap\beta)]

(54)

For example, if
\psi_{\pi}^{f}(\delta)\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)
with
\pi>\beta\geq\alpha

and
\{\pi,\delta\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)\cup(P\cap\beta)
, then

\pi\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
, and
for any
\gamma\in\{\delta\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)
, either
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
or

\gamma\in P\cap\beta
. If
\gamma<\alpha
, then

\gamma\in P\cap\alpha\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
.
If
\alpha\leq\gamma\in P\cap\beta
, then
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(P)
by the assumption, and
by IH we have
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)\cup(P\cap\gamma)
, i.e.,
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
.
Therefore
\{\pi,\delta\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
, and

\psi_{\pi}^{f}(\delta)\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
.

Using (
54
) we see from the assumption that

\forall\beta\geq\alpha[\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)\Rightarrow\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)]
.

7.2
.
2
.
Assume
\alpha\leq\beta<\alpha^{\dagger 0}
. Then by Proposition
7.2
.
1
we have

\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
.

\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)\Rightarrow\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)
is seen
by induction on
\ell\gamma
using the facts
\beta^{-0}=\alpha^{-0}
and

\beta^{\dagger 0}=\alpha^{\dagger 0}
.

\Box

Definition 7.3

1.

\mathrm{Prg[}X,Y]:\Leftrightarrow\forall\alpha\in X(X\cap\alpha\subset Y\to\alpha\in Y)
.

2.

For a definable class
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{X}]
denotes the schema:

\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{X}]:\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{Prg}[\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}]\to\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{Y}\mbox{ {\rm holds for} any definable classes }\mathcal{Y}
.

3.

For
X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,
W(X)
denotes the
well-founded part
of
X
.

4.

Wo[X]:\Leftrightarrow X\subset W(X)
.

Note that for
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

W(X)\cap\alpha=W(X\cap\alpha)
.

Definition 7.4

For
P,X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
and

\gamma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
, define
W_{i}^{\alpha}(P)\,(0\leq i\leq N)
and

D_{i}^{\gamma}[P;X]\,(0\leq i\leq N)

recursively on
i\leq N
as follows.

\displaystyle W_{0}^{\alpha}(P)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P))

(55)

\displaystyle D_{i}^{\gamma}[P;X]

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle Wo[P]\,\&\,P\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}=X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}\,\&\,

(56)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha<\mathbb{I}_{N}\left(\gamma^{-(i+1)}\leq\alpha\leq P\to W_{i}^{\alpha}(P)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=P\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}\right)

\displaystyle W_{i+1}^{\gamma}(X)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigcup\{P\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}:D_{i}^{\gamma}[P;X]\}\,(i<N)

(57)

where
\gamma^{-(N+1)}:=0
.
Obviously
D_{N}[X;Y]\Leftrightarrow D_{N}[X;Z]
for every
Y
and
Z
.
From
\mathcal{W}_{N}^{\gamma}(X)
define

\displaystyle D_{N}[X]

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle D_{N}[X;X]

(58)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle Wo[X]\,\&\,\forall\gamma\left(\gamma\leq X\to\mathcal{W}_{N}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{N+1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigcup\{X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}:D_{N}[X]\}

(59)

A set
P
is said to be an
i
-
distinguished set
for
\gamma
and
X
if
D_{i}^{\gamma}[P;X]
,
and a set
X
is an
N
-
distinguished set
if
D_{N}[X]
.

Observe that in
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
,

W^{\alpha}_{0}(P)
as well as
D^{\gamma}_{0}[P;X]
are
\Delta_{1}
.
Assuming that
D^{\gamma}_{i}[P;X]
is
\Delta_{i+1}
,

\mathcal{W}_{i+1}^{\gamma}(X)
is
\Sigma_{i+1}
, and

D^{\gamma}_{i+1}[P;X]
is
\Delta_{i+2}
.
Hence
D_{N}[X]
is
\Delta_{N+1}
, and

\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
is a
\Sigma_{N+1}
-class.
In
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}
,
each
\mathcal{W}_{i}^{\gamma}(X)
is a set, i.e.,

\forall\gamma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\forall X\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\exists Y[Y=\mathcal{W}_{i}^{\gamma}(X)]

for
0\leq i\leq N
,
and
\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
is a proper class.

Proposition 7.5

Let
D_{0}^{\gamma}[P;X]
and
\gamma^{-1}\leq\alpha\in P
.
Then
\forall\beta\geq\gamma^{-1}[\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)]
.

Proof
. Let
D_{0}^{\gamma}[P;X]
and
\gamma^{-1}\leq\alpha\in P
.
We obtain
\alpha\in P\cap\alpha^{\dagger 0}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P))\cap\alpha^{\dagger 0}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
by (
55
) and (
56
).
Hence
\forall\delta\geq\gamma^{-1}(\delta\in P\Rightarrow\delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(P))
, and

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)
for any
\gamma^{-1}\leq\beta\leq\alpha
by Proposition
7.2
.
1
.
Moreover for
\beta>\alpha
we have
\alpha\in P\cap\beta\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)
.

\Box

Proposition 7.6

If
P\cap\alpha=Q\cap\alpha
, then

W_{i}^{\alpha}(P)=W_{i}^{\alpha}(Q)
.

Proof
. For
i>0
, this follows from (
56
) and
\alpha^{-i}\leq\alpha
.
For
i=0
, we obtain
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Q)
by

P\cap\alpha=Q\cap\alpha
.
Hence

W_{0}^{\alpha}(P)=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P))=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Q))=W_{0}^{\alpha}(Q)

by (
55
).

\Box

Lemma 7.7

\alpha\leq P\,\&\,\alpha\leq Q\Rightarrow P\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=Q\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}

if
D_{i}^{\gamma}[P;X]
and
D_{i}^{\gamma}[Q;X]
.

Proof
. Suppose
\alpha\leq P
,
\alpha\leq Q
,
D_{i}^{\gamma}[P;X]
and
D_{i}^{\gamma}[Q;X]
.
We have
P\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}=X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}=Q\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
.
We may assume that
\gamma^{-(i+1)}\leq\alpha
since

\alpha^{\dagger i}\leq\gamma^{-(i+1)}
when
\alpha<\gamma^{-(i+1)}
.

By (
56
) we obtain

\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}_{i}(P)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=P\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
and

\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}_{i}(Q)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=Q\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
.
We obtain
Wo[P\cup Q]
by
Wo[P]
and
Wo[Q]
.
We show
\beta\in P\cap Q
by induction on
\beta\in(P\cup Q)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
.
Let
\beta\in(P\cup Q)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
and
P\cap\beta=Q\cap\beta
.
If
\beta<\gamma^{-(i+1)}
, then
\beta\in P\cap Q
by
P\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}=Q\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
.
Let
\gamma^{-(i+1)}\leq\beta
.

If
\alpha\leq\beta
, then
P\cap\alpha=Q\cap\alpha
, and

\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}_{i}(P)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=\mathcal{W}^{\alpha}_{i}(Q)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}

by Proposition
7.6
.
Hence

\beta\in P\cap Q
.

Let
\gamma^{-(i+1)}\leq\beta<\alpha
.
We obtain
\mathcal{W}^{\beta}_{i}(P)\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=\mathcal{W}^{\beta}_{i}(Q)\cap\beta^{\dagger i}
by
P\cap\beta=Q\cap\beta
and Proposition
7.6
.
By (
56
),

\beta\leq P
and
\beta\leq Q
,
we obtain

P\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=\mathcal{W}^{\beta}_{i}(P)\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=\mathcal{W}^{\beta}_{i}(Q)\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=Q\cap\beta^{\dagger i}
.
Hence
\beta\in P\cap Q
.

\Box

Lemma 7.8

(\Sigma^{1}_{N+1}\mbox{{\rm-CA}})

For each
i\leq N
,
\forall\gamma<\mathbb{I}_{N}\forall X\exists Y(Y=W^{\gamma}_{i}(X))
.
Let
\gamma<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

1.

For
i\leq N
,
W^{\gamma}_{i}(X)
is a well order:

Wo[W^{\gamma}_{i}(X)]
.

2.

For
i<N
,

W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)
is the maximal
i
-distinguished
set

for
\gamma
and
X
if
X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
is a well order:

Wo[X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}]\Rightarrow D^{\gamma}_{i}[W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X);X]
.
In particular
W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}=X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
holds.

Proof
.
7.8
.
1
.
Clearly
W^{\gamma}_{0}(X)=W(\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(X))
is a well order.
We show
Wo[W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)]
.
Let

\{\beta<\alpha\}\subset W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)
.
Pick a
P
and a
Q
such that

D^{\gamma}_{i}[P;X]
,
\alpha\in P
,
D^{\gamma}_{i}[Q;X]
and
\beta\in Q
by (
57
).
Lemma
7.7
yields

\beta\in Q\cap\beta^{\dagger i}\subset P
.
We obtain
Wo[W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\alpha]
by
Wo[P]
.

7.8
.
2
.
Assuming that
X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
is a well order, we see that

X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
is the minimal
i
-distinguished set for
\gamma
and
X
:

D_{i}^{\gamma}[X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)};X]
.
We obtain
W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}=X\cap\gamma^{-(i+1)}
.
Lemma
7.8
.
1
yields
Wo[W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)]
.

Let
\gamma^{-(i+1)}\leq\alpha\leq W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)
.
We show

W^{\alpha}_{i}(W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X))\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
.
Pick a
P
such that
D^{\gamma}_{i}[P;X]
and
\alpha\leq P
.
We obtain

W^{\alpha}_{i}(P)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=P\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}\subset W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}

by (
56
).
Let
D^{\gamma}_{i}[Q;X]
and
\beta\in Q\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
.
Lemma
7.7
yields

\beta\in Q\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=P\cap\beta^{\dagger i}
for
\beta^{\dagger i}\leq\alpha^{\dagger i}
.

Therefore we obtain

W^{\alpha}_{i}(P)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=P\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
, a fortiori

P\cap\alpha=\mathcal{W}^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\alpha
.
Hence

W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=P\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=W^{\alpha}_{i}(P)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}=W^{\alpha}_{i}(W^{\gamma}_{i+1}(X))\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}

by Proposition
7.6
.

\Box

Lemma 7.9

1.

Let
X
and
Y
be
N
-distinguished sets, and
\gamma<\mathbb{I}_{N}
.
Then
\gamma\leq X\,\&\,\gamma\leq Y\Rightarrow X\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}=Y\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}
.

2.

{\cal W}_{N+1}
is the
N
-maximal distinguished
class
, i.e.,

D_{N}[{\cal W}_{N+1}]
.

3.

For a family
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of
N
-distinguished sets,
the union
Y=\bigcup_{j\in J}Y_{j}
is also an
N
-distinguished set.

Proof
.
7.9
.
1
is seen as in Lemma
7.7
.

7.9
.
2
and
7.9
.
3

follow from Lemma
7.9
.
1
as in Lemma
7.8
.

\Box

Lemma 7.10

Let
D_{N}[X]
and
\gamma\in X\subset\mathbb{I}_{N}
. Then for each
0\leq i\leq N
,

\gamma\in W_{i}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger i}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger i}
and

D_{i}^{\gamma}[X;X]
holds.
In particular
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(X)
.

Proof
. By induction on
N-i
.
We obtain
\gamma\in W_{N}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}

by
D_{N}[X]
and
\gamma\in X
.
Lemma
7.8
with
Wo[X]
yields

D_{N-1}^{\gamma}[W_{N}^{\gamma}(X);X]
, and
D_{N-1}^{\gamma}[X;X]
follows.

Assuming
D_{i+1}^{\gamma}[X;X]
, we obtain

W_{i+1}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger(i+1)}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger(i+1)}
by

\gamma^{-(i+1)}\leq\gamma\in X
, and

D_{i}^{\gamma}[W_{i+1}^{\gamma}(X);X]
by Lemma
7.8
.
Hence
D_{i}^{\gamma}[X;X]
and
\gamma\in W_{i}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger i}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger i}
.

\Box

Proposition 7.11

Let
D_{N}[X]
,
\alpha\leq\gamma\in X
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(X)
.
Then
\alpha\in X
.

Proof
. Lemma
7.10
yields

\gamma\in W(\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(X))\cap\gamma^{\dagger 0}=W_{0}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger 0}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger 0}
.

\gamma\geq\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(X)
yields

\alpha\in W_{0}^{\gamma}(X)\cap\gamma^{\dagger 0}=X\cap\gamma^{\dagger 0}
.

\Box

Proposition 7.12

Let
D_{N}[X]
.

1.

Let
\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset X
with
\alpha+\beta=\alpha\#\beta
and
\alpha>0
.
Then
\gamma=\alpha+\beta\in X
.

2.

If
\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset X
, then
\varphi\alpha\beta\in X
.

Proof
. Proposition
7.12
.
2
is seen
by main induction on
\alpha\in X
with
subsidiary induction on
\beta\in X
using
Proposition
7.12
.
1
.
We show Proposition
7.12
.
1
.
By Lemma
7.10

we obtain

\alpha\in X\cap\alpha^{\dagger 0}=W_{0}^{\alpha}(X)\cap\alpha^{\dagger 0}
.
We see that
\alpha+\beta\in W_{0}^{\alpha}(X)=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X))
by induction on

\beta\in X\cap(\alpha+1)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.

\Box

Lemma 7.13

1.

\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}=\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}))\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

2.

(BI)

For
each

n<\omega
,

\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)]
, i.e.,
for each class
\mathcal{X}
,

\mathrm{Prg}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}),\mathcal{X}]\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)\subset\mathcal{X}
.

3.

For
each

n<\omega
,

\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)\subset W(\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}))
.
In particular

\{\mathbb{I}_{N},\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)\}\subset W(\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}))
.

Proof
.
7.13
.
1
.

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}\Rightarrow\alpha\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}

is seen by induction on
\ell\alpha
using Proposition
7.12
and
Lemma
7.9
.
2
.
Since
\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
is well-founded, we obtain

\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}))\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
.

7.13
.
2
.
We show
\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)]

by metainduction on
n<\omega
.
Let
D_{N}[Y]
. We obtain
Wo[Y]
, and
\mathrm{TI}[Y]
follows from (BI).
We have
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}=\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
,
and
\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}=Y\cap\gamma^{\dagger N}

for
\gamma\in Y\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}
by Lemma
7.9
.
1
.
We obtain
\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}]
, from which

\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)]
follows.

Assuming
\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)]
,

\mathrm{TI}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n+1}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)]
is seen from
the fact that
\mathrm{Prg}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}),A]\to\mathrm{Prg}[\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}),\mathtt{j}[A]]
, where
for a given formula
A
,
\mathtt{j}[A](\alpha)
denotes the formula

\forall\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\left[\forall\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\beta\,A(\gamma)\to\forall\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap(\beta+\omega^{\alpha})A(\gamma)\right]
.

\Box

7.2
Sets
\mathcal{G}^{X}

In this subsection we establish a key fact, Lemma
7.25
on distinguished sets.

Definition 7.14

\mathcal{G}^{X}:=\{\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}:\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\,\&\,\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\cap\alpha\subset X\}
.

Proposition 7.15

Let
D_{N}[X]
and
\alpha\in X
. Then
\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{X}
.

Proof
. By Lemma
7.10
we obtain

\alpha\in W_{0}^{\alpha}(X)=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X))
.
Hence
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)
.
On the other side Proposition
7.11
yields

\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\cap\alpha\subset X
.

\Box

Lemma 7.16

(\Sigma^{1}_{N+1}\mbox{{\rm-CA}})

Suppose
D_{N}[Y]
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{Y}
.
Let

P_{N}=W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha^{\dagger N}
.
Assume that the following condition (
60
)
is fulfilled.
Then
\alpha\in P_{N}
and
D_{N}[P_{N}]
.
In particular
\alpha\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
holds.

Moreover if there exists a set
Z
and an ordinal
\gamma
such that

Y=W_{N}^{\gamma}(Z)
and
\alpha^{-N}=\gamma^{-N}
,
then
\alpha\in Y
holds.

\forall\beta\geq\alpha^{-1}\left(Y\cap\alpha^{\dagger 1}<\beta\,\&\,\beta^{\dagger 0}<\alpha^{\dagger 0}\to W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y\right)

(60)

Proof
. If
Y=W_{N}^{\gamma}(Z)
with
\alpha^{-N}=\gamma^{-N}
, then

Y\cap\alpha^{-N}=Z\cap\alpha^{-N}
and
W_{N}^{\gamma}(Z)=W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)
.
Hence if
\alpha\in W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)
, then
\alpha\in Y
.

Lemma
7.8
.
2
yields

\forall i<N\left[W_{i+1}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger(i+1)}=Y\cap\beta^{\dagger(i+1)}\right]

(61)

Let
P_{i}=W_{i}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha^{\dagger i}
for
0\leq i\leq N
.
By
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha\subset Y
and
Wo[Y]

we obtain for
P_{0}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y))\cap\alpha^{\dagger 0}

P_{0}\cap\alpha=Y\cap\alpha=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha

(62)

Hence
\alpha\in P_{0}
.
We obtain
D_{i-1}^{\alpha}[W_{i}^{\alpha}(Y);Y]
for
i>0
.
This together with (
62
) yields for
0\leq i\leq N

P_{i}\cap\alpha^{-i}=Y\cap\alpha^{-i}

(63)

Claim 7.17

\alpha^{\dagger 0}=\gamma^{\dagger 0}\,\&\,\gamma\in P_{0}\Rightarrow\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(P_{0})
.

Proof
of Claim
7.17
.
Let
\alpha^{\dagger 0}=\gamma^{\dagger 0}
and

\gamma\in P_{0}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y))\cap\alpha^{\dagger 0}
.
We obtain
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)=\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)
by
Propositions
7.15
and
7.2
.
Hence

Y\cap\gamma\subset\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\gamma
.

\gamma\in W(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y))
yields

Y\cap\gamma\subset P_{0}
.
Therefore
we obtain

\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(P_{0})
.

\Box
of Claim
7.17
.

Claim 7.18

D_{i}^{\alpha}[P_{i};Y]
and
\alpha\in P_{i+1}
for each
0\leq i<N
.

Proof
of Claim
7.18
.
Obviously
Wo[P_{i}]
.
(
63
) yields
P_{i}\cap\alpha^{-(i+1)}=Y\cap\alpha^{-(i+1)}
.
Let
\alpha^{-(i+1)}\leq\beta\leq P_{i}
.
We show
W_{i}^{\beta}(P_{i})\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=P_{i}\cap\beta^{\dagger i}
.

Case 1
.
\beta^{\dagger i}=\alpha^{\dagger i}
:
First let
i=0
.
We obtain
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(P)=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(P)
by
Proposition
7.2
and Claim
7.17
.
Hence the assertion follows from (
62
).

Next let
i>0
.
(
63
) with
\beta^{-i}=\alpha^{-i}
yields

W_{i}^{\beta}(P_{i})=W_{i}^{\alpha}(P_{i})=W_{i}^{\alpha}(Y)
.

Case 2
.
\beta^{\dagger i}<\alpha^{\dagger i}
:
For
i>0
, (
61
) yields

W_{i}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=Y\cap\beta^{\dagger i}
.
We obtain

W_{i}^{\beta}(P_{i})\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=W_{i}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=Y\cap\beta^{\dagger i}=P_{i}\cap\beta^{\dagger i}

by (
63
).

Let
i=0
. We have
\beta^{\dagger 0}\leq\alpha^{-0}
.
First let
Y\cap\alpha^{\dagger 1}<\beta
.
Then the assumption (
60
) with
\alpha^{-1}\leq\beta

yields

W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y
.
We obtain

W_{0}^{\beta}(P)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}=W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}=P\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}

by (
62
).
It remains to show
Y\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}\subset W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)
.
Let
\gamma\in Y\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}
.
We obtain
\gamma\in W_{0}^{\gamma}(Y)
by Lemma
7.10
.
On the other hand we have
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)

by Propositions
7.15
and
7.2
.
Moreover (
62
) with Propositions
7.15
and
7.2
yields

\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
Hence
\gamma\in W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)
.

Next let
\beta\leq Y\cap\alpha^{\dagger 1}
.
We obtain
Y\cap\beta^{\dagger 1}=\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 1}
, and

\beta^{-1}=\alpha^{-1}\leq\beta<\alpha^{\dagger 1}=\beta^{\dagger 1}
with

\beta<\beta^{\dagger 0}\leq\alpha<\beta^{\dagger 1}
.
On the other hand we have
D_{0}^{\beta}[\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\beta}(Y);Y]
by
Lemma
7.8
.
Therefore

P\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}=Y\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}=\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}=W_{0}^{\beta}(\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\beta}(Y))\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}=W_{0}^{\beta}(P)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}
by (
62
).

Thus
D_{i}^{\alpha}[P_{i};Y]
is shown.
From
\alpha\in P_{0}
we see by induction on
i<N
that

\alpha\in P_{i}\cap\alpha^{\dagger(i+1)}\subset W_{i+1}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha^{\dagger(i+1)}=P_{i+1}

for the maximal
i
-distinguished set
W_{i+1}^{\alpha}(Y)
for
\alpha
and
Y
.

\Box
of Claim
7.18
.

Claim 7.19

D_{N}[P_{N}]
.

Proof
of Claim
7.19
.
Let
\beta\leq P_{N}=W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha^{\dagger N}
.
Then
\beta<\alpha^{\dagger N}
, and
\beta^{-N}\leq\alpha^{-N}<\alpha^{\dagger N}
.
We show

W_{N}^{\beta}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}
.

Case 1
.
\alpha^{-N}\leq\beta
:
By
W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha^{-N}=Y\cap\alpha^{-N}
with
Wo[Y]
,
and
\alpha^{-N}=\beta^{-N}
we obtain

W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)=W_{N}^{\alpha}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))=W_{N}^{\beta}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))
.

Case 2
.
\beta<\alpha^{-N}
and
\beta^{-N}\leq Y
:
We obtain
\beta^{\dagger N}\leq\alpha^{-N}
.
Hence

W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=Y\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}

by
D_{N}[Y]
.
Therefore

W_{N}^{\beta}(Y)=W_{N}^{\beta}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))
.
We obtain

W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\beta}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))\cap\beta^{\dagger N}
.

Case 3
.
\beta<\alpha^{-N}
and
Y<\beta^{-N}
:
Then
\beta^{\dagger N}\leq\alpha^{-N}
.
(
61
) yields

Y\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}
.
On the other hand we have

Y\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}
and

W_{N}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\beta}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))\cap\beta^{\dagger N}
.
Therefore

W_{N}^{\beta}(W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y))\cap\beta^{\dagger N}=W_{N}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger N}
.

\Box
of Claim
7.19
.

This completes a proof of Lemma
7.16
.

Lemma 7.20

Assume
D_{N}[Y]
,
\mathbb{I}_{N}>\mathbb{S}\in Y\cap(St_{k}\cup\{0\})
and

\{0,\Omega\}\subset Y
for
0<k\leq N
.
Then
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
.

Proof
. Let us verify the condition (
60
) in Lemma
7.16

for
\alpha=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}
.
Let
\alpha^{-1}\leq\beta
.
We have
\alpha=\alpha^{-1}\leq\beta
. Hence
\alpha^{\dagger 0}\leq\beta^{\dagger 0}
,
and (
60
) is vacuously fulfilled.

Thus
it suffices to show that
\alpha=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}\in\mathcal{G}^{Y}
.

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)
follows from

\mathbb{S}\in Y\cap\alpha
, cf. Definition
7.1
.
6
.
We show

\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha\Rightarrow\gamma\in Y
by induction on

\ell\gamma
.
By Proposition
7.12
and the assumption
\{0,\Omega\}\subset Y
,
we can assume
\mathbb{S}\neq\gamma=\psi_{\sigma}^{f}(a)<\alpha=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}<\sigma
,
cf. Definition
7.1
.
6
.
Suppose
\mathbb{S}<\gamma
. Then
\mathbb{S}\in C_{a}(\gamma)
, and

\alpha=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}\in C_{a}(\gamma)\cap\sigma\subset\gamma
.
We obtain
\gamma<\mathbb{S}
.
Lemma
7.10
with
\mathbb{S}\in Y
and
D_{N}[Y]
yields

\mathbb{S}\in W_{0}^{\mathbb{S}}(Y)\cap\mathbb{S}^{\dagger 0}=Y\cap\mathbb{S}^{\dagger 0}

for
W_{0}^{\mathbb{S}}(Y)=W(\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{S}}(Y))
, where

\forall\delta[\delta\in Y\Rightarrow\delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y)]
.
We obtain
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{S}}(Y)
by
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)
,

\mathbb{S}<\alpha
and Proposition
7.2
.
1

Hence
\gamma\in W_{0}^{\mathbb{S}}(Y)\cap\mathbb{S}^{\dagger 0}\subset Y
follows.
Therefore
\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{Y}
.

Proposition 7.21

\{0,\Omega\}\subset\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
.

Proof
. For each
\alpha\in\{0,\Omega\}
and any set
Y\subset OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
we have

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)
.
First let
\alpha=0
.
We obtain
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\emptyset)\cap\alpha\subset\emptyset
, and

0\in\mathcal{G}^{\emptyset}
.
Moreover
D_{N}[\emptyset]
, and
there is no
\beta
such that

\beta^{\dagger 0}<\alpha^{\dagger 0}

since
\alpha^{\dagger 0}=\Omega
is the least in
SSt_{0}
.
Hence the condition (
61
)
is fulfilled, and we obtain

0\in X=W_{N}^{0}(\emptyset)\cap 0^{\dagger N}
with
D_{N}[X]
by
Lemma
7.16
.

Next let
\alpha=\Omega
.
Let
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\cap\alpha
.
We show that
\gamma\in X
by induction on
\ell\gamma
as follows.
We see that
each strongly critical number
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\cap\alpha
is in
X

from Definition
7.1
.
Otherwise
\gamma\in X
is seen from IH using Proposition
7.12
and

0\in X
.
Therefore we obtain

\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{X}
.

Let
\beta^{\dagger 0}<\alpha^{\dagger 0}
. Then
\beta^{\dagger 0}=\Omega
and
\beta<\Omega
.
Let
\gamma\in W_{0}^{\beta}(X)\cap\Omega
.
We show
\gamma\in X
.
We obtain
D_{0}^{0}[X;X]
by Lemma
7.10
, and

\gamma\in W_{0}^{\beta}(X)\cap\Omega=W_{0}^{0}(X)\cap\Omega=X\cap\Omega
.
Hence the condition (
60
) is fulfilled, and we obtain

\Omega\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
by
Lemma
7.16
.

Lemma 7.22

(\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC}})

If

\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{W}_{N+1}}
,

N
-distinguished
set

Z
such that

\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{W}_{N+1}}
.

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})
.

N
-distinguished set
X_{0}
such that
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X_{0})

\{0,\Omega\}\subset X_{0}

.

.
We obtain

\displaystyle\forall n\forall X\exists Y\{D_{N}[X]\to D_{N}[Y]

\displaystyle\land

\displaystyle\forall\beta\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\left(\ell(\beta)\leq n\land\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(X)\cap\alpha\to\beta\in Y\right)

\displaystyle\land

\displaystyle\forall k\forall\mathbb{S}\in(St_{k}\cup\{\Omega\})\left(\ell(\mathbb{S})\leq n\land\mathbb{S}\in X\to\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}\in Y\right)\}

Since
D_{N}[X]
is
\Delta^{1}_{N+2}
,

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{-DC}
yields a set
Z
such that

and

\displaystyle\forall n\{D_{N}[Z_{n}]\to D_{N}[Z_{n+1}]

\displaystyle\land

\displaystyle\forall\beta\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\left(\ell(\beta)\leq n\land\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Z_{n})\cap\alpha\to\beta\in Z_{n+1}\right)

\displaystyle\land

\displaystyle\forall k\forall\mathbb{S}\in(St_{k}\cup\{\Omega\})\left(\ell(\mathbb{S})\leq n\land\mathbb{S}\in Z_{n}\to\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}\in Z_{n+1}\right)\}

Let
Z=\bigcup_{n}Z_{n}
.
We see by induction on

that
D_{N}[Z_{n}]
for every
n
.
Lemma
7.9
.
3
yields
D_{N}[Z]
.
Let
\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Z)\cap\alpha
.
Pick an
n
such that
\beta\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Z_{n})
and
\ell\beta\leq n
.
We obtain
\beta\in Z_{n+1}\subset Z
.
Therefore
\alpha\in\mathcal{G}^{Z}
.
Furthermore let
\mathbb{S}\in Z\cap(St_{k}\cup\{\Omega\})
.
Pick an
n
such that
\mathbb{S}\in Z_{n}
and
\ell(\mathbb{S})\leq n
.
We obtain
\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}\in Z_{n+1}\subset Z
.

Proposition 7.23

Let
D_{N}[Y]
and

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
Assume
Y\cap\beta<\delta
.
Then
F_{\delta}(\alpha)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\alpha
.
Let
\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}\not\ni\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
We have
\mathcal{E}(\alpha)\leq\alpha

First consider the case
\alpha\not\in\mathcal{E}(\alpha)
.
If
\alpha\in Y\cap\beta\subset\mathcal{G}^{Y}
by Proposition
7.15
, then

\mathcal{E}(\alpha)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha\subset Y\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
by Proposition
7.5
.
Otherwise we have
\alpha\not\in\mathcal{E}(\alpha)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
In each case IH yields
F_{\delta}(\alpha)=F_{\delta}(\mathcal{E}(\alpha))\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.

Let
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
for some
\pi,f,a
.
If
\alpha<\delta
, then
F_{\delta}(\alpha)=\{\alpha\}
, and there is nothing to prove.
Let
\alpha\geq\delta
. Then
F_{\delta}(\alpha)=F_{\delta}(\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))
.
On the other side we see
\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
from
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)

and the assumption.
IH yields
F_{\delta}(\alpha)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.

Finally let
\alpha\in N(\rho)
.
Then
F_{\delta}(\alpha)=F_{\delta}(\rho)
.
If
\rho\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
, then IH yields
F_{\delta}(\rho)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
Otherwise we have
\alpha\in Y
, and
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)
.
Hence
\rho\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(Y)\cap\alpha\subset Y\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.

Proposition 7.24

Let
\gamma<\beta
.
Assume
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)
and

G_{\beta}(\alpha)<\gamma
.
Moreover assume

\forall\delta[\ell\delta\leq\ell\alpha\,\&\,\delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma\Rightarrow\delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)]
.
Then
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.

Proof
. By induction on
\ell\alpha
.
If
\alpha<\gamma
, then
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma
.
The third assumption yields
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
Assume
\alpha\geq\gamma
.
Consider the case
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
for some

\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)
and
\pi>\gamma
.
If
\pi\leq\beta
, then
\{\alpha\}=G_{\beta}(\alpha)<\gamma
by the second assumption. Hence this is not the case, and we obtain

\pi>\beta
.
Then
G_{\beta}(\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))=G_{\beta}(\alpha)<\gamma
.
IH yields
\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
. We conclude
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
from
\pi>\beta
.

Next let
\gamma\leq\alpha\in N(\rho)

with
\rho\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)
.
If
\alpha<\beta
, then
\{\alpha\}=G_{\beta}(\alpha)<\gamma
, and this is not the case.
Let
\alpha\geq\beta
. Then
G_{\beta}(\alpha)=G_{\beta}(\rho)
.
IH yields
\rho\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
, and

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
by
\alpha\geq\beta
.

\Box

The following Lemma
7.25
is a key result on distinguished classes.

Lemma 7.25

Suppose
D_{N}[Y]

with
\{0,\Omega\}\subset Y
, and for
\eta\in\Psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\cup\bigcup_{\mathbb{S}\in SSt}L(\mathbb{S})
, cf. Definition
6.12
,

\eta\in\mathcal{G}^{Y}

(64)

R(\eta)\cap\{\gamma\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\mathbb{I}_{N}:Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}<\gamma\}\cap\mathcal{G}^{Y}\subset Y

(65)

and

\eta\in L(\mathbb{S})\,\&\,\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger k}\Rightarrow\forall i<k(\mathbb{T}^{\dagger i}\in Y)

(66)

Then

\eta\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
.
Moreover if there exists a set
Z
and an ordinal
\gamma
such that

Y=W_{N}^{\gamma}(Z)
and
\eta^{-N}=\gamma^{-N}
,
then
\eta\in Y
holds.

Proof
. By Lemma
7.16

and the hypothesis (
64
) it suffices to show
(
60
)

\forall\beta\geq\eta^{-1}\left(Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}<\beta\,\&\,\beta^{\dagger 0}<\eta^{\dagger 0}\to W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y\right).

Assume
Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}<\beta
and
\beta^{\dagger 0}<\eta^{\dagger 0}
.
We have to show

W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y
.
We prove this by induction on
\gamma\in W_{0}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}
.
Suppose
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}
and

\mbox{{\rm MIH : }}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\gamma\subset Y.

We show
\gamma\in Y
.
We can assume that

Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}<\gamma

(67)

since if
\gamma\leq\delta
for some
\delta\in Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}
, then by

Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}<\beta
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)

we obtain
\delta<\beta
,

\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y)
and
\delta\in W(\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y))\cap\delta^{\dagger 0}=Y\cap\delta^{\dagger 0}
by Lemma
7.10
.
Hence
\gamma\in W(\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y))\cap\delta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y
.

Moreover we can assume
\gamma\not\in(Reg_{0}\setminus\{\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\})\cap\beta
.
For otherwise
\gamma\in Y

by Definition
7.1
.
6
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta
.

We show first

\gamma\in\mathcal{G}^{Y}

(68)

First
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)
by

\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\beta^{\dagger 0}
and Proposition
7.2
.
Second we show the following claim by induction on
\ell\alpha
:

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma\Rightarrow\alpha\in Y

(69)

Proof
of (
69
).
Assume
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma
.
We can assume
\gamma^{\dagger 0}\leq\beta
for otherwise we have

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma=\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\gamma\subset Y
by MIH.

By induction hypothesis on lengths,
Proposition
7.12
, and
\{0,\Omega\}\subset Y
,
we can assume that

\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)
for some
\pi>\gamma
such that

\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)
.

Case 1
.
\beta<\pi
:
Then
G_{\beta}(\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f))=G_{\beta}(\alpha)<\alpha<\gamma
by Proposition
6.19
.
1
.
Proposition
7.24
with induction hypothesis on lengths yields

\{\pi,a\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
.
Hence
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\gamma
by
\pi>\beta
.
MIH yields
\alpha\in Y
.

Case 2
.
\beta\geq\pi
:
We have
\alpha<\gamma<\pi\leq\beta
.
It suffices to show that
\alpha\leq Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}
.
Then by (
67
) we have
\alpha\leq\delta\in Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}
for some
\delta<\gamma
.

\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y)\ni\alpha\leq\delta\in Y\cap\delta^{\dagger 0}=W(\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y))\cap\delta^{\dagger 0}

yields
\alpha\in W(\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(Y))\cap\delta^{\dagger 0}\subset Y
.

Consider first the case
\gamma\not\in\mathcal{E}(\gamma)
.
By
\alpha=\psi_{\pi}^{f}(a)<\gamma<\pi
,
we can assume that

\gamma\not\in\{0,\Omega,\mathbb{I}_{N}\}
.
Then let
\delta=\max S(\gamma)
denote the largest immediate subterm of
\gamma
.
Then

\delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(Y)\cap\gamma
, and
by (
67
),

Y\cap\eta^{\dagger 1}<\gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)
we have

\delta\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(Y)\cap\gamma

Corollary 7.38

For
each

\zeta\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})
,

\mbox{{\rm MIH}}(\zeta;\mathcal{W}_{N+1})
holds.

Proof
. For each

, we have

.

.

7.36

7.34

7.36
,
\mbox{SSIH}(\zeta,a,f)

6.3
yields

o_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(g)<o_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(f)\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})
for

\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\Lambda
,
where
\Lambda=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(b)
and

b=\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a))\geq\mathtt{p}_{0}(\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a))
.
Hence

o_{\Lambda}(g)<o_{\Lambda}(f)\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
by
\Lambda\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
.

\Box

Lemma 7.39

For
each

n<\omega
, the following holds:

If one of the followings holds, then

\alpha\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}
for
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
.

1.

\alpha=\mathbb{S}^{\dagger k}
with
\mathbb{S}\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap(St_{k}\cup\{\Omega\})
.

2.

\alpha=\psi_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(a)
with

a\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)
.

3.

\alpha=\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)\in L(\mathbb{S})

for
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger k}
with
\mathbb{T}\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}

and

k(\alpha)\cup\mathtt{h}(\alpha)=\{\kappa,a,\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\alpha)\}\cup\mathrm{fld}(f)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)
.

4.

\alpha\in N(\rho)

for
\rho\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap L(\mathbb{S})
with

\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger k}
such that
\mathbb{T}\in\mathcal{W}_{N+1}

and
\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\rho)<\omega_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{N}+1)
.

Proof
.
7.39
.
1
is seen from Lemma
7.20
.

7.39
.
2
follows from Lemma
7.28
.

7.39
.
3
follows from Corollary
7.38
.

7.39
.
4
follows from Corollaries

7.34
and
7.38
.

\Box

Let us conclude Theorem
1.2
.
For each
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
,

\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})

is seen
by metainduction on the lengths
\ell\alpha
using
Propositions
7.12
,

7.21

and Lemma
7.39
.
Note that
\ell(\mathtt{g}^{*}_{0}(\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a)))<\ell(\psi_{\kappa}^{f}(a))

and
\ell(\mathbb{T})<\ell(\rho)
for
\rho\in L(\mathbb{S})
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger k}
.
Therefore we obtain

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC+BI}}\vdash\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{I}_{N}}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1})\cap\Omega=\mathcal{W}_{N+1}\cap\Omega=W(\mathcal{C}^{0}(\mathcal{W}_{N+1}))\cap\Omega=W(OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}))\cap\Omega
, and

\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC+BI}}\vdash Wo[\alpha]

for each
\alpha<\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
.

8
Outcomes on
\mathbf{Z}_{2}

In this final section let us conclude some standard outcomes of
an ordinal analysis of the theory
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
.

Let
\mathrm{TI}[\Pi^{1-}_{0},\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})]
denote a schema of transfinite induction

\forall\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega\,(\mathrm{Prg}[OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}),A]\to OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\alpha\subset A)
up to
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
in
OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})

applied to arithmetic formulas
A\in\Pi^{1-}_{0}
in the language of the first-order arithmetic
{\sf PA}
.
Let
T_{0}={\sf PA}+\bigcup\{\mathrm{TI}[\Pi^{1-}_{0},\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})]:N<\omega\}
, and

T_{1}=\mathrm{FiX}^{i}(T_{0})
denote the intuitionistic fixed point theory over
T_{0}
.
The language of the theory
T_{1}
is expanded by unary predicate symbols
I
for each operator
\Phi(X,x)
, in which
every occurrence of a unary predicate symbol
X
is strictly positive.
The axioms in
T_{1}
are obtained from
T_{0}
by adding the axioms
\forall x[I(x)\leftrightarrow\Phi(I,x)]
for a fixed point
I
.
The axiom schema
\mathrm{TI}[\Pi^{1-}_{0},\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})]
of transfinite induction as well as
schema of complete induction may be applied to arbitrary first-order formulas in the expanded language with the predicates
I
.
The underlying logic in
T_{1}
is the intuitionistic first-order logic with the axiom
\forall x,y(x=y\to I(x)\to I(y))
.
The excluded middle
\forall x(\lnot I(x)\lor I(x))
for the predicate
I
is not available in
T_{1}
.

Lemma 8.1

\mathrm{FiX}^{i}(T_{0})
is a conservative extension of
T_{0}
.
Moreover the fact is provable in the fragment
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}
of the first-order arithmetic:

I\Sigma^{0}_{1}\vdash\mathrm{Pr}_{T_{1}}(\lceil\varphi\rceil)\to\mathrm{Pr}_{T_{0}}(\lceil\varphi\rceil)
,
where
\mathrm{Pr}_{T}(x)
is a standard provability predicate for a theory
T
.

Proof
. The fact is seen as in
[
1
,
3
]
.
To formalize a proof of the fact in
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}
, follow a finitary analysis in section 4.4 of
[
3
]
.

\Box

Theorem 8.2

\mathbf{Z}_{2}
is a conservative extension of
{\sf PA}+\bigcup\{{\rm TI}[\Pi^{1-}_{0},\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})]:N<\omega\}
.
Moreover the fact is provable in the fragment
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}
.

Proof
. Assume that
\mathbf{Z}_{2}\vdash A
for an arithmetic sentence
A\in\Pi^{1-}_{0}
.
Pick an
N<\omega
such that
\Sigma^{1}_{N+2}\mbox{{\rm-DC+BI}}\vdash A
.
By Lemma
2.3
we obtain
{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}+(V=L)\vdash A^{set}
, and hence

{\sf KP}\omega+\Pi_{N}\mbox{{\rm-Collection}}\vdash A^{set}
.
Then by Lemma
2.5
we obtain
S_{\mathbb{I}_{N}}\vdash A^{set}
.

Now we see that the proof of Theorem
1.1
in sections
4
and
5
is formalizable in
the intuitionistic fixed point theory
T_{1}=\mathrm{FiX}^{i}(T_{0})
over
T_{0}
.
Let us regard each of the relations

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta;\mathtt{D})\vdash^{*a}_{c,\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
and

(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma},\Theta,\mathtt{Q})\vdash^{a}_{c,\xi,\Lambda_{0},\gamma_{0}}\Gamma
as a fixed point of a strictly positive operator.
Then by applying transfinite induction to first-order formulas with the fixed point predicates, Theorem
1.1
is proved.
Therefore we obtain
\mathrm{FiX}^{i}(T_{0})\vdash A
, and
T_{0}\vdash A
by Lemma
8.1
.

\Box

We see readily that
the transfinite induction
{\rm TI}(\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega}))
up to
\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega})

is equivalent to the
\Pi^{1}_{1}
-soundness
{\rm RFN}_{\Pi^{1}_{1}}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})
of
\mathbf{Z}_{2}

over
\mathrm{RCA}_{0}
, where

\mathrm{TI}(\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega}))
denotes a
\Pi^{1}_{1}
-sentence

\forall N\forall\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega\forall Y\,(\mathrm{Prg}[OT(\mathbb{I}_{N}),Y]\to OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\alpha\subset Y)
.

Definition 8.3

Let
\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})
be an ordinal term.

1.

DS_{\alpha}
denotes a
\Pi^{0}_{2}
-sentence saying that ‘there is no primitive recursive and descending sequence
\{f(n)\}_{n}
of ordinals
with
f(0)<\alpha
’.
This means that
f(0)<\alpha\Rightarrow\exists n(f(n+1)\not<f(n))
.

2.

WDS_{\alpha}
denotes a
\Pi^{0}_{3}
-sentence saying that ‘for every primitive recursive and weakly descending sequence
\{f(n)\}_{n}
of ordinals
with
f(0)<\alpha
, there exists an
n
such that
\forall m\geq n(f(m)=f(n))
’.
This is equivalent to the principle that ‘for every primitive recursive sequence
\{f(n)\}_{n}
of ordinals, there exists
an
n
such that
\forall m(f(n)\leq f(m))
.

3.

DS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}:\Leftrightarrow\forall\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega\,DS_{\alpha}
and

DS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega})}:\Leftrightarrow\forall N>0\,DS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}
.

Also

WDS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}:\Leftrightarrow\forall\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega\,WDS_{\alpha}
and

WDS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega})}:\Leftrightarrow\forall N>0\,WDS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}
.

4.

A computable (total) function
f
on integers is said to be
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
-recursive

if
f
is defined from
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
-recursive functions
g,r,h
by
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
-recursion:

f(y,x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g(y,x,f(y,r(y,x)))&\mbox{{\rm if } }r(y,x)<x<\Omega\mbox{ {\rm in }}OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\\
h(y,x)&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

5.

\mathrm{RFN}_{\Sigma^{0}_{n}}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})
denotes the uniform reflection principle of
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
for
\Sigma^{0}_{n}
-formulas.

Corollary 8.4

1.

The 2-consistency
\mathrm{RFN}_{\Sigma^{0}_{2}}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})
of
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
is equivalent to

WDS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega})}
over
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}
.

2.

\mathbf{Z}_{2}
is
\Pi^{0}_{3}
-conservative over
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}+\{WDS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}:0<N<\omega\}
.

3.

The 1-consistency
\mathrm{RFN}_{\Sigma^{0}_{1}}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})
of
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
is equivalent to

DS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\mathbb{I}_{\omega})}
over
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}
.

4.

\mathbf{Z}_{2}
is
\Pi^{0}_{2}
-conservative over
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}+\{DS_{\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})}:0<N<\omega\}
.

5.

For computable total function
f
on
\mathbb{N}
,

f
is provably computable in
\mathbf{Z}_{2}

iff
f
is
\psi_{\Omega}(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{I}_{N}+1})
-recursive for an
N<\omega
.

Proof
. Each follows from Theorem
8.2
as in chapter 4 of
[
3
]
.

\Box

For the consistency
\mathrm{Con}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})
of
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
we obtain the following.

Corollary 8.5

There are primitive recursive predicate
B
and primitive recursive function
f
such that
both of
\forall N>0\forall\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega(f(N,\alpha)<\alpha\to B(N,f(N,\alpha))\to B(N,\alpha))

and
\forall N>0\forall\alpha\in OT(\mathbb{I}_{N})\cap\Omega\,B(N,\alpha)\to\mathrm{Con}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})

is provable in
I\Sigma^{0}_{1}
.

Proof
. This is seen from Theorem
8.2
as in section 4.3 of
[
3
]
.

\Box
